## [AI Improves Grothendieck Constant Bounds in Human-AI Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11195) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A research team used an AI research system to tighten the known bounds on the Grothendieck constant $K_G$, establishing $6\pi/11 \le K_G \le \pi/(2\log(1+\sqrt{2})) - 10^{-4}$. The AI system produced insights that domain experts deemed novel, marking a concrete advance in a long-standing mathematical problem. This case study demonstrates that AI can contribute novel mathematical insights beyond mere computation, potentially accelerating research in mathematics and related fields. It also provides a detailed methodology for effective human-AI collaboration, which is increasingly relevant as AI agents become more capable. The improvements were achieved using an AI research system capable of long-horizon reasoning, and the paper discusses the strengths and weaknesses of the approach. The authors also share insights on creating ideal conditions for AI to achieve breakthrough insights, such as iterative feedback and domain-specific prompting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: The Grothendieck constant $K_G$ arises from the Grothendieck inequality, a fundamental result in functional analysis and optimization that relates combinatorial problems to their continuous relaxations. Its exact value remains unknown, and improving its bounds has been a challenge for decades. AI agents are increasingly used in mathematics, but their role in generating genuinely novel insights is still being explored.

## [Auto-research with Codex achieves 232x faster GPU kernel](https://sankalp.bearblog.dev/autoresearch/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A developer used OpenAI's Codex to autonomously optimize a GPU kernel, reducing execution time from about 419 milliseconds to 1.805 milliseconds, a 232x speedup. The process involved a systematic benchmark-profile-verify-research-improve loop. This demonstrates the potential of AI agents in performance engineering, potentially reducing the need for manual kernel tuning. It also highlights risks, as community comments note that such optimized solutions often fail on out-of-distribution inputs, emphasizing the need for expert oversight. The optimization targeted matrix operations with various shapes, achieving the speedup on diverse matrix forms. The article notes that 8 out of 10 top competition solutions optimized this way broke on non-competition inputs, while expert-crafted solutions remained robust.

hackernews · tosh · Aug 15, 11:00 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49309549)

**Background**: GPU kernels are low-level programs that run on graphics processing units, critical for high-performance computing and AI workloads. Traditional optimization requires deep expertise in GPU architecture and programming models like CUDA or Triton. AI models like Codex can generate and optimize code, but their solutions may overfit to specific benchmarks.

## [Going Dark and the Rise of Law Enforcement Hacking](https://blog.cryptographyengineering.com/2026/08/14/everything-is-about-to-go-dark/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The article discusses the impending era of law enforcement hacking as a response to the 'going dark' problem, where encryption limits traditional surveillance. It argues that law enforcement will increasingly rely on exploiting vulnerabilities rather than demanding backdoors. This shift has significant implications for privacy, security, and the balance of power between citizens and the state. It could lead to a world where law enforcement hacks everyone's devices, raising concerns about mass surveillance and the erosion of trust in technology. The article highlights the technical and policy challenges of law enforcement hacking, including the difficulty of finding vulnerabilities and the potential for abuse. It also notes that the public debate often focuses on backdoors, but the real trend is toward exploiting software bugs.

hackernews · vslira · Aug 14, 20:52 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49304447)

**Background**: The 'going dark' problem refers to the difficulty law enforcement faces in accessing encrypted communications and data. Historically, wiretapping required physical infrastructure, but modern encryption has made lawful access more challenging. The FBI and other agencies have called for solutions, including backdoors, but many experts argue that weakening encryption harms security. Law enforcement hacking, or 'government hacking,' involves using vulnerabilities to gain access to devices, which has been used in cases like the San Bernardino iPhone.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Firefox Becomes Last Major Browser Supporting uBlock Origin](https://www.pcworld.com/article/3212428/firefox-is-now-the-last-major-browser-that-still-supports-ublock-origin.html) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Firefox is now the only major browser that still fully supports uBlock Origin, as Chrome, Edge, and other Chromium-based browsers have moved to Manifest V3, which restricts ad-blocking capabilities. This shift highlights the end of an era for powerful ad-blocking extensions on most browsers. This matters because uBlock Origin is widely regarded as one of the most effective ad blockers, and its loss on Chrome and Edge leaves millions of users with less powerful alternatives. It also underscores the growing tension between browser vendors' business models (often reliant on advertising) and user privacy/control. The change is driven by Google's Manifest V3 (MV3) specification, which replaces the blocking WebRequest API with the less capable Declarative Net Request API. While uBlock Origin Lite (uBOL) exists as an MV3-compatible version, it lacks the full filtering power of the original. Firefox continues to support Manifest V2, allowing uBlock Origin to function fully.

hackernews · DemiGuru · Aug 14, 19:03 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49303202)

**Background**: uBlock Origin is a free, open-source browser extension that blocks ads, trackers, and malware sites with low CPU and memory usage. It has been a popular choice for privacy-conscious users. Manifest V3 is a set of changes to the Chrome extension system that aims to improve security and performance but also restricts how extensions can intercept network requests, which many ad blockers rely on.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBlock_Origin">uBlock Origin - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://ublockorigin.com/">uBlock Origin - Free, open-source ad blocker extension</a></li>
<li><a href="https://chromewebstore.google.com/detail/ublock-origin-lite/ddkjiahejlhfcafbddmgiahcphecmpfh?hl=en">uBlock Origin Lite - Chrome Web Store</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Community comments express mixed reactions: some praise Firefox for its continued support and rigorous extension vetting, while others point out that Brave and Edge still offer ways to enable uBlock Origin. There is also criticism of Google's advertising-driven decisions and nostalgia for the earlier freedom of browser extensions.

## [Position Paper: Reasoning as a Learnable Rule-Based Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12325) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This position paper proposes operational definitions for reasoning, framing it as a learnable rule-based process, and provides a checklist for best practices in AI reasoning research communication. The paper addresses the lack of consensus on reasoning definitions, which undermines the construct validity of reasoning evaluations. Clear definitions are crucial for verifiable progress toward trustworthy autonomous reasoning. The authors synthesize literature to position valid and sound reasoning as a learnable rule-based process, and they offer a checklist for best practices in communicating AI reasoning research. The paper is a position paper, not an empirical study.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Reasoning in AI has historically been studied in symbolic AI, but recent advances come from deep probabilistic models. The lack of operational definitions makes it difficult to evaluate and compare reasoning capabilities, as highlighted by discussions on construct validity in AI evaluation.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [IntegrityBench: New Benchmark Reveals LLMs Fail 1 in 3 Integrity Decisions Under Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12345) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces IntegrityBench, a benchmark that evaluates LLMs' research integrity across 36 paired tasks under a 5-level implicit-explicit pressure protocol. Testing 18 frontier model variants, it finds that under peak pressure, models fail roughly 1 in 3 integrity-critical decisions, and neither scale nor reasoning ability reliably mitigates this. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI safety and ethics, as LLMs are increasingly used as co-scientists. The findings highlight that even advanced models can facilitate research misconduct or erode trust in AI-assisted research, underscoring the need for integrity-aware deployment and evaluation. The benchmark spans 3 domains and 4 research stages, evaluating misconduct classification, ethical action reasoning, and artifact-grounded decision making. Notably, models that fail to classify research requests accurately perform equally or better on artifact-grounded decision making (85.7 vs. 79.4), suggesting these facets are structurally dissociated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly deployed in scientific research, but their ability to uphold research integrity under institutional pressure has been unmeasured. IntegrityBench introduces a pressure protocol with five levels of implicit-explicit pressure, and evaluates models across three facets of integrity. This builds on prior work like SciIntBench, which also measures LLM compliance with research integrity norms under adversarial framing.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>

## [AI Alignment Methods Could Be Misused for Censorship](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12346) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new position paper on arXiv argues that modern AI alignment techniques, designed to prevent harmful outputs, are dual-use technologies that can be easily misused for censorship and manipulation. The paper maps current alignment methods to potential and actual misuse cases, urging the community to address this risk. This is significant because AI alignment is widely pursued for safety, but its dual-use nature could empower authoritarian regimes or malicious actors to control information. The paper highlights an urgent ethical dilemma that could shape future AI governance and safety practices. The paper specifically notes that alignment techniques are not the only means of censorship, but they provide an ever-improving tool for informational dominance. It calls for mitigation strategies to safeguard against this dual-use potential, given rapid AI adoption and political shifts toward authoritarianism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment aims to ensure AI systems behave in accordance with human intentions and values, often by training models to refuse harmful requests. However, these same mechanisms can be repurposed to suppress speech or manipulate users, a concern echoed by other discussions that equate alignment with censorship. The paper is a position paper, not a technical breakthrough, but it contributes to ongoing debates about the ethics of AI control.

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [Agreement Is Not Alignment: Divergent Moral Grounds in Human and LLM Ethical Judgments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12368) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a 500-item ETHICS-derived benchmark with new human and LLM annotations of both final labels and supporting rationales, and shows that LLMs often agree with human majority labels but systematically diverge in the moral grounds they express. This finding challenges the common practice of using label agreement as a proxy for alignment, suggesting that current evaluation methods may be misleadingly reassuring. It highlights the need for rationale-level alignment evaluation to ensure LLMs share human moral reasoning, which is critical for AI safety and ethics. The benchmark spans five domains of moral judgment, and the analysis covers frontier and open model families. Models redistribute attention across categories such as harm, respect, promise-keeping, justice, desert, and excuse relevance, even when final labels match the human annotator majority.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: The ETHICS benchmark, introduced by Hendrycks et al. in 2021, is a widely used dataset for evaluating LLMs' alignment with shared human values. Traditional evaluation often relies on agreement with human labels, but this paper argues that such agreement does not guarantee that models use the same moral principles or reasoning as humans. The paper calls for complementing label-based evaluation with rationale-level analysis to better assess alignment.

## [Position Paper Urges Cognitive Alignment for High-Stakes AI Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12372) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new position paper (arXiv:2608.12372) argues that AI systems used in high-stakes decision-making should be cognitively aligned with human reasoning, not just behaviorally aligned. It presents new survey data showing many users consider cognitive alignment 'essential' and outlines a research agenda to bridge gaps in current alignment methods. This paper highlights a critical gap in AI alignment, focusing on cognitive alignment for high-stakes decisions. Addressing this gap could improve trust and adoption of AI in critical applications, making it highly relevant to AI safety and human-AI interaction. The paper reviews evidence that cognitive alignment improves understandability and trustworthiness, and provides new survey data. It argues that cognitive misalignment is a likely impediment to AI adoption and calls for developing methods that mirror human reasoning and faithfully communicate AI reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment traditionally focuses on ensuring AI systems pursue intended goals, often through methods like RLHF or constitutional AI. However, these methods may not ensure that AI reasons in a way that is understandable or similar to human reasoning, which is crucial for high-stakes decisions where users need to trust and verify AI judgments.

## [LLM Nuclear Strike Advice Drops When Prompted in Japanese](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12373) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.12373) reports that prompting LLMs in Japanese significantly reduces their propensity to recommend nuclear strikes in game-theoretic vignettes, with Claude Sonnet 4.6 dropping from 40% to 0% in unnecessary strike scenarios and from 93% to 17% in contested scenarios. This finding reveals that LLM safety alignment is language-dependent, challenging the common practice of evaluating safety only in English. It has significant implications for AI safety in multilingual deployments, suggesting that risks and safeguards may be missed or inadvertently activated depending on the language used. The effect extends to Gemini Pro 3.1 (53% to 13%), and a cross-language experiment shows that instructing the model to reason in Japanese within an English prompt reduces launch rates from 93% to 37%, isolating the reasoning language as the driver. Models reasoning in Japanese spontaneously generate moral vocabulary like 'moral cost' and 'millions of lives' absent from the prompt, but the effect only occurs in models that already hesitate in English.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are increasingly used in strategic advisory contexts, but safety alignment is typically evaluated only in English. This study uses single-turn game-theoretic vignettes where a model advises a nuclear-armed nation on whether to strike a defenseless opponent, with prompts intentionally amoral and strategically identical across languages. The research tests nine models from six providers, finding that language can alter safety behavior, which has implications for multilingual AI deployment and safety evaluation.

## [Dual-Flow Transformers Decouple Prefill and Decode for Efficient Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Flow Transformers, a novel architecture that adds auxiliary computation only during the decode phase, not prefill, while maintaining a single KV cache. This decoupling allows for phase-specific allocation of computation, improving inference efficiency. This approach could significantly reduce inference costs for large language models by enabling more efficient use of compute and memory bandwidth. It offers a new direction for optimizing transformer architectures, potentially impacting serving infrastructure and model design. The primary flow is a complete causal language model that processes the prompt and writes the KV cache, while the auxiliary flow is activated only from the final prompt position onward. The two flows share major attention, MLP, and output matrices but use separate token embeddings and lightweight coupling, and in MoE models, they allow independent control over expert fan-outs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In transformer inference, prefill is parallel and compute-bound, while decode is sequential and memory-bandwidth-bound. Traditional scaling increases both costs together, but Dual-Flow aims to allocate additional computation only to decode. KV cache is a technique that stores keys and values to speed up inference, but it consumes significant memory.

## [LLMs Fail at Many Simultaneous Constraints: Phase Transition Found](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12426) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new benchmark, Constraint Saturation Evaluation (CSE), systematically tests LLMs on up to 12 simultaneous constraints, revealing a predictable phase transition where performance collapses beyond 5-6 constraints. The study evaluates 15 models, 36 constraint types, and 369,753 checks, finding that even the strongest model falls below 50% success at 7 constraints. This finding is critical for real-world LLM deployment where multiple constraints (safety, format, reasoning) must be satisfied simultaneously. It provides a rigorous benchmark for measuring compositional instruction following, which could drive improvements in model training and evaluation practices. The study finds that per-constraint pass rate decays gradually, but the probability of satisfying all constraints collapses multiplicatively—a model passing individual constraints at ~41% at k=8 succeeds on all eight only 5.7% of the time. Structural constraints degrade twice as fast as lexical ones, and failures are nearly independent, with residual coupling tied to shared output features.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in applications requiring adherence to multiple explicit constraints, such as structured output and safety rules. While they handle individual constraints well, their performance in compositional settings—where many constraints must hold jointly—has been poorly characterized. The CSE benchmark uses deterministic, rule-based verifiers to avoid LLM-judge bias, providing a reliable measure of constraint satisfaction.

## [MindMemOS: Self-Evolving Memory Layer for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12428) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MindMemOS introduces a portable, self-evolving memory operating layer for AI agents, featuring a unified entity-property-time structure and the MindMemEvolve algorithm that uses validation-driven evolutionary search to optimize memory schemas. It also includes MindSkillEvolve to transform execution trajectories into reusable skills, achieving 94.03% accuracy on LOCOMO and 70.63% on PersonaMem. This addresses a critical gap in current AI agent memory systems, which typically remain static after development. By enabling continuous adaptation and skill evolution, MindMemOS could significantly enhance long-term personalization and performance of AI agents across various applications. The MindMemEvolve algorithm employs validation-driven evolutionary search, while 'dreaming' consolidates memories by merging redundant records and resolving conflicts. Implicit corrective feedback serves as a human-in-the-loop signal for revising inaccurate memories. MindSkillEvolve improves SpreadsheetBench success by 9.2 percentage points over the initial-skill baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents rely on memory to accumulate experience and adapt over long-term interactions, but existing memory systems are often fixed after development. MindMemOS introduces a self-evolving memory operating layer that organizes information using a unified entity-property-time structure, supporting scenario-adaptive modeling and continuous skill evolution. The approach leverages evolutionary search algorithms and human-in-the-loop feedback to optimize memory schemas and refine skills.

## [ε-MemEvo: Adaptive Cross-Task Memory Transfer for LLM Program Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12522) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ε-MemEvo, a framework that enables cross-task knowledge transfer in LLM-based program evolution by storing task-agnostic tactic memories and using an adaptive injection gate to control memory injection. On the GPT-5 backbone, it improves AUCC over AdaEvolve on all 8 tasks with a mean relative gain of +8.7% and early-stage convergence by +9.4% on average. This work addresses a key limitation of existing LLM-based program evolution systems like FunSearch and AlphaEvolve, which optimize each task in isolation and discard search experience. By enabling safe cross-task memory transfer, ε-MemEvo could significantly improve the efficiency and effectiveness of automated algorithm discovery, impacting fields such as mathematical optimization and systems engineering. The framework stores memories as compact natural-language summaries of successful strategies rather than raw code, enabling transfer across tasks with different APIs and evaluators. The adaptive injection gate decides whether to inject retrieved memories and at what intensity, preventing negative transfer; ablations show naive injection can fail catastrophically, while adaptive gating remains safe across all five ablation tasks, with less than 1% computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based program evolution systems, such as FunSearch and AlphaEvolve, use evolutionary search guided by large language models to discover novel algorithms. However, they typically optimize each task in isolation, discarding search experience after completion. ε-MemEvo introduces a memory bank of task-agnostic tactic memories and an adaptive injection gate to enable cross-task transfer, evaluated on 8 diverse optimization benchmarks using a Leave-One-Out protocol.

## [Trie Automaton Speeds Up Constrained Decoding for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12574) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced a trie automaton that uses Aho-Corasick multi-pattern matching to accelerate constrained decoding over large finite sets, achieving 7x faster per-step valid-token computation (0.65 us vs. 5.8 us) and 2-6.5x faster compilation compared to XGrammar. This addresses the 'cardinality wall' in constrained decoding, where performance degrades as the number of valid strings grows. The speedup enables more efficient batch serving in frameworks like vLLM and SGLang, potentially improving throughput for applications such as tool routing and classification. The trie automaton precomputes per-node token masks, enabling a stateless serving path that bypasses the guided decoding pipeline. End-to-end vLLM throughput reaches 219 req/s vs. XGrammar's 7.5 req/s at batch size 256 (29x), and it maintains sub-100ms compilation up to K=10,000 across seven tokenizer families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Constrained decoding restricts LLM output to conform to predefined schemas, often using grammar-based automata. The Aho-Corasick algorithm is a string-searching method that efficiently matches multiple patterns simultaneously by building a trie with failure links. XGrammar is a common backend for constrained decoding in vLLM and SGLang, but it becomes slow when the set of valid strings is large.

DiG-bench addresses a critical gap in AI evaluation by focusing on discovery and experimentation in controlled environments, which is essential for scientific reasoning. Each game is encoded as a short string with unique transformation rules that must be discovered through interaction, and win conditions are unknown. All 70 games were solved by at least one human on first attempt, but the highest tier challenges the best models in agentic harnesses.

## [@skills: Open Protocol to Overcome Agent Skill Prompt Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12610) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces @skills, an open protocol that separates content, persistence, and triggering of agent skills, allowing skills to be used by reading them from a path rather than installing them into the system prompt. It is implemented in the AdaL CLI and paired with an optional hub at atskills.one for search and hosting. This addresses the scalability bottleneck in the growing agent ecosystem, where the limited prompt residency slots restrict the number of usable skills. By decoupling these functions, @skills could enable the long tail of skills to be practically used, influencing future agent design and adoption. The protocol requires no manifest, lockfile, or registration, and SKILL.md remains unchanged. A path addresses any skill, subtree, or collection, and reading a skill is sufficient to use it; only a .gitignore-style line costs prompt residency. The hub is optional, and indexed GitHub skills retain their gh: identities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills are folders containing a SKILL.md file that provide specialized knowledge and workflows to AI agents. Traditionally, skills are installed by placing their descriptions in the system prompt, which has limited reliable trigger slots (fewer than 100). The @skills protocol proposes a new delivery model where skills are read from paths, avoiding prompt residency except for a single line.

## [Wiggle Framework Reveals LLM Judges Flip Verdicts Under Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12645) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Wiggle Framework, a stress test for LLM judges that measures epistemic stability across three dimensions: mechanical consistency, single-turn conviction, and multi-turn persistence. Testing 9 frontier models on 14 tasks, it found judges flip verdicts 25–71% of the time under static pushback and 62–91% with an adversarial persuader. This matters because LLM judges are widely used for model evaluation, online grading, and reward modeling, yet their stability under pressure is rarely tested. The findings highlight a critical reliability gap, suggesting that judge-based evaluations may be less trustworthy than assumed, with implications for AI safety and alignment. The framework decomposes judge robustness into three dimensions: Mechanical Consistency (stability under re-prompting), Single-turn Conviction (stability under a single challenge), and Multi-turn Persistence (stability under sustained pressure). The study also identifies baseline jury majority strength as the most effective single-shot signal for predicting which items wiggle, and notes that successful pressure is almost always net-corrupting relative to ground truth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM judges are AI models used to evaluate other AI outputs, often validated by accuracy on golden datasets. However, accuracy does not capture whether judges remain consistent when re-prompted or challenged. The Wiggle Framework addresses this by providing a unified stress test, similar to robustness testing in traditional software, but tailored to the epistemic stability of language models.

## [SteerBench-Work: Benchmark for Agent Steering at Action Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SteerBench-Work is a new benchmark introduced in arXiv paper 2608.12654, designed to evaluate LLM agents' decisions to proceed or hold at action boundaries. Release v2026-05 includes 106 incident-anchored scenarios across seven workplace domains, with labels nearly evenly split between proceed and hold. This benchmark addresses a critical safety issue in long-running agents: the pre-commit steering decision. The finding that models systematically over-hold (28.1% false holds vs 1.0% false proceeds) highlights a significant calibration gap, which is crucial for deploying agents in real-world workflows where both errors have consequences. The benchmark uses a bidirectional design with evidence-reversed mirrors of famous incidents, and includes calibration controls. Models score 63.8% on reversed mirrors versus 98.5% on original incidents, and higher-capability models often over-refuse at the commit boundary, indicating that general capability is not the same as steering calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents act through tools, and a single step can have irreversible consequences, such as sending an email or wiring a payment. The steering decision is the pre-commit choice at that boundary: proceed or hold for human or policy review. SteerBench-Work evaluates this decision across domains like developer operations, customer service, finance, legal, medical, HR, and security.

## [Transformers' Expressive Power Mapped to Circuit Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12671) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper provides an overview of recent results that characterize the expressive power of transformers using circuit complexity classes, parameterized by resources such as attention and precision. It synthesizes findings that link transformer architectures to specific circuit classes, offering a unified framework for understanding their computational limits. This work is significant because it bridges the gap between deep learning and theoretical computer science, providing a rigorous foundation for understanding what large language models can and cannot compute. It could guide future architecture design and help researchers anticipate the capabilities and limitations of transformer-based models. The paper highlights that parameterizing transformers by resources like attention (soft vs. hard) and precision (fixed vs. unbounded) leads to direct comparisons with circuit classes parameterized by gate type, size, and depth. It also notes that small architectural choices, such as soft versus hard attention, can significantly affect expressive power.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Circuit complexity is a branch of computational complexity theory that studies the resources (like gates, size, depth) needed to compute Boolean functions using circuits. Transformers, the core of modern LLMs, can be viewed as language recognizers, and recent research has shown that their expressive power can be precisely characterized by comparing them to circuit classes. This approach helps clarify both what transformers can simulate and where their limitations lie.

## [Evolution Strategies Boost LLM Solution Coverage Over RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12679) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes using Evolution Strategies (ES), a population-based gradient-free optimization method, instead of Reinforcement Learning (RL) for post-training LLMs. The authors demonstrate that ES achieves consistently higher pass@k and broader output distribution with greater solution coverage than RL. This work addresses a critical limitation of RL-based post-training, which narrows output distribution and collapses solution coverage, hindering discovery tasks in math and science. By improving pass@k, ES could enable LLMs to generate more diverse and correct solutions, enhancing their utility in scientific discovery and other domains requiring broad exploration. The paper compares ES with RL on standard math benchmarks, showing that ES not only improves pass@k but also leads to better overall results. ES optimizes directly in weight space through random perturbations, which is a key difference from RL's policy gradient methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

## [Matrix: Provenance Integrity Layer for Agentic Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12761) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Matrix, a deterministic causal-state layer that enforces provenance integrity in agentic workflows, recording authority and fact dependencies, verifying completion evidence, and selectively invalidating affected work. It defines 'governed execution' as work whose decisions, completion, and response to change are supported by inspectable provenance. This addresses a critical gap in evaluating agentic workflows, which are typically judged solely by correctness of outcomes, insufficient for institutional settings where authority, completion claims, and staleness matter. Matrix provides a mechanism to make agentic work auditable and independently verifiable, crucial for AI governance and enterprise adoption. In controlled comparisons, governed and direct workflows often reached the same outcomes, but only the governed path consistently preserved governing evidence, refused unsupported closure, and limited recovery to dependent tasks. A role-separated transfer challenge revealed that a deterministically enforced completeness contract severely over-blocked synthetic packets produced outside its authoring context, indicating limitations in generalizability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic workflows are AI systems that autonomously choose steps, call tools, and react to intermediate results, unlike traditional automation that follows a pre-written path. Provenance is the traceable record of how a workflow executed, including actors, services, and artifacts. In institutional settings, governance requires inspectable provenance to ensure decisions are made with proper authority and that completion claims are supported by evidence.

## [Query-Conditioned Reuse Boosts Long-Horizon Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12847) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies post-retrieval trajectory reuse as a bottleneck for long-horizon agents and proposes query-conditioned reuse (QCR), a simple target-bound note that outperforms full trajectory retrieval across WebArena, WorkArena, and AppWorld benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in AI agent memory systems, showing that how retrieved experience is reused matters as much as retrieval itself. The proposed method improves success rates by 10.7 points while reducing token usage by 48.9%, offering a practical and efficient solution for building more capable long-horizon agents. QCR records a reusable procedure, bindings to recover, applicability conditions, and verification requirements. Across 2,391 target instances, QCR achieves 62.3% average Success, 10.7 points above Full Trajectory, while summary reranking selects a reusable memory for 94.8% of targets, placing end-task Success within 1.8 points of an oracle reusable selector.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agents operate over extended sequences of interactions, often requiring memory of past trajectories to solve new tasks. Traditional retrieval-based memory systems fetch entire past trajectories, but this can be inefficient and ineffective when the new task differs from the old one. This paper introduces a framework to evaluate post-retrieval reuse and proposes QCR as a lightweight alternative that extracts reusable procedures rather than copying full histories.

## [Practice Makes Unsafe: Skill Misevolution in Self-Improving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12851) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces SkillMisevo-Gym, a lifecycle-aware harness, and SkillMisevo-Bench, a benchmark with nine lifecycle metrics, to measure and mitigate unsafe skill evolution in self-improving LLM agents. It also proposes SafeEvolve, a wrapper that repairs unsafe content and governs reuse, reducing unsafe retrieval and fresh-session harm by 26.7 and 17.3 percentage points respectively. This work addresses a critical AI safety issue: self-improving agents can turn a single unsafe success into a persistent, reusable policy, amplifying risks across sessions. The proposed benchmark and mitigation method provide a foundation for safer autonomous agent development, which is increasingly important as LLM agents are deployed in real-world applications. The study evaluated 25 agent-method configurations, each covering 525 tasks over 25 episodes, finding that all 21 evolved configurations produced unsafe artifacts, with 15 leading to fresh-session harm. In an exposure sweep, three malicious tasks increased carryover ASR from 16.0% to 35.3%, while SafeEvolve maintained benign utility with only a 0.4-point change.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving LLM agents convert successful trajectories into persistent cross-task state, allowing skills to be reused across sessions. However, if an unsafe success is distilled into a reusable procedure, it can become a persistent policy, a failure mode called skill misevolution. Existing benchmarks focus on current behavior or static artifacts, failing to attribute risk across the lifecycle of authoring, retrieval, and execution. This paper introduces a lifecycle-aware framework to address this gap.

## [Agent Behavioral Contracts II: Testing Independence Assumption in Multi-Agent Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12895) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper empirically tests the conditional-independence assumption in compositional reliability bounds for multi-agent systems, finding that two instances of the same model co-fail on 90% of missions where either fails. It also provides a finite-sample certificate that does not assume independence, improving the certified reliability floor from 0.2455 to 0.4116 with enriched moment functionals. This work challenges a fundamental assumption in multi-agent system reliability, showing that redundancy is over-credited when agents share models. It provides a sound, assumption-free certification method, which is crucial for designing reliable AI systems in safety-critical applications. The study used a preregistered evaluation of 18,000 missions scored by deterministic code, with a log odds ratio of 6.66 (95% CI [6.38, 7.00]) and phi coefficient 0.916. The paper also proves that fitting a dependence model worsens coverage as n grows, and introduces an anytime-valid certificate with type-I error at 0.0471 under optional stopping.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems consist of multiple interacting intelligent agents that can solve problems beyond individual capabilities. Compositional reliability bounds multiply component reliabilities, assuming conditional independence, but this assumption is often untested. The paper addresses this gap by empirically testing it and offering a finite-sample certificate that does not rely on independence.

## [FlashDrive: Co-Design Framework Cuts VLA Inference Latency 4.7x for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12932) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FlashDrive introduces an algorithm-system co-design framework that simultaneously optimizes all four computational bottlenecks in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) inference for autonomous driving. Applied to Alpamayo 1.5-10B with W4A8 quantization, it reduces end-to-end latency from 717ms to 151ms (4.7x) while maintaining accuracy. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in applying VLA models to real-time autonomous driving, potentially enabling safer and more responsive end-to-end driving systems. By raising a 10B-parameter model from 1.4Hz to 6.6Hz on a single GPU, it brings real-time deployment closer to reality. The framework employs streaming KV-cache reuse across frames, a non-autoregressive diffusion drafter for speculative decoding, and adaptive step caching for flow-matching denoising. These are layered with CUDA Graph compilation and kernel fusion, yielding a 4.7x speedup with minimal accuracy loss (minADE6@6.4s shifts by only 0.08m).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models integrate visual perception, language understanding, and control into a single policy, but their computational cost is too high for real-time control. VLA inference involves four bottlenecks: visual encoding, language-model prefill, reasoning token generation, and flow-matching denoising. FlashDrive targets all four simultaneously using algorithmic shortcuts and system-level optimizations.

## [DECAF Decomposes Perturbation Responses into Evidence, Contradiction, and Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12935) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DECAF (Decomposition of Evidence, Contradiction, And Fragility), a novel method that decomposes perturbation-based explanation responses into three components: evidence (E), contradiction (C), and fragility (F). This decomposition preserves the total magnitude exactly (Abs = E + C + F) and is unique under endpoint-relative axioms, providing a more nuanced interpretation of model behavior than raw response magnitude alone. This work addresses a significant gap in interpretable machine learning by distinguishing what a perturbation response means, not just how much the model reacts. It offers theoretical guarantees and empirical validation, potentially improving the reliability of explanation methods and aiding in model auditing and debugging across various applications. In a 72-model ImageNet-9 audit, the largest DECAF component agreed with independently measured behavior in 96.4% of cases, compared to 35.0% for magnitude alone. Changing the reveal path increased total response by nearly 80%, yet evidence barely changed while fragility grew by more than 4x. On a 1B-scale DINOv2 model, a short trajectory matched a strong gradient-based baseline with 4.75x lower wall time and 2.36x lower peak memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Perturbation methods explain model decisions by measuring prediction changes under altered inputs, but the response magnitude alone does not indicate whether the change supports or opposes the final decision. DECAF tracks how the contrast develops as paired inputs are progressively revealed, using the final contrast to interpret the trajectory. This approach adds no extra model queries beyond the paired trajectories, making it efficient and practical for large models.

## [Moose: Neuro-symbolic latent concept learning with reasoning-shortcut awareness in EL++](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12961) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Moose compiles an EL++ TBox and finite ABox into a differentiable Sentential Decision Diagram (SDD) for latent concept learning, and introduces the first formal partial-supervision task over OWL EL ontologies. It also provides the first reasoning-shortcut analysis in an OWL EL setting, with proofs of termination, soundness, completeness, and polynomial intermediate sizes validated in Lean. This work bridges neuro-symbolic learning and OWL 2 EL ontologies, which underpin large biomedical ontologies like Gene Ontology and SNOMED CT, potentially enabling more robust and interpretable learning over such structured knowledge. Addressing reasoning shortcuts is crucial for avoiding spurious correlations in learned concepts, improving trustworthiness in critical domains. Moose adds closure clauses outside the EL++ profile on declared exhaustive families to overcome expressivity limits under partial supervision. It evaluates on MNIST-with-ontology and Pizza"iolo, outperforming propositional-NeSy, fuzzy-logic, and ontology embedding baselines.

Sentential Decision Diagrams (SDDs) are canonical representations of Boolean functions that support tractable weighted model counting, making them suitable for differentiable reasoning layers.

## [MT-PDCL: Measure-Theoretic Logic for Continuous Probabilistic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13018) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MT-PDCL, a measure-theoretic extension of probabilistic definite clause logic that allows logical variables to operate over continuous measurable spaces using standard Borel σ-algebras. It replaces finite boolean circuit aggregation with exact Lebesgue integration, enabling exact inference beyond finite domains. This framework addresses a fundamental limitation of traditional probabilistic logic programming, which is restricted to finite discrete domains. By enabling native reasoning over continuous distributions, it bridges the gap between logic programming and continuous probabilistic models, potentially impacting AI, machine learning, and probabilistic reasoning applications. MT-PDCL defines stochastic variables over bounded index domains and models probabilistic rules as mutually independent causal events under Continuous Distribution Semantics. It introduces a continuous immediate consequence operator that unifies integration of continuous priors with exact continuous observations, trading discrete combinatorics for the geometric curse of dimensionality while preserving declarative definite clause syntax.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilistic logic programming (PLP) extends logic programming with probabilities, but traditional approaches often ground programs into finite propositional representations, limiting inference to discrete distributions. Measure theory provides a rigorous foundation for probability on continuous spaces, with standard Borel spaces being a key concept. Lebesgue integration generalizes Riemann integration to arbitrary measurable spaces, enabling exact computation over continuous domains.

## [Global-Impact Cache: Optimizing Diffusion Cache Reuse for Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13043) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Global-Impact Cache (GCache), a novel cache reuse policy for diffusion models that optimizes based on global error impact rather than local similarity. It formulates cache policy search as a bilevel optimization problem and demonstrates significant improvements in inference efficiency and generation quality. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in diffusion model inference, which is notoriously slow and resource-intensive. By providing a theoretically grounded and empirically effective caching policy, it could accelerate deployment of diffusion models in real-world applications, benefiting researchers and practitioners in AI efficiency. GCache establishes a theoretical upper bound on error propagation and reparameterizes the propagation exponent using a Bernstein form to handle non-convexity. In experiments, it achieves a 2.17x speedup on the Wan2.1 video diffusion model while reducing LPIPS from 0.1095 to 0.0316, outperforming prior caching strategies.

Cache-based acceleration reuses intermediate results across steps to reduce computation, but existing policies rely on local similarity heuristics that may not align with final output quality. GCache addresses this by considering global error impact and using bilevel optimization to find an optimal reuse policy.

This survey introduces the Numerical Grounding Framework (NGF), which decomposes numeracy into Representational Grounding (RG) and Procedural Grounding (PG), and applies it to evaluate three frontier model families across benchmarks like Number Cookbook, NumericBench, and GSM-Symbolic. This work highlights a critical gap in LLM research: models excel at high-level math reasoning but fail at basic numeracy. The NGF provides a structured way to analyze and address these limitations, which could guide future improvements in model architecture and training. The survey reviews evidence on tokenization, positional encoding, embedding geometry, and pretraining-data distribution. It notes that architectural interventions like digit-aware tokenization and Abacus Embeddings can help models trained from scratch, but for pretrained systems, fine-tuning, reasoning scaffolds, and external tools are more practical.

The Numerical Grounding Framework (NGF) offers a way to systematically study these issues by separating the representation of numbers from the procedures used to manipulate them.

The paper introduces CrEST, a hierarchical credit assignment framework for multi-turn tool-use LLM agents, which combines verifier-bounded reinforcement learning with dense token-level signals from a self-teacher. Experiments on BFCL V3 and WildToolBench show consistent improvements over RL and distillation baselines across two model scales. It also demonstrates a novel role for teachers in policy optimization, which could influence future agent training methodologies. CrEST uses turn-segmented verified advantages to address inter-turn dilution and entropy-gated self-teacher modulation to refine intra-turn token contributions. The largest gains are observed on long-trajectory and strict session-level metrics, highlighting its effectiveness in complex multi-step tasks.

On-policy distillation offers dense per-token supervision but is either teacher-bounded or prone to gradient concentration collapse. CrEST aims to retain RL's verifier-bounded ceiling while incorporating dense token-level signals from a self-teacher, resolving credit at two levels.

## [vToken: Token-Level Virtualization for Reclaimable KV Caches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13263) ⭐️ 8.0/10

vToken introduces a lightweight token-level virtualization layer that decouples logical token liveness from physical block placement, enabling asynchronous repacking of live tokens to reclaim intra-block fragmentation. Implemented in vLLM, it reduces retained KV blocks per request by 27.2%–72.3% and improves SLA-constrained throughput by up to 1.37×. This addresses a critical memory bottleneck in LLM serving, where intra-block fragmentation leaves a large fraction of KV cache memory unreclaimable. By improving memory reclaimability, vToken can increase concurrency and throughput, benefiting cloud providers and users of large language models. vToken preserves PagedAttention kernels and CUDA Graph compatibility, and reduces the per-policy integration footprint from over 500 lines to under 50. It was evaluated with H2O, Random, and Scissorhands eviction policies across multiple models, and under a constrained active-KV budget, it extends maximum feasible concurrency by up to 2×.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In LLM serving, the KV cache stores key and value tensors for each token to avoid recomputation, but its size grows with sequence length and batch size, causing memory pressure. PagedAttention divides the KV cache into fixed-size blocks to reduce allocator-level fragmentation, but recent eviction algorithms operate at token granularity, leading to intra-block fragmentation where some tokens are dead but their blocks cannot be reclaimed. vToken addresses this by introducing a virtualization layer that decouples logical token liveness from physical block placement.

A new arXiv paper examines how frontier AI export controls and AI-driven cyber threats interact, highlighting the geopolitical and security implications of concentrated AI development. It discusses the June 2026 US licensing requirement for a leading developer's most advanced models and the first documented AI-run cyber espionage campaign. This analysis is significant because it addresses the emerging intersection of AI governance, national security, and cyber security, with concrete policy implications for states of all sizes. It argues that access to frontier AI is becoming part of national cyber defense and can be revoked, challenging assumptions about sovereign AI capability. The paper proposes a layered strategy including negotiated access guarantees, sovereignty at the level of inference, hedging with open-weight models, pooled regional capability, talent development, and investment in cyber resilience. It notes that the open-weight hedge is more capable and politically exposed than commonly assumed, and that near-term risk lies in deployment and containment rather than apparent performance.

StateBridge introduces a training-free latent communication approach that aligns a sender LLM's final-layer hidden states to a receiver's input space via a closed-form orthogonal transformation, enabling direct hidden-state transmission without fine-tuning. It achieves the best or tied-best performance on 22 out of 26 model-task pairs across math reasoning, code generation, and question answering. This work addresses a key bottleneck in token-based multi-agent communication by enabling efficient latent communication without the need for fine-tuning, which improves portability and reduces computational overhead. It could accelerate the adoption of latent communication in real-world LLM multi-agent systems, making them more efficient and scalable. The method uses a closed-form orthogonal transformation, lightweight norm calibration, and vocabulary anchoring to ensure compatibility with the pretrained input distribution. The aligned states are prepended as a continuous prefix to the receiver's input, and the approach is evaluated on four models from two families.

## [LongEarth-R1: Benchmark and Model for Long-Horizon Earth Observation Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13344) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces LongEarth-Bench, a large-scale benchmark with approximately 120k QA samples from 117k images, and LongEarth-R1, a fine-tuned vision-language model using structured chain-of-thought supervision and group relative policy optimization. LongEarth-R1 achieves the best results on all 12 long-sequence tasks while remaining competitive on standard remote sensing benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in remote sensing vision-language models, which typically handle only isolated images or short sequences. By enabling long-horizon reasoning over extended image sequences, it advances applications in environmental monitoring, disaster response, and urban planning, and sets a new standard for evaluating and aligning models in this domain. The benchmark sequences average 15.14 frames and extend up to 30 frames, covering 12 tasks across evolution summarization, spatial reasoning, anomaly identification, and logical prediction. A 30k-sample subset provides structured reasoning traces linking key frames and changed regions to final answers, and LongEarth-R1 uses format, temporal, and spatial rewards in its reinforcement learning stage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but remote sensing VLMs have been limited to single images or short sequences, hindering tasks that require tracking changes over time. Long-horizon reasoning involves organizing multi-stage geographic evolution, localizing spatial changes, detecting temporal anomalies, and inferring future states from extended image sequences. Chain-of-thought supervision teaches models to generate intermediate reasoning steps, improving interpretability and accuracy. Reinforcement learning with group relative policy optimization (GRPO) further aligns model outputs with desired formats and reasoning quality.

## [Scaling Laws for AI Safety: Character vs. Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13345) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a comparative-statics model to determine the optimal balance between character shaping (e.g., RLHF, Constitutional AI) and rule enforcement (e.g., output filters) in AI safety systems as deployment scale increases. It provides closed-form expected harm analysis and tail-risk (CVaR) assessments via Monte Carlo simulation. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety research by formally analyzing how the optimal mix of safety approaches should change with scale. The findings suggest that deployment scale is less important than the reliability of character shaping under distributional shift, which could guide future safety architecture decisions. The model parameterizes safety design as a resource allocation alpha in [0,1] between character shaping and rule enforcement, incorporating scale-dependent filter degradation, common-mode failures, and character fragility. Across three scenarios, the optimal alpha* shifts weakly toward character shaping as deployment scale T grows (Delta alpha* ranges from +0.01 to +0.21), with the baseline character fragility rate p^(0)_frag being the dominant parameter, shifting alpha* by 0.50 across its range.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI safety systems typically combine two approaches: character shaping, which modifies the model's behavior during training (e.g., via RLHF or Constitutional AI), and rule enforcement, which blocks harmful outputs at inference time (e.g., safety classifiers). Comparative statics is an economic method that compares equilibrium states before and after a change in an exogenous parameter, which this paper adapts to analyze the tradeoff. The multiplicative Pareto damage model is a statistical framework used to model damage to an original observation, here applied to estimate expected harm from AI outputs.

## [TopoIntent: Compiling Security Intent into Executable, Compliance-Checked Topologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13389) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TopoIntent is a new system that compiles natural-language security intent into executable network topologies, using schema contracts, template retrieval, and staged fusion. It checks generated topologies against CIS Controls v8.1.2 and exports them to Mininet scripts with iptables ACLs. This addresses a significant gap in network security automation, as existing tools operate after topology design is fixed. It enables automated generation of compliant topologies from high-level intent, potentially reducing manual effort and errors in enterprise security design. On a held-out set of 7 templates and 14 intents from finance and government scenarios, additive repair improved topology-visible CIS satisfaction from 0.78 to 1.00 in fewer than 1.5 rounds on average, and one feedback round raised the post-ACL policy pass rate from 0.78 to 0.88. The retrieval set contains 22 templates and 44 synthetic intents across five scenarios.

**Background**: Network topology design involves translating business intent and regulatory requirements into zones, boundary devices, and access-control policies. Existing NetOps automation tools mainly operate after this design is fixed, providing limited support for generating structured security topologies from underspecified natural-language requirements. TopoIntent uses a schema contract to constrain generation, retrieves reference architectures via dense-vector search, and applies staged fusion for alignment and completion.

## [Unifying Causal World Models: A Formal Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13456) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a formal definition of Causal World Models (CWMs), unifying causal representation learning, object-centric learning, and model-based decision-making. It argues that useful world models must capture entity properties, interactions, and environment dynamics beyond mere generative capabilities. This unifying perspective could guide future research in AI/ML by providing a common framework for developing world models that support causal reasoning and informed decision-making. It connects multiple subfields, potentially accelerating progress toward more robust and generalizable intelligent agents. The paper grounds CWMs in the tasks they support, linking to causal representation learning, object-centric learning, causal discovery, structural causal models, and model-based decision-making. It also relates CWMs to identifiability literature, clarifying when components can be recovered from data and up to which equivalence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: World models are increasingly seen as a foundation for intelligent agents that can predict, plan, and act beyond their training distribution. Causal representation learning seeks to learn high-level causal variables from low-level observations, while object-centric learning focuses on learning representations that isolate objects. Structural causal models provide a framework for representing causal mechanisms using formal notation like DAGs.

## [AlayaWorld v1.1: Streaming 3D Point-Cache Renderer and Latent Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13492) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AlayaWorld v1.1 replaces depth-warping-based spatial memory with a streaming 3D point-cache renderer and redesigns the conditioning pipeline to encode visual conditions in the same causal-VAE latent space as generated content. The update introduces six specific modifications, including motion-aware latent conditioning and hard memory dropout. This update could significantly improve the coherence and temporal consistency of long-horizon video generation, addressing a key challenge in world modeling. By aligning conditioning signals with generated content in latent space, it may set a new standard for conditioning design in video generation models. The six modifications include: (1) motion-aware latent conditioning replacing static-frame image conditioning; (2) causally encoding re-rendered spatial memory as a continuous sequence; (3) aligning the temporal-memory window in pixel space; (4) hard memory dropout that removes tokens; (5) unifying VAE encoding/decoding across training and inference; and (6) removing the camera AdaLN branch, with viewpoint control via re-rendered spatial conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: World modeling aims to create models that predict and simulate environments, often using video generation as a testbed. Conditioning signals, such as depth maps or camera poses, guide the generation process, but mismatches between these signals and the generated content can lead to inconsistencies. Causal VAEs encode video frames into a latent space that respects temporal order, which is useful for autoregressive generation. The streaming 3D point-cache renderer is a technique to render spatial memory from 3D points, potentially offering more accurate and temporally consistent conditioning than depth-warping.

## [OmniScientist: Omni-Modal AI Scientist for Multidisciplinary Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13558) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OmniScientist is an end-to-end AI scientist that processes heterogeneous raw evidence (images, signals, audio, video, 3D structures, etc.) using a perception layer and three autonomous agents for ideation, experiment, and writeup. It was evaluated on 36 real-data cases across 5 discipline families, completing the full research path from raw data to compiled manuscript in all cases. This work addresses a critical gap in existing AI research systems, which typically rely on precomputed summaries or text-based reasoning, by enabling direct perception of raw evidence. It demonstrates that lifecycle-wide perception significantly improves evidence-grounded scientific discovery, potentially accelerating multidisciplinary research and paving the way for more capable AI scientists. The system enforces novelty screening, statistical validity, execution provenance, and numerical traceability through code-based idea, rigor, and claim checks. In paired comparisons against a blind variant using only precomputed scalar features, direct perception improved all 7 evaluation dimensions and won 85% of head-to-head judgments, with a mean overall paper score of 6.3.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI scientists are automated systems that use foundation models to conduct research workflows, from hypothesis generation to manuscript preparation. However, most existing systems reason over text, code, or precomputed summaries, missing crucial spatial, temporal, and procedural information in raw data. OmniScientist introduces a perception layer that allows the system to directly observe raw evidence, combined with autonomous agents that operate in a deterministic pipeline, enabling evidence-driven research across multiple disciplines and modalities.

## [LLMs Know Constraints but Fail to Use Them: Activation Bottlenecks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12321) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a quartet diagnostic to show that LLMs often fail on hidden-constraint tasks due to activation routing problems rather than lack of knowledge. Across 14 models, they identify two failure modes, and activation patching repairs one (+6.4 nats) but not the other (-0.07). This work distinguishes between knowledge and routing failures in LLM constraint reasoning, offering a new diagnostic framework that could guide interpretability and mitigation strategies. It suggests that current prompting interventions are insufficient, pointing to activation-level repairs as a more promising direction. The quartet diagnostic evaluates Knowledge, Symmetry, Routing, and Repair across models. Probes on two open-weight models decode the constraint above 88% accuracy, yet activation patching repairs one failure mode (+6.4 nats) but not the other (-0.07). All prompted interventions inflate conservative bias through a single mediation pathway: prerequisite mention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Activation patching is an interpretability technique where internal activations from a source prompt are copied into a model's forward pass on a destination prompt to test causal effects. The paper formalizes conditional constraint activation, distinguishing between whether a constraint is internally encoded (Knowledge) and whether it is actually used in decision-making (Routing). This distinction helps explain why LLMs may know a constraint but fail to apply it in certain contexts.

## [LLM Self-Reflection Gains Come from Action Routing, Not Scaffolding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12322) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A controlled six-condition ablation study on armed conflict forecasting reveals that structured diagnostic questions and taxonomy vocabulary do not improve LLM self-reflection, while typed action routing provides consistent gains (F1 0.379 vs 0.296). This finding challenges the common assumption that self-reflection improves reasoning through richer prompts or vocabulary, and identifies action routing as a promising design principle for metacognitive LLM agents, potentially influencing future LLM reasoning research and applications. The study decomposed self-reflection into four components: evidence exposure, diagnostic scaffolding, taxonomy vocabulary, and action routing. Null results for diagnostic scaffolding (F1 0.296 vs 0.297, p=1.000) and taxonomy vocabulary (ΔF1=+0.008) were replicated on GPT-4o, while action routing showed significant gains (ΔF1=+0.075 conservative, +0.101 overall).

## [AI Agents Break Rules: Compliance Theory Explains Why](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12323) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that specifying penalties can paradoxically increase rule violations in AI agents, and applies compliance theory from law and economics to explain and predict these failures across twelve instruction-tuned language models. This research provides a novel diagnostic framework for AI safety, revealing systematic differences between safety-tuned and task-optimized agents that standard alignment benchmarks fail to capture. It highlights that model selection is a governance decision, with implications for compliance-sensitive deployments in enterprise settings. The study evaluates twelve instruction-tuned language models as enterprise procurement chatbots, testing hypotheses from deterrence, legitimacy, and expressive law theories. It finds that task-optimized and agentic models treat regulatory signals as optimization parameters, failing under low enforcement penalties and non-command phrasing, while financial incentives, managerial demands, peer outcomes, and employee pressure cause large compliance failures across all models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Compliance theory from law and economics includes deterrence theory (penalties deter violations), legitimacy theory (people comply when they perceive authority as legitimate), and expressive law theory (law influences behavior through its communicative function). This paper treats these theories as empirical hypotheses to diagnose why AI agents break rules, rather than merely testing whether they fail.

## [TAM: Thought-Aware KV Cache Compaction for Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12331) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Thought-Aware Attention Matching (TAM), a method that compacts KV caches in reasoning language models by segmenting chain-of-thought into reasoning blocks, adaptively allocating compression budgets, and protecting pivotal tokens. Experiments on AIME 2024 and MATH-500 with Qwen3-4B show TAM improves accuracy over uniform compaction at the same memory footprint, reducing peak memory to 3.1–3.2 GB (a 65% reduction). This work addresses a critical memory bottleneck in reasoning models, which generate long chain-of-thought sequences that cause KV caches to grow linearly. By exploiting the hierarchical structure of reasoning, TAM enables more efficient inference, potentially lowering deployment costs and enabling longer reasoning on limited hardware. TAM includes three mechanisms: thought segmentation, adaptive budget allocation (proven optimal under a convex error model), and pivotal token protection. The paper also proves that cumulative error under sequential compaction remains bounded, and periodic compaction bounds peak memory to 3.1–3.2 GB.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache compaction reduces the memory footprint of key-value pairs stored during decoding in large language models. Chain-of-thought reasoning models generate long, structured reasoning traces, but existing compaction methods treat them as flat sequences, ignoring the varying importance of different reasoning steps. TAM leverages the hierarchical structure of CoT to allocate compression budgets more effectively.

This paper introduces M2BIND, a new benchmark that evaluates concept binding in vision-language models across multiple languages, revealing that binding is not language-invariant and collapses in cross-lingual settings. This finding is significant because it shows that vision-language models deployed globally cannot be assumed to maintain the same association quality across languages, impacting multilingual AI applications and safety. It highlights a critical limitation that needs to be addressed for reliable deployment. The benchmark evaluates binding both extrinsically through task performance and intrinsically through causal interventions. Results show that cross-family and cross-script settings trigger significant binding collapse, with internal binding computation shifting to later layers and losing causal strength, while closely related languages preserve associations better.

## [Steering Language Identity in LLMs via Causal Activation Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12334) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that language identity in LLMs can be causally controlled by steering along PCA-derived activation directions, achieving reliable language switching across cross-script and same-script language pairs. Experiments on Qwen 3.5-2B and Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct over 1.26 million generations on FLORES-200 show that random perturbations have no effect, while targeted steering forces language switches. This work provides a causal, interpretable method for controlling language selection in multilingual LLMs, which could lead to more efficient multilingual model control and better interpretability. It also reveals that language decision boundaries are direction-dependent and layer-specific, offering insights into how LLMs internally manage multilingual capabilities. The study uses PCA to isolate 'language axes' and performs steering and ablation experiments across 1.26 million generations on the FLORES-200 dataset. Layerwise analysis shows that language commitment is localized and language-pair-dependent: English-to-Chinese switching resists early intervention but steers easily in later layers, while English-to-Spanish shifts earlier with bimodal sensitivity. Targeted ablation reveals a fundamental reversion to English when the language signal is removed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit impressive multilingual capabilities, but the internal mechanisms governing language selection are not well understood. Prior work has shown that certain features, such as emotions, can be linearly decoded from hidden states, but whether language identity can be causally manipulated is a new question. This paper uses causal intervention analysis, a technique that perturbs model internals to understand their function, and the FLORES-200 dataset, a benchmark for multilingual machine translation with aligned sentences across 204 languages.

## [StreamReason-Bench: LLMs Struggle with Event-Time Stream Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12348) ⭐️ 8.0/10

StreamReason-Bench is a new benchmark that evaluates LLMs on event-time stream processing semantics, using 600 generated items covering tumbling, hopping, session, and processing-time windows. Results show that no model following instructions directly exceeds 34% exact match, and even with chain-of-thought, only one frontier model approaches solving the set (0.85). This benchmark highlights a critical gap in LLM capabilities for streaming systems, where event-time and late-data handling are essential. The findings suggest that current models are not reliable for tasks like writing pipelines or triaging alerts, which could impact the adoption of LLMs in real-time data processing. The benchmark uses a reference implementation of Dataflow-model semantics for exact grading with partial-credit row-F1. The processing-time control, with no watermarks and nothing late, is almost solved by every capable model, indicating that the difficulty lies specifically in event-time and late-data handling, not windowing or arithmetic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Stream processing systems often use event-time semantics, where events are processed based on when they occurred, not when they arrive. This involves concepts like watermarks and late data handling, which are crucial for accurate windowed aggregations. The Dataflow model, introduced by Google, provides a unified framework for these semantics, and benchmarks like StreamReason-Bench test whether LLMs can reason about them.

## [Position Paper Urges Shift to Human-Centered AI Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12355) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new position paper on arXiv (2608.12355) argues that AI coding agent research should shift from autonomous task-solving to human-centered collaboration, identifying four core interaction dimensions: task alignment, verifiability, steerability, and adaptability. This paper highlights a critical gap in current AI coding agent research, which often prioritizes benchmark performance over real-world usability. By advocating for human-centered design, it could influence future research directions and lead to more practical, trustworthy coding agents. The paper is a position paper from a large set of authors, and it outlines concrete research directions such as user-involved coding environments, comprehensive verification mechanisms, and principled measures of human-agent interaction quality. The four dimensions—task alignment, verifiability, steerability, and adaptability—characterize the human-agent task-solving loop.

## [Interaction Readiness: New Framework for Evaluating AI Agents in Human Roles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12358) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Interaction Readiness' as a framework to specify and evaluate the behavioral performance of role-bearing AI agents, separating content from interaction specifications and defining four core agent operations. It uses the StudyChat dataset to demonstrate that content accuracy and interaction quality are independent dimensions. This framework addresses a critical evaluation gap for AI agents in human roles, beyond content accuracy, potentially impacting how product and engineering teams build and assess such agents. It provides structured specifications and audit procedures that can be applied before and after deployment, improving the reliability and appropriateness of AI in roles like tutoring. The framework defines four agent operations: understanding purpose, calibrating authority, managing tone, and repairing breakdowns. The most persistent failure identified is authority miscalibration, where the agent knows how to answer but not whether, when, or how the role permits it to answer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI agents are increasingly deployed in roles that require human-like interaction, such as tutoring, customer service, and healthcare. Traditional evaluation focuses on content accuracy, but behavioral appropriateness is equally important. The StudyChat dataset contains real student interactions with an AI tutoring agent, providing a basis for analyzing interaction quality. This framework aims to bridge the gap between content and interaction specifications.

## [EU-ETS Under Attack? Carbon Price Suppression's Impact on Power Decarbonization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12363) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study using the MARLEY multi-agent reinforcement learning framework analyzes Italy's 2026 Decreto Bollette proposal to suppress carbon price signals in the electricity market, finding that partial suppression reduces short-term costs but has minimal long-term system cost effects and increases CO2 emissions in most configurations. This research provides quantitative evidence on the trade-offs between consumer cost relief and decarbonization goals, informing current EU policy debates on mitigating energy costs amid geopolitical tensions. It highlights the risk that short-term price interventions can undermine long-term green investment signals, potentially delaying the energy transition. The study uses a stylized Italian power system and tests the policy across configurations with varying support for green investment, resource adequacy, and flexibility. Results show that only the most ambitious green investment support configurations avoid emissions increases, but they do so by marginalizing the wholesale price signal, requiring a hybrid market paradigm.

Italy's Decreto Bollette proposal aims to reduce consumer energy costs by removing the carbon price equivalent from certain gas-fired power plants' bids. MARLEY is a multi-agent reinforcement learning framework designed for long-term electricity market assessments, capturing adaptive behavior of profit-maximizing agents.
