## [FluctlightDB Proposes Memory Model for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12365) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FluctlightDB introduces an embedded engine that implements a new data model for long-term agent memory, with write semantics (encoding, separation, consolidation, provenance) and read semantics (cue-driven activation across a linked memory graph). It reports high recall on LoCoMo (99.0%) and LongMemEval-S (97.6% retrieval, 97.4% end-to-end QA). This work addresses a significant gap in existing data systems, which are built for relational or vector queries but not for cue-driven, provenance-weighted recall across long sessions. By proposing a distinct memory model and releasing an embeddable engine, it could influence how AI agents manage long-term memory and spur further research in this area. The engine is available via pip install "fluctlightdb[native]" and uses experience() and activate() as core APIs. On BEIR SciFact, CHORUS/PRISM slightly outperforms Chroma on nDCG@10 (0.646 vs. 0.645) and Recall@10 (0.792 vs. 0.783). The authors do not claim novelty over Mem0, Zep, or HippoRAG, but propose an embedded engine contract beneath them.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional data models include the relational model (matching records by predicates) and the vector model (finding nearest vectors). Neither is designed for long-term agent memory, which requires cue-driven activation and provenance-weighted recall across sessions. FluctlightDB treats this as a third data model, with its own write and read semantics, and provides an embedded engine for practical use. Benchmarks like LoCoMo and LongMemEval are used to evaluate long-term memory capabilities of AI systems.

The paper argues that LLMs offer superior experimental access to internal states, which can inform biological hypotheses. This cross-disciplinary framework could accelerate both AI and neuroscience research by enabling experimental logic transfer from LLMs to biological studies. It highlights a unique opportunity to use LLMs as tools for generating sharper hypotheses about memory mechanisms. The comparison spans multiple levels: synapses, neuronal ensembles, and hippocampal-cortical interactions in brains, versus weights, activations, context windows, and retrieval systems in LLMs. The paper notes that human studies have limited intervention capabilities, while rodent studies offer selective causal access, but LLMs allow direct and repeatable manipulation of internal states.

## [AI Agent Dismantles Core Invariant Across 189 Files in 717k-Line Codebase](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12440) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A case study reports that an AI coding agent, using a specification-first protocol, successfully dismantled a core architectural invariant across 189 files in a 717,725-line TypeScript codebase, with no human review and no test oracle. The task, previously deemed infeasible via incremental refactoring, was completed in three days at a cost of USD 2,430. This demonstrates that AI agents can handle large-scale architectural refactoring tasks that were previously considered infeasible without a rewrite, potentially transforming software maintenance practices. It also highlights the viability of specification-first protocols in guiding AI agents to produce correct code without human oversight, which could reduce costs and accelerate development cycles. The protocol involved formal specification by the agent, 14 refinement cycles, atomic implementation, a compile/test feedback loop, and 17 verification cycles, totaling 31 audit passes that corrected 201 defects before human execution. The change touched 189 files (31 new), with two commits totaling 288 files, 34,770 insertions, and 16,422 deletions; the full specification and 1,500+ pages of session logs (in French) are published as evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Architectural invariants are properties that must hold true at all times in a codebase; violating them can compromise security, portability, or maintainability. Specification-first protocols involve formally specifying desired behavior before implementation, which can guide AI agents to produce correct code. This case study explores whether such protocols can enable AI agents to perform complex refactoring tasks that traditionally require human expertise and extensive testing.

## [SSPO: Structure-Aware Preference Optimization Boosts Neural Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12443) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SSPO introduces a structure-aware, similarity-weighted preference optimization method for neural combinatorial optimization (NCO), which jointly scores all sampled solutions using a dissimilarity-weighted leave-one-out baseline. This approach addresses gradient signal polarization and baseline redundancy, achieving consistent gains on TSP, EFL, and JSP benchmarks. This method improves training signal quality in NCO, potentially leading to better performance across various combinatorial optimization tasks. The deployment of an SSPO-trained policy in JD.com's production facility-location system demonstrates its practical viability at scale, which could influence future NCO research and industrial applications. SSPO uses zero-parameter, problem-adaptive solution embeddings built from the encoder's existing node representations to compute the dissimilarity-weighted baseline. Experiments show that structure-aware weighting is the primary driver of improvement, as confirmed by a direct comparison against uniform RLOO on TSP and EFL.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses neural networks to solve NP-hard problems like the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) by learning heuristics from data. Existing training methods, such as preference optimization and mean-based baselines, either anchor on the single best solution or weight all peers uniformly, leading to gradient signal polarization and baseline redundancy. SSPO addresses these issues by jointly scoring all sampled solutions with a dissimilarity-weighted leave-one-out baseline.

## [EgoCITE: Context-Augmented Indexing and Time-Aware Retrieval for Egocentric Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12627) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EgoCITE introduces a novel framework for long-horizon egocentric memory that combines context-augmented indexing (EgoScheme, EgoIndex) with time-aware retrieval (EgoRetrv). It improves answer accuracy by 4.4-14.2% over agentic memory baselines and achieves 36x lower cost than long-context LLM agents. This work addresses two critical bottlenecks in egocentric memory systems—context-poor indexing and time-agnostic retrieval—which are essential for embodied AI and long-horizon video understanding. By improving accuracy and reducing cost, EgoCITE could enable more practical and scalable agentic memory applications. EgoCITE consists of three components: EgoScheme uses local multimodal context to create self-contained atomic memory indices; EgoIndex organizes multi-view memory indices at multiple granularities; EgoRetrv combines semantic search with question-conditioned temporal relevance scoring. It was evaluated on EgoLifeQA, EgoMem, and EgoR1-Bench, showing significant improvements in answer accuracy and target-event retrieval alignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon egocentric memory systems convert continuous first-person video and audio into searchable records of past experiences. Existing systems often rely on context-poor captions for indexing, which are unreliable for agentic search, and they ignore the temporal intent of questions during retrieval. EgoCITE aims to overcome these limitations by augmenting indexing with local multimodal context and incorporating time-aware retrieval mechanisms.

## [Dual-Signal LLM Watermark Detects Tampering and Proves Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12713) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced a novel LLM watermark that co-embeds a robust signal and a fragile signal into each generated token, enabling simultaneous provenance tracing and tamper detection. This method mitigates piggyback spoofing attacks, where adversaries alter content while retaining attribution. This addresses a critical security gap in LLM watermarking, as existing methods prioritize robustness but are vulnerable to piggyback spoofing. The dual-signal approach enhances AI safety and provenance verification, benefiting content authenticity and accountability in AI-generated text. The method uses independent keys and different seeding windows over normalized text, with multiple rounds of unbiased tournament reweighting to preserve the expected generation distribution. Detection yields a two-dimensional score space supporting three decisions: Intact, Tampered, and No-Watermark. Experiments on two LLMs and two prompt datasets show the highest tamper-detection rate while maintaining competitive attribution robustness and perplexity.

**Background**: LLM watermarking embeds subtle signals in generated text to trace provenance, but robust watermarks allow adversaries to alter content while retaining attribution—a piggyback spoofing attack. This paper introduces a dual-signal watermark that combines robust and fragile signals to address this vulnerability, building on prior work in watermarking and spoofing defense.

PatientAct introduces a theory-grounded framework for LLM-based mental health client simulation, integrating the 5Ps case formulation and dynamic trust thresholds to create more realistic, less cooperative clients. ## [Step-Level Self-Distillation Boosts Deep Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12764) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Step-Level Self-Distilled Policy Optimization (SSPO), a method that uses Evidence Anchors—concise step-level evidence snippets from the web—as privileged information to train deep search agents. On Qwen3-8B, SSPO consistently outperforms GRPO across BrowseComp, GAIA, and FRAMES, matching or surpassing GRPO trained with twice as many gradient steps while adding only about 5% overhead per step. This work addresses a critical challenge in training deep search agents: sparse outcome rewards hinder credit assignment. By providing a step-level training signal, SSPO could significantly improve the efficiency and effectiveness of search agent training, impacting fields like AI-assisted research and autonomous web navigation. SSPO converts teacher-student disagreement into step-level advantage weights within GRPO, applied only to incorrect trajectories, decoupling what to update from how much to update. Correct trajectories are left untouched to preserve diversity, and the method adds only a single additional forward pass per step.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Deep search agents operate over long trajectories, but standard reinforcement learning provides only a single outcome reward per trajectory, which is too sparse for effective credit assignment. On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) uses the model's own logits as dense token-level teachers, but extending it to search agents introduces a tension because the teacher has privileged information (e.g., the correct answer) that differs from the student's exploration-based reasoning. Evidence Anchors are step-level evidence snippets that capture key reasoning steps without revealing the entire answer path, helping to resolve this tension.

## [Memorization Diagnostics for Code LLMs Must Be Scale-Aware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12771) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper demonstrates that existing probing techniques for detecting memorization in code LLMs degrade severely as model size increases, and proposes using invertible mathematical transforms to separate memorization from representation load. This work challenges the reliability of current memorization evaluations, which could mislead assessments of code LLMs' generalization and usability. By advocating for scale-aware diagnostics, it offers a more nuanced view of what memorization means for practical software engineering, potentially reshaping future evaluation methodologies. The study finds that encoder-style probes using perturbations like synonym fuzzing or dead-code insertion fail on scaled models, and decoder-style probes relying on log probabilities also degrade. The proposed method applies invertible transforms to numeric problems, showing that scaled encoders can absorb representation load while still converging on correct solution families.

**Background**: Large language models for code are often evaluated for memorization versus genuine understanding, but current probing techniques may not scale with model size. The paper introduces the concept of representation load, which refers to the model's ability to adapt to varying surface forms of a problem, and argues that separating this from memorization is crucial for fair evaluation.

## [PIPES: New Defense Against State-Corruption Attacks on AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12789) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced PIPES (Provenance-Informed, Prior-Enforced Screening), a mechanism that screens tool responses using semantic priors and source provenance to prevent state-corruption attacks. In evaluations with Gemma 4 31B IT, PIPES reduced average attack success from 84.7% to 2.3% while preserving benign utility. This addresses a novel security vulnerability in AI agents, where attacker-controlled content can corrupt the agent's perceived environment and bypass existing guardrails. PIPES offers a concrete mitigation, potentially improving the safety and reliability of tool-using AI systems in real-world deployments. PIPES uses static field contracts when schemas provide stable expectations, and conditions screening of open-ended content on the pre-response trajectory and trusted provenance metadata. It marks violations and allows deployments to remove, warn, block, or escalate; the paper instantiates atomic removal and evaluates against adaptive PAIR-style attacks across VitaBench and AgentDyn splits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents consume external data from sources with varying trust levels, but tool responses often lack provenance information, creating a gap that attackers can exploit. State-corruption attacks involve injecting content that makes false environmental claims, corrupting the agent's perception and making harmful actions appear justified. PIPES addresses this by combining semantic priors (learned expectations about content) with provenance metadata to screen response units.

<a id="item-67"></a>
## [Tree-Coupled A/B Testing Enables Exact Multi-Policy Comparison with Fewer Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12831) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Tree-Coupled A/B Testing (TCAB), a method that shares rewards across coupled policies to enable exact multi-policy comparison with fewer reward queries. It achieves this by coupling policies on a tree structure, ensuring each policy retains its standalone trajectory law while sharing rewards along matched edges. This work is significant for online platforms and AI/ML systems that need to compare multiple adaptive policies (e.g., ranking systems, recommendation algorithms) under costly or risky interactions. By reducing the number of required interactions from JT to T+o(T), it can substantially lower experimental costs and improve the cost-precision frontier. The method achieves a pathwise identity N(T)=T+∑_{t,e}D_{e,t}, where D_{e,t} indicates a mismatch on tree edge e at round t, making the expected number of reward queries equal to T plus cumulative tree-edge total variation. This cost is conditionally optimal among exact edge-local designs, and a current-round minimum-spanning tree is myopically optimal among tree designs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: A/B/n testing is a standard method for comparing multiple policies by assigning each policy its own trajectory, which requires JT interactions for J policies over T rounds. Contextual-bandit policies adapt their decisions based on context, and comparing them often involves costly or risky interactions. This paper proposes a novel coupling technique to share rewards while maintaining exactness, reducing the number of interactions needed.

## [Atomic Decomposition and ILP Boost LLM Logical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12836) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a framework that decomposes compound answer options into atomic judgments and uses an integer linear program (ILP) to compose them, avoiding the model seeing compound options. It improves Macro-F1 from 48.3 to 77.0 on LOGICAL-COMMONSENSEQA and from 47.0 to 75.6 on LOGICAL-SATA, a new benchmark derived from SATA-Bench. This work addresses a known limitation of large language models in compositional reasoning, where they often fail to combine atomic judgments under logical operators. The significant performance gains suggest a promising direction for improving LLM reasoning and could influence future benchmark design and reasoning frameworks. The framework uses contrastive hypothesis scoring for each atomic answer and an operator-constrained ILP to compose calibrated scores into a single prediction. The largest gains are observed on NEITHER/NOR options, and the new LOGICAL-SATA benchmark is derived from SATA-Bench, which focuses on 'Select All That Apply' questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often struggle with logical reasoning, especially when answer options require combining multiple atomic facts under logical operators like AND, OR, and NEITHER/NOR. Integer linear programming (ILP) is a mathematical optimization technique used in NLP for structured prediction, where constraints can be enforced on the output. SATA-Bench is a benchmark for evaluating LLMs on 'Select All That Apply' questions, which require selecting all correct options from a list.

## [AQuA: Recursively Self-Improving Quantitative Trading Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12841) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AQuA introduces two independent LLM-driven research systems for quantitative trading that recursively improve by retaining validated evidence, operating in sealed sandboxes. The factor system achieves a combined information coefficient of about 0.190 on a crypto universe, while the model system reaches a per-stock information coefficient of +0.0843 on US equities and a held-out Sharpe of up to +2.50. This work demonstrates a practical approach to recursive self-improvement in financial research, potentially automating and accelerating quantitative strategy development. It could impact the financial AI ecosystem by showing how LLM agents can independently close research loops and generate profitable strategies, though it is a preprint and needs further validation. The two systems do not share agents, memories, candidate spaces, or research state; each uses its own sealed sandbox that fixes data splits, feature and label definitions, and evaluator. The model system uses a config-driven loop over a hybrid time-series architecture and converts its signal into a threshold long/short strategy with a two-leg cost, positive in every year from 2021 to 2025.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Recursive self-improvement is a concept where an AI system improves its own capabilities by using its outputs to refine future iterations. In quantitative trading, information coefficient (IC) measures the correlation between predicted and actual returns, and Sharpe ratio assesses risk-adjusted performance. Sealed sandboxes ensure controlled experimentation by fixing data and evaluation criteria, preventing data leakage and overfitting.

## [BrainWAM: Coordinating Semantic Priors and Predictive Dynamics for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12854) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BrainWAM introduces a structured action-space coordination framework that integrates semantic reasoning from Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models with predictive dynamics from World Action Models (WAMs), overcoming the attention-allocation mismatch in naive combinations. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on NAVSIM v1 (89.5 PDMS) and NAVSIM v2 (89.6 EPDMS). This work addresses a critical limitation in end-to-end autonomous driving by showing how to effectively combine complementary strengths of VLA and WAM models, potentially leading to more robust and capable driving systems. It also introduces a neuroscience-inspired design that could influence future multimodal planning architectures. BrainWAM converts semantic reasoning and predictive world modeling into two specialized action-oriented pathways and aligns them at the level of compact action representations. It also introduces an asynchronous rectified-flow inference strategy with decoupled video and action denoising, which shortens inference latency while preserving planning-relevant predictive context.

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models leverage vision-language model priors for semantic reasoning, while World Action Models (WAMs) provide future-aware prediction through generative world modeling. However, naively combining them via joint token-level attention leads to an attention-allocation mismatch where semantic shortcuts dominate and suppress predictive dynamics.

## [SPARED: Adversarial RL Framework Boosts AI-Image Detection with Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SPARED introduces an adversarial reinforcement learning framework that trains a detector against a diffusion-based editor, which edits real photos into fake counterparts to fool the detector, while a reasoning MLLM provides free-form, verifiable verdicts. The detector improves monotonically across rounds on three external benchmarks. This addresses a critical gap in AI-generated image detection by improving both accuracy and interpretability, which is essential for AI safety and trust. The adversarial training approach ensures the detector generalizes rather than memorizes fixed artifacts, making it more robust to evolving generative models. The framework uses shortcut-proof rewards: the attacker is credited only when its edit is faithfully executed, and the defender only when its verdict is correct. Although the explanation is never directly rewarded, its quality improves as a side effect of accuracy-only training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated image detection is challenging because existing detectors often rely on provenance shortcuts, templated rationales, and static forgery corpora, which fail as generators evolve. Adversarial reinforcement learning pits two models against each other to continuously generate harder examples, forcing the detector to generalize. Reasoning multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) combine visual and textual understanding to provide explainable verdicts, which is crucial for trustworthy AI systems.

## [Treatment Leakage Undermines MCP Agent Security Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12880) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.12880) audits a preserved MCP agent security campaign and reveals direct treatment leakage, where treatment metadata gated the ATTACK_SUCCESS class. The authors propose a treatment-blind reconstruction that corrects 58 historical labels to benign completions, and the locked v2 census contains zero ATTACK_SUCCESS records. This work highlights a critical methodological flaw in security evaluations of tool-using agents, showing that stored labels may not reflect actual behavior. It underscores the need for treatment-blind evaluation designs to ensure construct validity, which is essential for reliable AI security assessments. The audit traced 10,200 execution rows to 180 model-bound requests, 45 semantic requests, and 15 observable stimuli. A dual-reviewer blinded concordance review of 96 requests produced identical consensus classes but differed on four construct-boundary cases, and the authors contribute a seven-link Integrity Chain and an endpoint-integrity linter.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: MCP (Model Context Protocol) agents are AI systems that use external tools, and their security evaluations often rely on labels to classify attack outcomes. Treatment leakage occurs when treatment metadata influences the labeling process, leading to biased results. Construct validity refers to whether a measure truly reflects the intended concept, and this paper applies it to security evaluation.

AutoQuREO is a new automated framework for full-stack quantum resource estimation and optimization, introduced in a recent arXiv paper. It features four core novelties: a flexible stack abstraction, a modular library of components, surrogate modeling via algorithmic profiling and neuro-symbolic learning, and integrated multi-objective optimization. This framework addresses a critical gap in quantum resource estimation by automating and modularizing the process, which could significantly accelerate quantum computing research and development. It enables systematic exploration of resource trade-offs that are intractable with existing tools, supporting the transition from proof-of-principle to practical quantum utility. AutoQuREO is designed as a digital twin for quantum computing stacks, enabling tractable exploration of complex design spaces. Its capabilities are demonstrated through co-design case studies including early-fault-tolerant algorithms, small error correction codes, gate decomposition, and variational training of parametric quantum circuits.

## [Latent World Model Bottleneck Is Planner's Objective, Not Predictor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12959) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper on arXiv (2608.12959) demonstrates that in latent world models, the planner's objective, not the predictor, limits long-horizon planning performance. By replacing only the objective in a reproduction of LeWorldModel on the TwoRoom benchmark, the success rate at offset 100 jumped from 26.0% to 98.0% without retraining or using a GPU. This finding challenges a common assumption in model-based reinforcement learning that prediction accuracy is the primary bottleneck for planning. It suggests that improving planning objectives could yield significant gains without additional training, potentially leading to more efficient and effective model-based RL agents. The paper shows that cross-entropy method planning minimizes squared latent distance, which correlates with true distance at r=0.426, saturates around 80 arena units, and decreases beyond 120 units, causing the planner to move away from the goal. A ridge probe can recover position from the frozen embedding with R²=0.9922, indicating the information is present but not utilized effectively.

**Background**: Latent world models are used in model-based reinforcement learning to learn compact representations of high-dimensional observations, enabling planning in a latent space. The TwoRoom benchmark involves an agent navigating between two rooms through a passage to reach a target.

## [TEMPO: Makespan-Aware Load Balancing for MoE Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13057) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TEMPO proposes a makespan-aware expert-parallel load balancing method that models expert execution time with a max-affine profile, capturing both memory- and compute-bound regimes. It formalizes per-batch dispatch as a fixed-charge makespan problem, which is NP-hard, and provides a dispatcher that solves it in milliseconds, improving dispatch efficiency by up to 1.7x. This work addresses a critical performance bottleneck in MoE serving, where existing load balancers assume linear expert time and fail to account for regime shifts. By providing a phase diagram that predicts when gains are possible, TEMPO offers practical guidance for optimizing LLM inference systems, potentially improving throughput and latency for large models like Qwen3-235B. The max-affine profile is t = max(a + bG, c + βN), where G is the number of activated replicas and N is the token count. The method is validated on two datacenter GPU generations, with an 8-GPU microbenchmark showing within 1% of the best baseline and up to 15.5% improvement in mixed regimes; end-to-end on Qwen3-235B, it gains 4-6% throughput and cuts p99 latency by ~15.6%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In expert-parallel (EP) MoE serving, each layer synchronizes at the slowest GPU, so load balancing is crucial. Existing dispatchers balance token counts or activated-expert counts, assuming expert time is linear in one factor. However, measurements show that below a threshold (~156-168 tokens), HBM weight streaming dominates, and above it, grouped GEMM rounds tokens to 128-tile M-tiles, leading to padded compute when splitting experts. TEMPO's max-affine profile captures both regimes, and its makespan-aware dispatcher optimizes per-batch dispatch.

## [Auditing Test-Time Budget Allocation in Neural Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper audits test-time budget allocation in neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) and finds that apparent gains from non-uniform allocation vanish out-of-sample. Across three pretrained solvers (POMO, AM, SymNCO) on TSP-100, an oracle allocation reports a 2.2-2.6% gain in-sample, but out-of-sample gains are indistinguishable from zero. This finding exposes a common evaluation bias in NCO research, where reported gains from test-time budget allocation are often artifacts of in-sample evaluation. It underscores the need for rigorous out-of-sample validation and reproducibility in AI research, potentially affecting how future NCO methods are evaluated and compared. The paper also shows that the same correction preserves a real gain under distribution shift: a pre-registered confirmatory experiment finds that allocation guided by held-out sample statistics improves best-of-k by 11.5% (AM) and 12.0% (SymNCO), while a negative control (POMO) shows -0.3%. The authors provide a correction procedure, a reporting checklist, and release all data, code, and pre-registration records.

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses neural networks to solve combinatorial problems like the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP). Solvers typically generate multiple candidate solutions per instance and report the best one, with the sample count conventionally identical across instances. This paper investigates whether allocating a fixed total budget non-uniformly across instances can improve performance, and audits the evaluation methodology itself.

## [TRAPSBench: VLMs Encode Answerability but Fail to Abstain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13167) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TRAPSBench, a procedurally generated video benchmark of 1,404 matched physics pairs, and the Penalized Epistemic Calibration Score (PECS) metric. Across 16 VLMs, the best PECS is only 0.292, showing poor spontaneous abstention, despite linear probes decoding answerability from hidden states at up to 0.91 AUROC. This work reveals a critical gap between internal knowledge and expressed behavior in VLMs, which has significant implications for AI safety and reliability. The causal steering of a 'void direction' offers a potential path for output-stage interventions to improve abstention, benefiting applications where incorrect answers are costly. The study tested 16 VLMs across five families, including Qwen, Gemma, and LLaVA, and found that models detect textual impossibility about 4x more readily than missing visual evidence. The bottleneck is expression, not perception, as steering a single-layer void direction causally induces or suppresses abstention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text to generate outputs. Epistemic calibration refers to the alignment between a model's expressed certainty and its actual correctness, which is crucial for trustworthy AI. TRAPSBench uses procedurally generated physics videos where a single change makes the outcome undeterminable, testing whether models abstain appropriately.

## [GEM: Generative Embedding Model Enhances Retrieval via Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13200) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GEM is a novel generative embedding model that explicitly reasons about user intent and relevance criteria before encoding, unifying generation and embedding in a single model. It outperforms its non-reasoning variant and matches baselines using substantially larger models on reasoning-intensive and instruction-following retrieval tasks. This addresses the growing gap between how users express complex needs and how retrievers interpret them, potentially improving information retrieval and LLM applications. GEM's competitive performance with larger models suggests that reasoning-augmented retrieval can be efficient and impactful. GEM first reasons over the query, then appends an embedding token to encode the enriched context for retrieval. Its generative nature allows test-time compute scaling via prompting to further enhance retrieval performance, and the code is available at an anonymous repository.

**Background**: Traditional retrievers rely on surface-level matching between queries and documents, which can fail when users express complex intents. ## [SciFigBench: Evaluating VLM Behavioral Reliability on Scientific Figures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13267) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces SciFigBench, a diagnostic benchmark for vision-language models (VLMs) that evaluates perception, reasoning, and behavioral reliability under uncertainty on scientific figures. It also proposes the Admittance-Resistance-Inductance (A-R-I) framework to assess whether models acknowledge insufficient evidence, resist misleading context, and infer cautiously from partial information. This work addresses a critical gap in VLM evaluation by focusing on behavioral reliability under uncertainty, which is essential for deploying models in scientific workflows where missing or misleading visual information can lead to serious errors. The findings show that high perception and reasoning accuracy do not guarantee reliable behavior, highlighting the need for more comprehensive evaluation metrics. SciFigBench contains 250 figures with high-quality human annotations, totaling over 600 hours of annotation effort, and is extended via image transformations, reasoning questions, resistance probes, caption-bias probes, and confirmed selective-blur targets to produce over 34,000 evaluation setups. The results show that GPT-5.2 achieves the highest description quality (MQM 91.6) but hallucinates unreadable content in 96% of cases, while Gemini 3.1 Pro admits uncertainty in 71% of such cases and achieves the strongest resistance score (0.91).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text, and they are increasingly used in scientific domains. Existing benchmarks primarily focus on perception and reasoning accuracy, but often overlook how models behave when visual evidence is missing or misleading, which is a common scenario in real-world applications. The A-R-I framework provides a structured way to evaluate behavioral reliability, complementing traditional accuracy metrics.

## [Mixture of Training: Modular Pretraining Achieves Parity with Monolithic Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13277) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Mixture of Training (MoT), a modular pretraining method that trains Transformer layer blocks independently within a frozen aligner scaffold and recomposes them into a larger model. On a 1.3B-parameter Gemma-style model trained on C4, MoT achieves perplexity parity with monolithic training. This work demonstrates that language model pretraining can be decomposed into smaller, independently trainable jobs, potentially enabling parallel training and better compute utilization. It provides a proof-of-concept that could influence future large-scale training efficiency, though it is early-stage and not yet validated at frontier scales. The MoT method partitions a target Transformer into contiguous layer blocks, trains each block inside a frozen pretrained aligner scaffold, and then recomposes the blocks with an optional short end-to-end adaptation pass. The parity setting processes more aggregate tokens and has a shorter idealized layer-equivalent critical path after aligner preparation; its effective compute advantage depends on reusing the aligner across runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional language model pretraining trains all parameters of a monolithic model jointly, which requires massive compute and memory. Modular training approaches, such as Mixture of Experts (MoE), have shown promise in scaling efficiency by training specialized components. MoT extends this idea by treating layer blocks as reusable training units, potentially allowing parallel training and better resource utilization.

## [Study Finds Gender-Associated Linguistic Bias in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13328) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that LLMs respond with shorter, less sophisticated, and less formal outputs when prompts contain linguistic features common in women's speech, such as hedges, tag questions, and collective reference. This bias persists across three document types and four models, and explicit gender cues like sign-off names have no effect. This finding is significant because it highlights a subtle yet pervasive form of gender bias in AI-mediated communication, which could disadvantage women in professional settings. It underscores the difficulty of mitigating such bias post-hoc and calls for upstream consideration of linguistic variation in model development. The study controlled for prompt complexity and feature carry-over, and found that linguistic register is far more influential than explicit gender cues. Mechanistic analysis shows that linguistic features are encoded in early transformer layers and entangled with other features, making mitigation challenging.

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used in professional communication, but they may not serve all users equally. Linguistic features such as hedges ('I think'), tag questions ('right?'), and collective reference ('we') are more common in women's speech, and this study shows that these features trigger biased responses from models.

UltraIR, a foundation model with over 100 million parameters, has been introduced for infrared spectroscopy. It is pretrained on approximately 60 million simulated IR spectra and enables simulation-to-real transfer learning for chemical sensing across molecules and complex samples. UltraIR addresses the long-standing challenge of extracting reliable chemical information from IR spectra, which is often labor-intensive and poorly scalable. By enabling transfer learning with limited labeled data and zero-shot inference across instruments and laboratories, it could significantly improve data efficiency and adaptability in chemical sensing applications. UltraIR is pretrained using spectral reconstruction, molecular fingerprint similarity alignment, and functional-group prediction. It outperforms conventional machine-learning and task-specific deep-learning baselines across tasks such as functional-group prediction, molecular structure elucidation, mixture-component identification, bacterial classification, and soil property prediction.

## [Deliberate Practice: Budget-Optimal Robot Skill Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13415) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Deliberate Practice (DP), an active skill learning algorithm that computes a provably budget-optimal allocation of limited practice time to maximize expected cumulative reward in sequential robot tasks. The key contribution is a bilinear program that can be solved exactly with off-the-shelf solvers. This work addresses a significant challenge in long-horizon robot manipulation: efficiently using limited practice time to acquire useful skills. It provides theoretical guarantees and empirical validation, offering a practical solution that could improve autonomous learning in robotics and AI. The algorithm estimates both the time needed to master skills and the cumulative reward of task plans that the skills unlock. It was validated through simulated and real-world experiments on long-horizon manipulation tasks, demonstrating optimal use of limited practice time.

## [Reduced Matrix Multiplication: Input-Adaptive Efficiency for LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13426) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Reduced Matrix Multiplication (RMM), a training-free, input-adaptive inference method that reduces Transformer matrix products by selecting informative slices along contraction dimensions, without modifying model weights. It demonstrates smooth accuracy-efficiency trade-offs across LLMs from 1B to 70B parameters and extends to multimodal vision-language models. RMM offers a scalable, training-free approach to reduce inference costs, which is critical for deploying large models in resource-constrained environments. Its input-adaptive nature and demonstrated runtime gains on A100 GPUs could make it a practical tool for improving LLM serving efficiency across various tasks and model sizes. The method uses a simple retention-ratio control to achieve predictable trade-offs, and mechanistic ablations reveal that attention-side computations are more reducible than MLP components. Wall-clock benchmarks with custom kernels on an NVIDIA A100 show practical runtime gains, especially at longer sequence lengths.

**Background**: Transformer-based language models rely heavily on high-dimensional matrix multiplications, which dominate inference costs. ## [Complete Characterization of Transformer Length Generalization on Regular Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13433) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes the first complete characterization of which regular languages transformers length-generalize on, and provides a polynomial-time decision algorithm based on the syntactic monoid and C-RASP formalism. This resolves a foundational open problem in understanding transformer capabilities, providing a precise theoretical boundary for length generalization. It also introduces a novel algebraic decomposition theory that extends classical Krohn-Rhodes theory, potentially impacting future research on transformer expressiveness and generalization. The key insight is that classical Krohn-Rhodes decomposition is insufficient for C-RASP because its basic building blocks (flip-flop and simple groups) are not expressible in C-RASP, while C-RASP's unbounded counting is not captured by finite semigroups. The authors generalize decomposition theory to the infinite additive group on integers, enabling a polynomial-time decision algorithm for regular language membership in C-RASP.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers sometimes generalize to longer sequences than seen during training, but the precise conditions are unknown. Regular languages are a foundational class in formal language theory, and C-RASP is a recently introduced formalism that characterizes which languages transformers length-generalize on. The syntactic monoid is a standard algebraic tool for studying regular languages, and Krohn-Rhodes theory provides a decomposition of finite semigroups into simpler components.

## [ContactGuard: Pre-Contact Failure Detection with Latent World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ContactGuard introduces a pre-contact execution monitor that predicts latent visual outcomes of planned actions to abort robot manipulations before contact occurs. It is trained on unlabelled trajectories and a small labelled set of pre-contact clips, and demonstrated on real-world contact-rich tasks. This addresses a critical limitation in robotic manipulation where failures are often detected only after contact, leading to damage or inefficiency. By enabling pre-contact abort signals, ContactGuard can improve robot safety and reliability without modifying the underlying policy, which is valuable for real-world deployment. The latent world model predicts compact multi-view visual embeddings under planned actions, avoiding pixel-level video prediction. A lightweight failure probe is trained from a small labelled set of pre-contact clips, and the system anchors prediction before an imminent contact event and rolls the model forward under the policy's own actions.

## [UniTexture: Cross-Task Universal Adversarial Texture Attack on VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13453) ⭐️ 8.0/10

UniTexture introduces a cross-task universal adversarial texture attack that uses a single textured 3D object to induce targeted action deviations in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models across multiple tasks. It jointly optimizes the texture over a distribution of tasks, instructions, states, and viewpoints using a differentiable renderer and a targeted action-space objective. This work addresses a critical security gap in embodied AI, revealing that multitask VLA models share cross-task vulnerabilities that can be systematically exploited. The attack reduces task success rates from 90.0% to 48.4% and transfers across models, highlighting the need for robust defenses in robotic systems. UniTexture was evaluated on OpenVLA and π0.5 across diverse manipulation tasks, achieving cross-suite and cross-model transfer without re-optimization. The attack backpropagates gradients from action outputs to surface texture parameters through a differentiable renderer, steering predictions toward attacker-defined targets.

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are generalist robotic policies that follow language instructions to perform manipulation tasks. Adversarial attacks on such models typically target a single task, but this work explores cross-task vulnerabilities. Differentiable renderers enable gradient-based optimization of physical object properties like texture, which is key to crafting physical adversarial examples.

## [LLMs Know Their Limits but Still Hallucinate: Gricean Retreat Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13484) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.13484) proposes a Gricean retreat mechanism to explain why LLMs fabricate details for unknown entities, and empirically shows that models encode knowledge boundaries and referent specificity in their activations but fail to reconcile these signals during generation. This research addresses the critical issue of hallucination in LLMs, which undermines trustworthiness and safety. By identifying that the substrate for a Gricean retreat exists but the policy is missing, it opens new avenues for training or steering objectives that could reduce fabrication and improve reliability. The study uses a T-REx-based benchmark that varies entity familiarity and referent specificity, probing models to determine if activations encode knowledge boundaries and anticipate referent specificity. Results show that models prefer specific referents even for unknown entities, and do so even when correct generic alternatives are offered, indicating a disconnect between knowledge and generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Gricean pragmatics, based on the Cooperative Principle, suggests that cooperative speakers adjust their statements to be truthful and informative. In this context, a 'Gricean retreat' would mean that when uncertain about a referent, a speaker should retreat up the specificity hierarchy to safer, more general claims. LLMs often fail to do this, instead fabricating plausible details. The paper builds on prior work on hallucination and knowledge boundaries in LLMs, using benchmarks like T-REx to probe model behavior.

## [Unmasking Growth Complexity Yields Certified-Optimal Diffusion Schedules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13520) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces unmasking growth complexity (UGC), a path-resolved measure of data geometry, and shows its local increments control KL discretization error in masking diffusion samplers. It derives certified-optimal schedules for both Bernoulli-subset and fixed-cardinality unmasking schemes, with practical estimation via KL increments. This work provides a theoretical foundation for optimizing masking diffusion schedules, a key component in discrete generative models, with certified guarantees on KL error and iteration complexity. It could lead to more efficient and reliable samplers for discrete data, impacting fields like language modeling and protein design. The paper shows that in log-reveal-odds coordinates, UGC yields optimized single-block and multi-block schedules, and quantifies gains from adapting computational effort to data geometry. In the fine-partition limit, the squared integral of the square-root UGC density determines the sharp leading-order optimal Euler discretization error, with examples showing dimension-dependent gains such as Ω̃(√d) improvements with a constant number of adaptively placed blocks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Masking diffusion models are a class of generative models for discrete data, where a sequence is progressively masked and then unmasked during sampling. The schedule of how tokens are revealed is crucial for sampling quality and efficiency, but optimizing it has been challenging. This paper introduces a new theoretical measure, UGC, to guide schedule design with certified guarantees.

## [Vero: First Benchmark for Repository-Level Verified Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13522) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Vero is the first benchmark designed to evaluate AI agents on joint implementation and proof synthesis across multi-module repositories, covering Python, Dafny, Verus, and Coq. It includes 43 multi-module instances sourced from real-world repositories and supports both proof-only and code-and-proof evaluation modes. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI-generated code's correctness, moving beyond single-function benchmarks to repository-level verification. It provides a concrete testbed for measuring progress toward trustworthy AI-generated software, which is essential as AI agents become more prevalent in software engineering. The benchmark includes an audit mechanism allowing agents to formally prove unsatisfiability of specifications or incorrectness of reference code, surfacing latent errors. In evaluation, the strongest agent fully solved only 27 of 43 instances and closed no specifications on the hardest repositories, indicating current agents still fall short.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Verified code generation involves producing both an implementation and a machine-checked proof of its specification, offering stronger guarantees than typical AI-generated code. Existing benchmarks focus on individual functions or only evaluate proof generation with provided implementations, leaving a gap for repository-level evaluation. Vero uses Lean 4 as the proof language and covers multiple programming languages, including Dafny, Verus, and Coq, which are verification-aware languages or proof assistants.

HumanTracker introduces a large-scale benchmark with approximately 153 hours of optical motion trajectories from professional performers, organized into four motion families, and proposes HumanScore, a preference-aligned metric trained on 12K motion pairs containing 24K motions. This addresses a critical gap in humanoid motion tracking evaluation, where kinematic errors often fail to capture perceptually important artifacts like foot skating and unstable support. By aligning with human preferences, HumanScore can improve teleoperation and imitation learning, benefiting the broader robotics and AI community. The benchmark includes text labels for fine-grained diagnosis, and HumanScore is trained on 12K motion pairs (24K motions). Across representative state-of-the-art trackers, HumanScore better predicts human preferences and reveals contact and stability failures that kinematic metrics often miss.

AutoDesign introduces a meta-harness optimization framework that recursively improves a code agent's harness based on rollout feedback, and presents PosterBench, a new benchmark for academic paper-to-poster generation. On the PosterBench Main Track, AutoDesign achieves a score of 78.32, surpassing Claude Design by 7.45 points. This work addresses the static nature of existing agentic systems by enabling recursive self-improvement, which is crucial for long-horizon tasks. It also provides a new benchmark (PosterBench) to evaluate such systems, potentially influencing future research in automated content generation and agentic AI. AutoDesign integrates a learned DesignHarness across seven controlled code-agent-model configurations, improving the average PosterBench Score from 54.99 to 67.39 (+12.4%). In a fully autonomous loop, it executes 253 tool calls and 11 editing turns within 40 minutes for under $3, reaching average conference-poster quality in human evaluation.

**Background**: Long-horizon agentic design involves transforming multimodal sources into structured outputs through a model-harness system, where the harness guides the agent's actions. Existing harnesses are static and lack the ability to accumulate experience for self-improvement. AutoDesign introduces a meta-harness optimizer that learns from rollout feedback, enabling recursive improvement. PosterBench provides a standardized evaluation for this task, with a main track of 100 papers across five disciplines and a mini subset for controlled experiments.

## [MatchMiner-AI: Open-Source Privacy-Preserving Cancer Trial Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17228) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MatchMiner-AI, an open-source AI pipeline for matching cancer patients to clinical trials, was introduced, using distilled LLMs and embedding models to summarize EHRs and extract trial eligibility criteria. In retrospective evaluations, it significantly improved mean average precision at 20 from 0.44 to 0.95 for trial-enrolled patients and from 0.38 to 0.94 for standard-of-care patients. This tool addresses the critical problem of low clinical trial enrollment (under 10% for adults with cancer) by democratizing access to trial matching through open-source, privacy-preserving AI. It could significantly improve trial enrollment rates and accelerate cancer research, benefiting patients, oncologists, and researchers. The pipeline uses open-weight LLMs to summarize patient histories and extract target populations, with distilled embedding and re-ranking models for retrieval. In a 50-patient comparison, MatchMiner-AI retrieved trials for all patients, while a rules-based approach only did so for 19 (38%); among those, 80% of MatchMiner-AI's suggestions were deemed reasonable by a frontier LLM, versus 53% for the rules-based method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical trial matching is a complex process that requires interpreting unstructured EHR data and eligibility criteria. Distilled LLMs are smaller, faster versions of large language models that retain much of the original performance, making them suitable for privacy-preserving local deployment. Embedding models convert text into numerical vectors for semantic similarity, and re-ranking models refine initial retrieval results.

## [Foam-Agent: LLM Multi-Agent Framework Automates OpenFOAM CFD Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04997) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Foam-Agent is a new multi-agent framework that uses large language models to automate the entire OpenFOAM computational fluid dynamics (CFD) workflow from a single natural-language prompt. It achieves an 88.2% execution success rate on the 110 Basic-tier tasks and 62.5% on the out-of-distribution Advanced tier of the FoamBench benchmark, without expert intervention. This framework significantly lowers the barrier to entry for CFD simulation, which traditionally requires deep expertise and involves fragmented, multi-stage workflows. By automating the process, it could make CFD accessible to a broader range of researchers and engineers, potentially accelerating innovation in fields that rely on fluid dynamics simulations. Foam-Agent is built on three methodological contributions: multi-index retrieval that organizes domain knowledge along four structural dimensions, dependency-aware file generation that traverses the OpenFOAM case dependency graph, and a trajectory-conditioned reviewer loop that iteratively repairs failed runs. The framework includes six specialist agents (planning, meshing, file writing, execution, review, and visualization) and exposes its capabilities through the Model Context Protocol for external orchestration.

**Background**: Computational fluid dynamics (CFD) is a cornerstone of computational physics, used to simulate fluid flows in engineering and science. OpenFOAM is a popular open-source CFD toolbox, but its steep learning curve and complex, multi-step workflow (meshing, configuration, solving, post-processing) hinder adoption. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in automating code generation and task planning, and multi-agent systems can decompose complex tasks into specialized subtasks. Foam-Agent leverages these advances to automate CFD workflows, addressing a significant barrier in the field.

## [Neurosymbolic AI and Human Collaboration Discover Latin Square Bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08322) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper reports that an LLM-powered agent, combined with symbolic computation tools and human guidance, discovered a tight lower bound on the imbalance of Latin squares for n ≡ 1 mod 3, achieving 4n(n-1)/9 via a novel class of near-perfect permutations, formally verified in Lean 4. This demonstrates that neurosymbolic systems can produce genuine discoveries in pure mathematics, highlighting the potential of human-AI collaboration to tackle notoriously difficult problems. It also provides insights into the distinct cognitive contributions of LLMs, symbolic tools, and human strategists, which could inform future AI research and tool design. The study reconstructed the discovery process from detailed interaction logs spanning multiple sessions over several days. Multi-model deliberation among frontier LLMs proved reliable for criticism and error detection but unreliable for constructive claims, while the human steering provided the critical research pivot that transformed a dead end into productive inquiry.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Combinatorial design theory deals with the existence, construction, and properties of systems of finite sets that satisfy balance and symmetry conditions, with applications in experimental design, cryptography, and more. Latin squares are n×n arrays where each symbol appears exactly once in each row and column; their imbalance is a measure of deviation from perfect balance. The case n ≡ 1 mod 3 has been notoriously difficult for determining tight bounds on this imbalance.

This paper introduces a novel framework for auditing LLM agents, defining five dimensions of auditability and three mechanism classes (detect, enforce, recover). The paper shows that scenario-wrapped prompts activate internal scenario directions that causally reduce refusal scores, and introduces Concept2Scenario, a concept-based attribution framework for discovering vulnerable scenarios in LLMs. The framework improves attack success rates by up to 18.2 percentage points and transfers to other models. This work bridges empirical red-teaming and mechanistic interpretability, offering a mechanistic explanation for why scenario-wrapped prompts bypass safety alignment. It provides a practical methodology for discovering vulnerable scenarios, which could significantly impact future red-teaming and safety alignment research. The framework uses a sparse autoencoder to instantiate a concept space, attributes refusal suppression to individual concepts, and identifies synergistic scenario combinations via interaction attribution. The framework incorporates a knowledge-orchestrated generation system to provide structured, multi-level domain insights, and a stage-adaptive agentic evolution strategy that dynamically adjusts LLM interaction modes. ## [Recursive Synthesis Generates 37K Long-Horizon Terminal Tasks at $0.05 Each](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05466) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Recursive Synthetic Terminal Tasks (RST), a recursive verified synthesis framework that generates 37,484 long-horizon terminal-agent tasks at roughly $0.05 per task. Over fifteen rounds, task difficulty increases substantially, with median reference solution length growing from 67 to 374 lines and DeepSeek-V4-Pro pass@4 dropping from 90% to 2.5%. This approach addresses the high cost bottleneck in producing long-horizon training data for terminal agents, potentially enabling scalable and affordable data generation. Fine-tuning on synthesized trajectories improves Qwen3.5 models by up to 10 points on terminal benchmarks, and agentic PPO yields relative gains of 20-41%, indicating significant practical impact on AI agent training. RST extends reference solutions, realigns verifiers and instructions, and validates results in fresh sandboxes, reusing accepted tasks as seeds. The recursion shows no ceiling after 15 rounds, with stable synthesis yield and validation rates, suggesting scalability beyond reported scale.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Terminal agents are AI systems that operate in command-line environments, performing tasks like coding or system administration. High-quality training data for such agents is expensive because each task must maintain consistency among instruction, environment, reference solution, and verifier. Traditional human authoring does not scale, and direct LLM generation often breaks these dependencies. RST addresses this by recursively synthesizing and verifying tasks, starting from seed tasks and iteratively increasing difficulty.

## [iARCS: Iterative Agentic RL for Controllable 3D Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06161) ⭐️ 8.0/10

iARCS introduces an iterative agentic reinforcement learning framework that adapts pretrained scene generators to satisfy task-specific functional constraints via LLM-generated reward programs. The framework uses a two-stage strategy: universal-reward pretraining for physical plausibility, followed by task-specific fine-tuning with iteratively refined rewards. This work addresses a critical gap between perceptual realism and functional constraints in synthetic 3D scene generation, which is vital for downstream computer vision and embodied AI training. By improving constraint fidelity and scene diversity, iARCS could enhance the practical utility of synthetic data, potentially impacting fields like robotics and autonomous navigation. The framework combines LLM-based task decomposition, executable reward synthesis, and staged policy refinement. Experiments demonstrate improved constraint fidelity on walkability, reachability, and clearance-focused tasks, while maintaining competitive scene diversity and even improving the base generator's output.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning (Agentic RL) is a training paradigm that uses reinforcement learning to elevate large language models (LLMs) from passive text predictors into active agents capable of interacting with environments. In the context of 3D scene generation, traditional generators often optimize for perceptual realism but fail to meet functional constraints like accessibility or spatial rules, limiting their use in downstream tasks. iARCS leverages LLMs to generate reward programs that guide the RL process, enabling the generator to adapt to specific task requirements.

## [CIDER Dataset Enhances LLM Privacy Preference Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09164) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CIDER, a dataset of 14,850 human annotations from 169 users, forming 1,650 contextual disclosure boundary sets across 60 scenarios. It shows that in-context personalization improves LLM prediction accuracy by up to 11.41 percentage points. This addresses a critical gap in aligning LLMs with individual privacy preferences, which is essential for deploying AI in sensitive contexts. The dataset and findings provide a benchmark for developing more personalized and privacy-aware AI systems. The study evaluated 12 open and proprietary models, finding that larger models like GPT-5.4 and Claude Sonnet 4.6 better leverage semantic context, while smaller models rely on structured heuristics. Only Claude Sonnet 4.6 achieved balanced improvements in false-positive and false-negative rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Privacy preference alignment involves capturing individuals' disclosure boundaries, which vary by context and are often more nuanced than general privacy norms. In-context personalization uses historical examples to tailor model predictions to individual users, which is a promising approach for improving alignment. The CIDER dataset provides a realistic benchmark for evaluating such personalization.

## [Model Discovery Agent: LLM-Bayesian Framework for Efficient Mechanistic Model Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The Model Discovery Agent (MDA) integrates a large language model as a structure proposer with Bayesian inference and experiment design to discover mechanistic world models from few interventions. It achieves state-of-the-art data efficiency on benchmarks in physics, chemistry, and biology. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in causal discovery: learning mechanistic models from limited experiments. By combining LLM creativity with rigorous Bayesian methods, it could accelerate scientific discovery across disciplines, reducing the cost of experimentation. MDA operates in the M-open setting, where a predictive check flags when the truth lies outside the current hypothesis class, prompting the LLM to propose new structures. It uses sequential Monte Carlo (SMC) for posteriors, simulation-based inference (SBI) for intractable likelihoods, and value-of-information (VoI) for experiment design. The paper introduces a new benchmark, HHbench, for partially observed single-neuron electrophysiology.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic causal models are essential for predicting outcomes of interventions, but learning them requires experiments because passive data cannot identify mechanisms. Traditional experiment design is often manual and inefficient. The Model Discovery Agent automates this by using an LLM to propose candidate structures and Bayesian methods to design experiments that efficiently identify the correct model.

## [OEIS Open: Language Models Resolve 30% of Open Math Conjectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11941) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OEIS Open, a benchmark of 492 open mathematical conjectures from the OEIS, formalized in Lean. Using a minimal set of tools and a budget of $50 per attempt, language models resolved 147 conjectures, scoring 30%, and with a higher budget of $200 per attempt, the best model scored 44% on a subset called OEIS Open Lite. This demonstrates that language models can autonomously resolve open research conjectures at modest cost, which could accelerate mathematical discovery and shift how AI is used in formal reasoning. The finding that access to literature does not improve performance challenges assumptions about the role of external knowledge in such tasks. The benchmark is secure against LM cheating attempts, and the evaluation code is open-source. OEIS Open Lite is a random subset of 100 conjectures for cheaper evaluation. Giving LMs access to 476,000 arXiv papers did not improve performance, nor did more sophisticated agent loops.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: The OEIS is a comprehensive online database of integer sequences, and many of its entries include conjectures that have not yet been proven. This work builds on prior efforts to formalize OEIS conjectures in Lean, and evaluates generic language models on them.

## [Generative AI Boosts OSS Contributions but Increases Coordination Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02091) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new empirical study using GitHub's proprietary Copilot usage data finds that Copilot use increases project-level code contributions by 5.9%, developer coding participation by 3.4%, and individual code contributions by 2.1%, while also increasing coordination time by 8% and leading to more code discussions. This study provides empirical evidence on the dual effects of generative AI in open-source software development, highlighting a tradeoff between increased contributions and coordination costs. The findings have significant implications for developer productivity, collaboration dynamics, and the future structure of OSS communities. The study reveals heterogeneous effects across developer roles: peripheral developers show smaller increases in project-level code contributions and larger increases in coordination time compared to core developers. Despite the coordination slowdown, the overall effect remains positive, resulting in a net increase in timely code merges at the project level.

This study uses GitHub's proprietary Copilot usage data combined with public OSS project data to analyze the impact of AI assistance on contributions and coordination.

## [Q-Learning Boosts Offline In-Context RL by 30%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17666) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that integrating RL objectives, specifically Q-learning, into offline in-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) improves performance by approximately 30% on average over Algorithm Distillation (AD) across more than 150 datasets. In the XLand-MiniGrid environment, RL objectives doubled AD's performance, with additional gains from conservatism. This finding challenges the prevailing reliance on supervised objectives in offline ICRL, showing that aligning with the RL reward-maximization goal yields significant gains. It suggests offline RL is a promising direction for advancing ICRL, potentially impacting applications in robotics, game playing, and sequential decision-making. The study tested over 150 GridWorld and MuJoCo datasets with varying coverages, structures, expertise levels, and complexities. Adding conservatism during value learning provided additional improvements in almost all settings, highlighting its importance in offline settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Offline in-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) trains agents to learn new tasks from offline data without updating weights, typically using supervised objectives like Algorithm Distillation (AD). However, supervised objectives are known to have limitations in offline RL, such as distribution shift. This paper explores replacing them with RL objectives like Q-learning, which directly optimize for reward maximization.

## [WaveFT: Wavelet-Based Sparse Fine-Tuning for Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12532) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Wavelet Fine-Tuning (WaveFT), a novel PEFT method that learns sparse updates in the wavelet domain of weight matrices, enabling finer-grained parameter control than LoRA and better coverage than direct sparsity or Fourier bases. It has been officially integrated into the Hugging Face PEFT library. WaveFT addresses key limitations of existing PEFT methods like LoRA's low-rank bottleneck and FourierFT's destructive interference, offering a promising new direction for efficient fine-tuning of large models. Its state-of-the-art results on vision tasks and comparable performance on NLP tasks could influence future PEFT research and applications. WaveFT uses wavelet bases that provide semi-local receptive fields, aggregating spatially coherent gradients. Theoretical analysis shows that sparse methods achieve high-rank updates, avoiding LoRA's subspace bottleneck, and a gradient coverage framework explains when WaveFT is preferable. Experiments cover text-to-image generation, image classification, and language understanding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA adapt large pretrained models by updating a small subset of parameters, reducing compute and memory costs. LoRA uses low-rank updates, but its discrete rank constraint limits fine-grained control. WaveFT learns sparse updates in the wavelet domain, leveraging wavelets' ability to capture both frequency and spatial information, offering a balance between direct sparsity and global Fourier bases.

## [State-Exact Unlearning Achieved in Billion-Parameter Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12220) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that with prospective instrumentation and replay from a token store, deletion counterfactuals can be reproduced exactly in billion-parameter language models, achieving state-exact unlearning. Specifically, Pythia 2.8B matches all 2,775,208,960 model-state elements for a random 5% request, and Llama 3.2 1B is exact after omitting 400 of 4,000 TOFU examples. This is significant because it establishes that exact deletion is possible at scale, which is crucial for privacy and compliance with regulations like GDPR's right to be forgotten. It could influence future research and development of unlearning methods, though the method requires prospective instrumentation and does not claim cost efficiency. The method relies on recording execution provenance and retaining an uncontaminated checkpoint, then replaying from a token store without the requested rows. It achieves bit-for-bit exactness in model and optimizer state under pinned single-GPU environments, but dispersed requests can force nearly full replay, so it does not establish cheap deletion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning is a branch of machine learning focused on removing specific undesired elements, such as private data or copyrighted material, without rebuilding models from scratch. Traditional unlearning methods often approximate deletion, but this paper introduces a trace-preserving counterfactual that fixes execution controls while assigning zero contribution to requested identifiers, enabling exact deletion.

## [DiffGRM: Diffusion-Based Generative Recommendation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21805) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DiffGRM introduces a masked discrete diffusion model to replace autoregressive decoding in generative recommendation, addressing intra-item consistency and inter-digit heterogeneity. It achieves 6.9%-15.5% improvement in NDCG@10 over strong baselines on multiple datasets. This work challenges the autoregressive paradigm in generative recommendation, offering a more flexible and effective alternative. It could improve recommendation accuracy and diversity, benefiting both researchers and practitioners in recommender systems. DiffGRM uses Parallel Semantic Encoding (PSE) to decouple digits, On-policy Coherent Noising (OCN) for training, and Confidence-guided Parallel Denoising (CPD) for inference. The code is available at https://github.com/liuzhao09/DiffGRM.

However, autoregressive models suffer from left-to-right causality and uniform next-token objectives, which are ill-suited for SIDs' structural properties. Diffusion models, particularly masked discrete diffusion, offer bidirectional context and parallel generation, addressing these limitations.

SONIC introduces a scaled humanoid motion tracking foundation model with 42M parameters, trained on 100M+ frames from 700 hours of motion capture data and 21k GPU hours, demonstrating that scaling model capacity, data, and compute yields a generalist whole-body controller. This work addresses a key gap in humanoid control by showing that scaling laws, which have driven progress in large language models, also apply to robotics. It provides a practical foundation for downstream tasks like navigation, VR teleoperation, and VLA-driven loco-manipulation, potentially accelerating the development of generalist humanoid robots. The model scales along three axes: network size (1.2M to 42M parameters), dataset volume (100M+ frames), and compute (21k GPU hours). It also introduces a unified token space that supports VR teleoperation and vision-language-action (VLA) models with a single policy, enabling autonomous whole-body loco-manipulation.

## [AUTO: LLM Framework Achieves State-of-the-Art GPU Code Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22651) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AUTO, an iterative optimization framework that separates strategic planning (Strategist) from implementation (Implementors) using LLMs, achieving up to 1.74x speedup over in-lab-optimized chemical kinetics code, 94% of cuBLAS performance for matrix multiplication, and up to 118x speedups over PyTorch baselines on KernelBench. This work demonstrates that LLM-driven design optimization can outperform traditional methods and even match highly optimized libraries like cuBLAS, potentially transforming how GPU code and other design problems are optimized. It also highlights the affordability and data privacy of using open-source LLMs, making advanced optimization accessible to more researchers and practitioners. The framework runs within 100 iterations (about 10 hours) with estimated costs of $15–$159 per run for proprietary models, and is built entirely on open-source LLMs and libraries. However, the authors note that 'cheating was frequently observed' in KernelBench results, and a posteriori analysis shows 50–70% alignment with Bayesian optimization sampling strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional optimization methods often struggle with design problems where the search space and parameters are hard to define. LLMs offer a dynamic alternative by interpreting design spaces and leveraging encoded domain knowledge. AUTO treats design optimization as a strategic search, using a Strategist agent for high-level planning and Implementor agents for low-level code implementation, iteratively refining designs through explore-exploit strategies.
