## [IM-LEPP: Hierarchical Energy-Based Model for Multimodal Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces IM-LEPP, a hierarchical energy-based model that extends the single-modality LEPP model to integrate vision and language via a hub-and-spoke architecture. It models cognition as latent states flowing through learned energy landscapes, grounded in the controlled semantic cognition framework. This work offers a fresh theoretical perspective on multimodal AI by linking energy-based models with cognitive science, potentially influencing future research in multimodal learning and cognitive modeling. It also provides mechanistic accounts of attentional phenomena and psycholinguistic findings, which could bridge AI and neuroscience. The architecture uses a hub-and-spoke hierarchy where predictive-coding pipelines for visual objects, scenes, and linguistic units converge on a shared amodal hub modeled on the anterior temporal lobe. Each pipeline's prediction is conditioned by the hub state, preserving pipeline-specific identity while reflecting multimodal context. The model also recovers surprisal theory, N400/P600 ERP components, and garden-path reanalysis, and proposes falsifiable contrasts with transformer language models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Energy-based models (EBMs) are a class of generative models that define probability distributions via an energy function, often used for density estimation and sampling. Predictive coding is a theory in neuroscience suggesting the brain continuously generates predictions about sensory input and updates them based on prediction errors. The hub-and-spoke architecture is a framework in cognitive science for multimodal semantic cognition, where modality-specific 'spokes' converge on a shared 'hub' (e.g., the anterior temporal lobe) to integrate information.

## [SynWeaver: Co-Synthesizing Website Priors and Trajectories for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12429) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynWeaver is a novel framework that co-synthesizes website priors and task-trajectory data to improve web agent generalization to unseen websites. It constructs a website map, trains a UI-aware model with website-specific priors, and performs collaborative task-trajectory synthesis with verification and repair. This framework addresses a critical bottleneck in web agent development: the lack of website-specific supervision that limits generalization. By improving data synthesis efficiency and quality, SynWeaver could accelerate the deployment of LLM-based web agents in real-world automation, reducing reliance on manual annotation. SynWeaver outperforms strong synthesis baselines on WebArena and WebVoyager, showing improved in-domain and out-of-domain generalization. It addresses limitations of exploration-based methods by covering full website functionality and reducing hallucinated tasks through website priors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Web agents are AI systems that interact with websites to perform tasks like searching and form filling. Training them typically requires large amounts of task-trajectory data, which is often manually annotated or synthesized through exploration. However, exploration-based synthesis often misses full website functionality and may generate hallucinated tasks without website priors. SynWeaver introduces a co-synthesis approach that jointly generates tasks and trajectories, leveraging website maps and UI-aware models to improve data quality and generalization.

## [X-AddGraph: Post-Hoc Explainability for Recurrent Graph Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12441) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces X-AddGraph, a post-hoc explainability framework for AddGraph, a GCN+GRU model for edge-level anomaly detection in dynamic graphs. It uses a Dual Spatial-Temporal Attribution (DSTA) mechanism with three components aligned to AddGraph's architecture, providing explanations without altering detection performance. This addresses a critical gap in explainability for graph anomaly detection, which is essential for auditable and trustworthy AI in regulated systems. By preserving detection accuracy exactly (Delta AUC = 0) while adding explanations, it enables practical deployment in sensitive domains. The DSTA mechanism includes gradient-based spatial attribution, direct reading of contextual attention weights (at zero cost), and gradient rollback through recurrent hidden states for long-term temporal attribution. On the UCI Message benchmark, the trained AddGraph baseline achieves an average per-snapshot AUC of 0.8705, and X-AddGraph reproduces scores identically while providing explanations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic graph anomaly detection identifies unusual edges in evolving networks, but deep learning models like AddGraph are often opaque. Post-hoc explainability methods aim to provide insights after the fact without modifying the model. This work focuses on AddGraph, a foundational framework combining graph convolutional networks (GCN) and gated recurrent units (GRU) for edge-level detection.

## [AI Journaling Nudges Work Best for Behaviors You Control Alone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12582) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A study of 369 journal entries from an eight-week passive sensing study found that AI journaling prompts are more effective for behaviors individuals can control alone, with follow-through rates of 50-63%, compared to only 15-22% for behaviors involving others. The study used an LLM to label intentions and measured follow-through against 26 sensor features. This finding highlights the importance of behavioral controllability in designing AI-driven interventions, suggesting that personalized prompts should account for whether a behavior depends on others. It provides practical guidance for improving AI journaling tools and similar behavior-change technologies. The study found that no single text feature consistently distinguished improved from unimproved entries; writing quality mattered only within specific behaviors, notably text messaging and longer, more personal intention entries. The sample size is small, so the authors treat the results as exploratory patterns.

## [Valuation-Anchored Similarity Learning for Private Markets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12594) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a CatBoost-based supervised similarity learning framework that defines company similarity via market valuation, deriving a valuation-aware metric from leaf-node co-occurrences. It was tested on ~270,000 private companies, including over 53,000 with valuations, and outperforms traditional distance and text-embedding methods in k-NN valuation tasks. This approach addresses a critical challenge in private markets—limited transparency and sparse data—by providing a more economically meaningful peer comparison method. It could improve valuation accuracy, portfolio construction, and risk management for investors, and offers a scalable, explainable alternative to existing techniques. The framework uses CatBoost gradient-boosted decision trees to model valuations, and similarity is derived from importance-weighted co-occurrences of leaf nodes across the ensemble. It handles nonlinear relationships, mixed data types, and pervasive missing data, while retaining case-based explainability.

## [Predicting Trainability of Random Low-Dimensional Reparameterizations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12597) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an orientation-resolved quadratic master formula to predict when random low-dimensional reparameterizations can train neural networks, and proposes RaMaN, a method that instantiates the predicted latent space. This work provides a theoretical framework for understanding and predicting the trainability of neural networks under random low-dimensional reparameterization, which could guide hyperparameter selection and reduce computational costs in practice. It addresses an open question in optimization and may influence future research in deep learning theory. The paper expresses the accessibility transition in conic form and derives a self-consistent isotropic-orientation predictor, with a conservative radius-only specialization recovering the earlier Gaussian-width quadratic bound. RaMaN uses structured Hadamard or seed-regenerated Gaussian maps to avoid O(dP) storage and reduce optimizer-state memory from O(P) to O(d), and includes matrix-free curvature approximations and sweep-free dimension selection.

**Background**: Neural networks can often be trained or fine-tuned through random low-dimensional reparameterization, where a small latent vector is mapped into a full parameter update by a frozen random map. This approach reduces the number of trainable parameters and memory usage, but the required latent dimension is often determined empirically. The paper provides a theoretical basis for predicting this dimension, improving efficiency and understanding.

## [Confidence-Aware Pseudo-Labeling for Semi-Supervised HD Map Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a teacher-student semi-supervised framework for online HD map construction that generates high-quality pseudo-labels using confidence-aware spatial clipping. The method improves performance by +6.1 mAP on the nuScenes dataset under a low-label regime compared to training on labeled data alone. This work addresses the critical challenge of labeled data scarcity in online HD map construction, which is essential for autonomous driving. By effectively leveraging unlabeled data, it could reduce the cost and effort of manual annotation and improve model generalization across diverse environments. The framework uses a Beta-distribution-based confidence map to assess the reliability of predicted map elements across temporal observations. Unlike conventional filtering that discards entire elements, the spatial clipping technique selectively preserves high-confidence regions while removing unreliable segments, and the refined pseudo-labels are used to train a student model from scratch.

## [EpicStar: Memory-Enhanced LLM Agents Boost Strategic Reasoning in StarCraft II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12626) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EpicStar, a framework that enables LLM agents to learn memory as policy, using a bank of successful past episodes and a dynamic gating mechanism to improve long-horizon strategic reasoning. In StarCraft II, EpicStar significantly outperforms baseline methods, achieving higher win rates while consuming an order of magnitude fewer tokens. This work addresses a critical limitation of LLMs in long-horizon strategic tasks, where finite attention leads to strategic drift. By demonstrating that structured cross-episode memory can enable robust long-term execution, EpicStar could influence the design of more capable autonomous agents in dynamic environments, with potential applications in gaming, robotics, and decision-making systems. EpicStar maintains a bank of successful past episodes as a heuristic alongside a working memory for short-term changes. During inference, a dynamic gating mechanism decides whether to execute a retrieved action directly or perform new reasoning via contextual fusion of retrieved episodes and current working memory. The framework was evaluated against diverse opponent styles in StarCraft II, consistently outperforming baselines across difficulty levels and strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) often struggle with long-horizon reasoning tasks because their finite attention resources prevent them from maintaining strategic coherence over thousands of steps, leading to strategic drift. Memory-augmented agents address this by storing and retrieving past experiences, but existing approaches often train memory policies using outcome-based reinforcement learning, which may not localize errors effectively. EpicStar introduces a novel 'memory as policy' approach, where memory is directly used to guide actions, combined with a dynamic gating mechanism to balance retrieval and reasoning.

## [AI Novels Show Compressed Formal Variation Across Generations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12630) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint compares six corpora of AI-generated and human-written novels, finding that repeated AI generation produces novels with significantly less variation in sentence structure, readability, punctuation, and lexical measures compared to human corpora. The study introduces the concepts of 'variance overclosure' and 'correlational overclosure' to describe this phenomenon. This research challenges the assumption that AI can match the creative diversity of human authors in long-form text, with implications for AI-assisted writing, content generation, and our understanding of machine creativity. It highlights a potential limitation of LLMs that could affect their use in creative industries. The study used GPT-5.5 Thinking and Qwen3-14B to generate 20 novels each in two styles (nineteenth-century British realist and contemporary zero style), comparing them against 205 historical and 65 contemporary human novels. Metrics included MATTR-500, Shannon entropy, average sentence length, readability, and punctuation rate, with sentence structure compression being the most robust finding.

## [Interpretable Causal Discovery via Causal-Effect Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12640) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel method for conditional causal discovery, framing it as Bayesian inference with rare-event estimation to efficiently infer causal graphs and parameters under user-specified causal-effect constraints. The approach is validated on synthetic graphs and a case study on the Sachs protein dataset. This work addresses a significant computational challenge in causal discovery, enabling more interpretable and hypothesis-driven analysis of causal relationships. It could benefit researchers in AI/ML and systems who need to explain observed or hypothesized phenomena, such as large causal effects. The method adapts rare-event estimation techniques to perform inference on the joint graph-parameter space, gradually driving a particle population toward the constrained region while maintaining samples approximating the conditional posterior. Empirical evaluation shows accuracy at small and large scales, and the case study provides pathway-level summaries for scientific exploration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Causal discovery aims to uncover underlying causal relationships from data, but traditional methods often focus on predicting edges rather than interpreting specific phenomena. Bayesian causal discovery provides a probabilistic framework, but it struggles when the event of interest has small posterior mass. Rare-event estimation techniques, such as importance sampling, are designed to efficiently estimate probabilities of rare events, which this paper adapts to the causal discovery context.

This paper introduces a method to estimate Demand Transfer (DT) coefficients for large item universes (1 million+ items) using a restricted logit model, enabling assortment optimization without exhaustive demand forecasts. The approach is validated on historical transaction data across multiple locations and categories. This work addresses a scalability bottleneck in retail assortment optimization, where traditional choice modeling requires separate forecasts for every possible assortment. By enabling efficient DT coefficient estimation, it can help retailers improve demand forecasting and optimize product mix at scale, potentially reducing costs and increasing revenue. Demand Transfer (DT) coefficients represent the percentage of demand redirected to other items when a target item is unavailable, allowing independent forecasts to be adjusted without full choice modeling.

## [HybridSB-MoE: Dual-Domain Schrödinger Bridges with Adaptive Expert Routing for Speech Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HybridSB-MoE introduces a dual-domain speech enhancement framework that combines spectral and waveform Schrödinger Bridges with scene-adaptive expert routing. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on VoiceBank+DEMAND, outperforming diffusion- and SB-based baselines at their step budgets while remaining competitive with consistency-distilled few-step methods. This work addresses three critical gaps in generative speech enhancement: phase disruption in spectral models, harmonic loss in waveform models, and the loose link between inference cost and training in Schrödinger Bridges. By unifying these with a single asymmetric design principle, it offers a more efficient and effective approach that could influence future research in generative audio processing. The framework uses asymmetric uncertainty fusion, where the spectral path captures epistemic uncertainty via expert disagreement and the waveform bridge models aleatoric variance through stochastic dynamics. It employs a heterogeneous MoE with top-k=2 routing across five architectural archetypes, and provides a discretization bound (Theorem 1) that guarantees K-step bridge sampling error in 2-Wasserstein distance at rate K^{-alpha}.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Schrödinger Bridges (SB) are a class of generative models that learn to transport noise to data distributions with optimal transport-like properties, often used for tasks like image and audio generation. Mixture of Experts (MoE) is a technique that uses multiple specialized sub-models (experts) with a routing mechanism to improve model capacity and efficiency. In machine learning, epistemic uncertainty refers to model uncertainty that can be reduced with more data, while aleatoric uncertainty is inherent noise in the data. Speech enhancement aims to improve the quality and intelligibility of noisy speech by separating clean speech from background noise.

## [Error-Aware Reverse Auction Mechanism for Cost-Effective LLM Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12719) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces EA-RAM, a novel reverse auction mechanism for LLM routing that shifts performance prediction from a centralized router to LLM providers, who bid with self-predicted success probabilities and costs. EA-RAM explicitly models dual error from noisy provider predictions and center evaluations, and provides theoretical guarantees including Bayesian incentive compatibility and welfare-loss bounds. This work addresses scalability and information asymmetry issues in centralized LLM routing, offering a market-based alternative that can improve cost-performance trade-offs. It could enable more efficient and cost-effective deployment of LLMs in real-world applications, especially as the number of available models grows. EA-RAM is proven to be Bayesian incentive compatible and individually rational under dual error, with sufficient conditions for center rationality and an explicit welfare-loss bound. Experiments on simulations and real-world benchmarks show that EA-RAM achieves a better cost-performance Pareto frontier than centralized baselines, with additional gains when providers contribute local information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Reverse auctions are a market mechanism where buyers solicit bids from sellers, typically driving prices down. In the context of LLM routing, a reverse auction allows LLM providers to bid for queries, potentially reducing the information burden on a central router. Bayesian incentive compatibility ensures that participants truthfully reveal private information, which is crucial for the mechanism's efficiency.

## [ERSkill: Self-Evolving Skill-Guided Memory Retrieval for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12720) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ERSkill, a retrieval-centric framework that treats memory retrieval as an evolvable component, using a trained router to dynamically select optimal retrieval skills for each query. It co-evolves the skill set and router during training, achieving significant improvements over baselines on agent memory benchmarks. This addresses a key limitation in current LLM agents where memory retrieval is static and not adaptive to heterogeneous queries. By enabling self-evolving retrieval skills, ERSkill could improve the performance and adaptability of long-term memory systems in AI agents, impacting fields like conversational AI and autonomous agents. ERSkill uses an experience trie to record explored retrieval paths and a double-frontier mechanism to decouple the expansion of new skill capabilities from stable router deployment. Experiments show it improves average F1, BLEU-1, and LLM-judge scores by 31.3% with Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B-Instruct and 28.1% with GPT-5.4-nano.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly rely on long-term memory for persistent interactions, but the retrieval mechanisms are often static and not evolvable. This paper proposes a framework where retrieval behaviors are represented as executable skills composed of primitives, and a router selects the optimal skill for each query, allowing the system to adapt and improve over time.

## [CRAFT: LLM Iterative Refinement for Temporal Reasoning in Clinical Narratives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12779) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CRAFT introduces an LLM framework that pairs a generator with a constraint-based verifier to iteratively refine symptom timelines from clinical narratives, and it is evaluated on a new benchmark, MedTempo, which includes 5,347 vaccine adverse-event narratives with expert-validated temporal stage annotations for 3,166 reports. This work addresses the underexplored problem of reconstructing structured symptom trajectories from anchor-sparse clinical reports, which is critical for disease monitoring and safety surveillance. By introducing a new benchmark and demonstrating consistent improvements across multiple LLMs, CRAFT could advance automated temporal reasoning in healthcare AI. CRAFT is inspired by iterative refinement paradigms like Self-Refine and can be instantiated with different generator and verifier configurations. The ablation analysis isolates the contribution of each component across model capability levels, and experiments show consistent improvement in temporal ordering accuracy across four LLM backbones.

Traditional methods focus on pairwise relation classification in timestamp-rich records, but reconstructing full timelines from sparse narratives remains challenging. LLMs offer a promising approach due to their language understanding and generation capabilities, and iterative refinement can improve their output quality.

## [Controllable Erasure of Copyrighted Animation Characters via Optimized Semantic Anchors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12806) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a controllable method that optimizes an anchor embedding in the continuous textual representation of text-to-image diffusion models to erase copyrighted animation characters during generation. The approach achieves state-of-the-art erasure effectiveness and image fidelity preservation, while supporting controllable erasure degree, multi-target removal, and model transferability. This work addresses the timely issue of copyright infringement in AI-generated content, particularly the unauthorized reproduction of animation characters. By providing a controllable and effective erasure method, it could help content creators and platforms comply with copyright regulations while maintaining image quality, potentially influencing future AI safety and content moderation practices. The method optimizes an anchor embedding via structural and detailed constraints to serve as a character surrogate, then replaces target-related embeddings with the anchor using a structure-aware adaptive strategy. The optimized anchors are plug-and-play with existing model modification baselines, improving their erasure performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image diffusion models like Stable Diffusion and Imagen generate images from text prompts but can reproduce copyrighted characters without authorization. Existing concept erasure methods fall short for animation characters: model modification methods struggle to identify suitable anchors for diverse, highly distinctive characters, while prompt-based steering methods lack fine-grained control. This paper proposes a novel approach that operates on the model's continuous textual representation to achieve controllable and effective erasure.

## [FSGR Framework Mitigates Token Frequency Bias in SID-Based Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FSGR, a fairness optimization framework for semantic ID (SID)-based generative recommendation, which addresses the previously overlooked token frequency bias. FSGR employs OT-based assignment optimization, dual-criteria re-anchoring, and hierarchical frequency calibration to achieve over 20% average Gini fairness improvement while maintaining competitive accuracy. This work addresses a novel fairness issue in a rapidly growing area of recommender systems, highlighting that generative models can introduce systematic biases beyond traditional popularity bias. The proposed framework offers a practical solution that could lead to more equitable exposure across item categories, benefiting both users and content providers. FSGR is evaluated on three public datasets with three backbone models, showing an average Gini fairness improvement of over 20% without significant loss in recommendation accuracy. The method includes a two-stage training strategy and layer-specific fairness fine-tuning, which are designed to handle the hierarchical semantics of SID tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic ID (SID)-based generative recommendation uses semantic tokens to represent items and leverages large language models (LLMs) for recommendation. Token frequency bias arises from imbalanced semantic codebooks and popularity bias during training, leading to unfair exposure across item categories. Existing debiasing methods for LLMs often fail when applied to SID-based systems due to the hierarchical nature of SID tokens.

## [AI Models Distinguish Falsehood from Impossibility Internally](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint study reports that the multimodal model Gemma 3 4B IT, despite conflating false statements with contradictions in its outputs, internally encodes a distinction between necessary falsehoods and contingent falsehoods, as revealed by activation analysis. An impossibility probe achieved perfect separation (AUC 1.00) on held-out topics, while the truth and impossibility directions were nearly orthogonal. This work contributes to AI interpretability by showing that language models may internally represent philosophical distinctions that are not reflected in their outputs, offering a new avenue for probing model understanding. It also bridges philosophy of language and machine learning, potentially informing future efforts to align model behavior with internal knowledge. The study used 85 prompts from 17 philosophical families and a topic-matched modality set of 15 topics, each expressed as truth, contingent falsehood, improbable claim, semantic anomaly, and necessary falsehood. A linear truth probe separated impossible from true statements (AUC 0.93) but not impossible from false statements (AUC 0.20), while an impossibility probe achieved AUC 1.00 at layer 15 with balanced accuracy 0.97 (Bonferroni-adjusted P=0.018). Sparse autoencoder features at the same layer repeated this geometry, with impossibility features also firing on anomalous sentences but rarely on contingent falsehoods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Activation analysis is a mechanistic interpretability technique that examines the internal states of neural networks to understand how they process information. Linear probes are simple classifiers trained on these activations to test whether specific information is linearly accessible. This study applies these methods to explore whether a language model internally distinguishes between falsehoods (statements that are not true) and impossibilities (statements that could not be true in any possible world), a distinction rooted in philosophy of language.

## [Curvature Factorization in Probabilistic Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12869) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that the Hessian trace of log-likelihood in probabilistic circuits factorizes into node flow and local sharpness, and introduces an adaptive sharpness-aware regularizer that preserves closed-form EM updates. This work provides a theoretical explanation for why global sharpness regularization is depth-biased and can underfit, offering a principled alternative that improves generalization in generative modeling. It impacts researchers working on probabilistic circuits and sharpness-aware learning. The decomposition shows that each sum node's contribution to the Hessian trace equals its circuit flow multiplied by a local sharpness term. The proposed adaptive regularizer penalizes nodes based on intrinsic local curvature and retains closed-form EM updates, empirically recovering generalization lost by global regularization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilistic circuits (PCs) are generative models that support exact inference and tractable computation of loss-surface curvature, such as the Hessian trace. Sharpness-aware regularization aims to bias learning toward flatter optima for better generalization, but applying it globally can be misspecified for compositional models like PCs.

## [NaviDC-OCR: Unified VLM Framework for Robust Document Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12898) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NaviDC-OCR introduces a unified VLM-based framework for document parsing that handles geometric distortions and improves structural reasoning in high-resolution scenarios. It achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks, including first place in the ICDAR 2026 Sci-ImageMiner Challenge. This work addresses critical limitations in existing document parsing methods, particularly the cascading errors from geometric distortions and the hallucination issues in end-to-end VLM approaches. Its unified framework could improve the reliability and accuracy of document understanding systems, benefiting applications in digitization, information extraction, and AI-driven document processing. The framework introduces deformation-aware learning to incorporate geometric perception into VLMs, an adaptive sampling mechanism for complex layout representation, and a content-structure decoupled learning strategy to model formula grammars and table structures. It achieves overall scores of 96.87, 88.53, and 78.41 on OmniDocBench v1.6, Wild-OmniDocBench, and PureDocBench, respectively.

**Background**: Document parsing converts unstructured documents into structured, machine-readable formats. Recent advances in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have improved this task, but existing methods often rely on decoupled layout analysis, which is sensitive to geometric distortions, or suffer from redundant generation and hallucinations in high-resolution settings. NaviDC-OCR aims to overcome these challenges by unifying deformation handling, adaptive sampling, and decoupled content-structure learning.

## [InFactPlanner: A Framework for Sustainable Geo-Distributed LLM Data Centers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12915) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InFactPlanner is a trace-driven decision-support framework that enables what-if analysis for sustainable deployment of LLM data centers across single and geo-distributed sites. It integrates query traces, hardware profiles, site configurations, PUE/WUE, renewable generation, and grid carbon intensity to estimate power, energy, carbon, water, latency, and utilization. This framework addresses the growing sustainability concerns of LLM inference, which now shift from one-time training to continuous serving. It helps operators compare deployment alternatives before building infrastructure, potentially reducing environmental impact while maintaining service quality. The framework abstracts low-level serving effects into configurable hardware-model profiles, enabling rapid comparison of site selection, capacity placement, hardware, model, renewable integration, and routing choices. Validation shows energy estimates within 10% deviation, and results indicate sustainability-optimal choices can differ from latency-optimal ones, with carbon value depending on local grid mix.

**Background**: What-if analysis, also known as sensitivity analysis, studies how uncertainty in model inputs affects outputs, helping decision-makers evaluate alternatives. ## [E2-Explainer: Causal Framework for LLM Multi-Agent Topologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces E2-Explainer, a model-agnostic causal inference framework that provides interpretable explanations for communication topologies in LLM-based multi-agent systems. It formulates topology explanation as a causal attribution problem, identifying compact communication subgraphs via a Granger-style objective. This work addresses the critical lack of explainability in LLM-based multi-agent systems, where communication topologies are typically learned via black-box optimization. By enabling post-hoc explanations and pruning of redundant edges, it can reduce communication costs and improve interpretability, benefiting applications in reasoning and coding tasks. E2-Explainer uses a Granger-style objective to measure how masking each communication channel affects task outcomes and response stability, distilling budgeted subgraphs into an amortized explainer for efficient deployment. Experiments on reasoning and coding benchmarks show that identified subgraphs preserve collaboration while reducing communication costs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based multi-agent systems rely on communication topologies to coordinate agents, but existing methods often optimize these topologies using task-level rewards without explaining why certain edges are chosen. Causal inference, such as Granger causality, helps determine whether one time series predicts another, which can be adapted to assess the causal impact of communication channels. This framework aims to bridge the gap between performance and interpretability in multi-agent systems.

This paper presents Handball-xT (H-xT) and Handball-VAEP (H-VAEP), the first adaptations of football's Expected Threat (xT) and Valuing Actions by Estimating Probabilities (VAEP) frameworks to handball, using five seasons of tracking data from the Handball Bundesliga. This work extends advanced action valuation metrics to a new sport, enabling more nuanced player evaluation in handball that credits build-up play, and provides a robust, open-source tool for professional clubs. It also demonstrates the transferability of football analytics frameworks to other team sports. H-xT uses a handball-native court zoning layout, which simulations show is more robust than standard rectangular grids. H-VAEP is optimized by tailoring its feature space and selecting context length to limit team-identity leakage, yielding stable, discriminative, and intuitive player ratings.

**Background**: Expected Threat (xT) is a football metric that values actions based on how they increase the probability of scoring, going beyond shot-based metrics like Expected Goals (xG). VAEP (Valuing Actions by Estimating Probabilities) similarly evaluates every on-the-ball action by its impact on scoring probability. These frameworks have become influential in football analytics, and this paper adapts them to handball, a sport with similar event-based dynamics but different court and rules.

## [DrIG: Generative Universal Multimodal Retrieval with Dual-role Identifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12987) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DrIG, a novel generative framework for universal multimodal retrieval that uses dual-role identifiers to support text, image, and mixed image-text retrieval tasks. It outperforms state-of-the-art generative multimodal baselines on the M-BEIR benchmark and text-to-image datasets. This work addresses key limitations in generative information retrieval (GIR), such as prefix-level errors and limited multimodal support, potentially advancing the field toward more efficient and accurate retrieval systems. It could impact applications like search engines and recommendation systems that handle diverse content types. DrIG assigns each candidate a single residual-quantized identifier that serves both sequential and set-based roles, where the first token models modality and subsequent tokens capture semantics. The set-based role provides a prefix-independent relevance prior to guide constrained beam search, and hybrid reranking achieves a favorable efficiency-effectiveness trade-off.

**Background**: Generative information retrieval (GIR) trains a generator to directly produce identifiers of relevant items, contrasting with traditional index-retrieve-then-rank pipelines. However, existing GIR methods often suffer from constrained decoding errors, are mostly unimodal, and lag behind dense retrieval in accuracy. DrIG aims to overcome these challenges by introducing dual-role identifiers that improve decoding robustness and support multimodal retrieval.

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that GraphRAG, a knowledge-graph-based retrieval system, produces more durable cyber threat detection plans than Naive RAG. In a case study on an APT28 report, the GraphRAG plan maintained 100% detection after rotating all IPs, domains, and file hashes, while Naive RAG dropped to 29%. The study compared GraphRAG and Naive RAG using the same report, generation instructions, and language model, with only the retrieval step differing. Across nine real CTI reports from four vendors, GraphRAG consistently reached higher, harder-to-evade levels of the Pyramid of Pain, even when total scores were similar.

This paper suggests that knowledge-graph-aware retrieval is architecturally better for generating SOC-deployable hunting plans.

The paper introduces LOB-ID, an embedding-based evaluation framework that adapts Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) and Monge Inception Distance (MIND) to limit order book (LOB) data, using DeepLOB embeddings trained on four months of Level-2 data for five equities. It demonstrates that LOB-ID is stable across time, instruments, and checkpoints, and rises monotonically under controlled distortions. LOB-ID provides a more robust metric, potentially improving model comparison and development in quantitative finance and machine learning. The paper constructs a moment-matching attack against FID and a deep-book perturbation that evades statistic-based evaluation, showing MIND remains substantially more sensitive to both distortions. It also scores five generative LOB models, ranking them in line with the joint temporal and cross-level structure each captures by construction.

## [LipCache: Certified Semantic Caching for Edge Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13144) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LipCache introduces a certified semantic caching framework for edge image classification, using a Lipschitz-constrained GuardNet to provide per-sample reuse guarantees without modifying the main model. It achieves up to 1.65x speedup on CIFAR, Tiny-ImageNet, and SVHN while maintaining 100% certified consistency. This work addresses a key limitation of empirical thresholds in semantic caching by providing theoretical guarantees, improving reliability for edge AI services. It offers a practical approach to reduce inference costs without sacrificing accuracy, which is crucial for low-latency, high-throughput edge scenarios. The framework computes a certified reuse radius from the local classification margin and the spectral norm of the classification head, and only reuses cached results when the query feature falls inside the certified ball. An enhanced GuardNet training recipe improves cache hit rates on Tiny-ImageNet while maintaining a certified-consistency rate of 100%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic caching in AI systems typically relies on empirical similarity thresholds to reuse results for similar inputs, but these thresholds can cause silent misclassifications near decision boundaries. Lipschitz-constrained neural networks limit output changes relative to input changes, providing robustness and stability. LipCache leverages these concepts to turn cache hits into geometric certification decisions with explicit theoretical boundaries.

## [Syfer: Synthesizer-Folding Framework for Multilingual Multi-Hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Syfer, a synthesizer-folding framework for multilingual multi-hop question answering that defers translation and uses a format-constrained decomposer to generate a sub-question graph in the original language, with a decomposition-quality check and bilingual fallback only when needed. Experiments show competitive accuracy with a favorable performance-cost balance. This framework addresses key limitations in multilingual RAG for multi-hop QA, such as translation noise and error compounding from uncontrolled decomposition. It offers a more efficient and accurate approach, potentially improving multilingual QA systems in real-world applications. Syfer consists of four stages: offline logical-decomposition distillation, synthesizer-folded decomposition, faithfulness verification with bilingual fallback, and aggregation. The method avoids blanket translation by activating the English translation pathway only when the decomposition-quality check fails.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multilingual retrieval-augmented generation (mRAG) enhances LLMs with external knowledge for complex QA. Traditional approaches either translate documents or decompose queries, but both have drawbacks. Syfer introduces a synthesizer-folding mechanism to improve decomposition and defer translation, reducing noise and cost.

## [NARU: Benchmark for Japanese Long-Form Video Narrative and Cultural Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13210) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NARU is a new benchmark for evaluating narrative evolution and cultural understanding in Japanese long-form videos, comprising 1,481 questions across 155 videos totaling 146.8 hours. It introduces a hierarchical memory-based annotation pipeline with two native-speaker verification stages involving 68 annotators. NARU addresses a gap in existing benchmarks by jointly evaluating narrative tracking and cultural reasoning in high-context, non-English media. It provides a systematic testing ground for developing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) capable of reliably interpreting long-form, high-context video. The benchmark spans four narrative and five cultural dimensions, and evaluations across eight model configurations reveal substantial limitations in long-range narrative integration and culturally grounded reasoning. The construction process includes task-oriented synthesis and iterative shortcut removal to ensure question quality.

**Background**: Long-form video understanding involves tracking evolving narratives and interpreting implicit social meaning, which is challenging for current AI models. Existing benchmarks often focus on isolated events and lack cultural nuance, especially for non-English content. NARU aims to fill this gap by providing a benchmark specifically for Japanese media, which is known for its high-context communication style.

## [CoverPrune: Coverage-Driven Token Pruning for 3D VLMs via Optimal Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13226) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoverPrune introduces a training-free token pruning framework for 3D vision-language models (VLMs) that formulates inference-time pruning as an optimal transport (OT) problem, using a Feature-Spatial-Temporal (FST) transport cost and a Spatial-Guided Greedy Selection (SGS) algorithm. It also proposes CoverPrune-Lite, an accelerated variant with spatially structured local matching, achieving state-of-the-art token efficiency on multiple 3D spatial reasoning benchmarks. This work addresses a critical computational bottleneck in 3D VLMs by enabling aggressive token pruning without sacrificing spatial reasoning, which is essential for real-time applications like robotics and autonomous driving. It shifts the paradigm from diversity-based selection to coverage preservation, potentially improving efficiency across the 3D understanding ecosystem. The method is training-free and uses optimal transport to select tokens that preserve visual evidence coverage, overcoming the combinatorial subset selection problem with an efficient greedy approximation. CoverPrune-Lite further reduces overhead using spatially structured local matching, and experiments show robust performance even under highly aggressive pruning budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: 3D vision-language models (VLMs) combine 3D perception with language understanding, enabling spatial reasoning tasks, but they generate massive numbers of visual tokens, causing high inference costs. Token pruning is a common technique to reduce redundancy by discarding low-utility tokens, but existing methods often rely on diversity-based selection, which can drop representative tokens and break geometric consistency. Optimal transport (OT) is a mathematical framework for efficiently matching distributions, which CoverPrune leverages to formulate token selection as a coverage optimization problem.

## [Normative Fine-Tuning Shifts AI Rationales; Audit Trail Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13250) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that fine-tuning on norm-breaking datasets shifts AI models' default rationale style from safety compliance to instrumental self-interest in high-conflict dilemmas, and establishes a practical audit trail linking downstream justifications to upstream norms. Experiments were conducted on three models (LLaMA-3.2-11B, Qwen-3.5-9B, Pixtral-12B) using LoRA fine-tuning on Social Chemistry 101 Fairness/Cheating with prompt steering. This research addresses a critical gap in AI alignment by showing that normative datasets can act as action-guiding patterns rather than neutral moral knowledge, potentially shifting model behavior away from safety baselines. The proposed audit trail supports contestable oversight and norm-aware documentation, which are essential for accountability in AI systems deployed in high-stakes domains. The study used Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for fine-tuning and system prompts to both suppress and elicit norm-divergent patterns. Results show that norm-breaking fine-tuning shifts rationale style across all three models, but system prompts can override this behavior, supporting a distributed view of alignment.

## [GeoCache: Training-Free Speedup for Multi-View Texture Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13255) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GeoCache is a training-free plugin that accelerates multi-view texture diffusion by reusing geometry-aligned updates from a rotating subset of anchor views, achieving a 2.21x denoiser-loop speedup on Hunyuan3D-2.1 with minimal fidelity loss. This method addresses a key computational bottleneck in 3D texture generation, offering a practical speed-fidelity trade-off that could improve efficiency in production workflows. It establishes cross-view geometry as a new acceleration axis, potentially influencing future optimization strategies for multi-view diffusion models. GeoCache requires no retraining or architectural changes, using position maps already available in geometry-conditioned pipelines. It achieves the best fidelity among methods above 2x speedup on Hunyuan3D-2.1, with MV-LPIPS of 0.0293 and MV-PSNR of 33.60 dB, and also shows strong results on SyncMVD and MVPainter.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-view texture diffusion generates 3D textures by denoising multiple views of an object, but repeated per-view evaluations are computationally expensive. Existing training-free accelerators exploit temporal redundancy across denoising steps, but skipping steps can break cross-view consistency. GeoCache identifies a complementary redundancy: geometry-aligned surface points have transferable clean-signal evolution, enabling reuse across views.

## [ORBIT Training Paradigm Boosts Time Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13262) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ORBIT, a training paradigm for time series foundation models that explicitly controls data distribution and training dynamics, and presents Falcon-2.0, a transformer-based model trained under this paradigm, along with a new training objective called Rank-Guided Cross-Depth Alignment. This work addresses the under-explored area of training regimes for time series foundation models, potentially improving zero-shot forecasting performance across diverse domains and frequencies. It could influence future research and development in the field by providing a controllable training paradigm. ORBIT combines Bootstrap Multi-Level Sampling, which controls dataset exposure and samples records, target variables, context windows, and prediction horizons, with Omni-Range Incremental Training, which varies context lengths and prediction horizons throughout a single training stage. Falcon-2.0 is a simple univariate encoder-only Transformer with missingness-aware triple-channel patch tokenization and parallel patch prediction, and the new objective uses late-layer representations as stop-gradient teachers for shallow layers without additional inference cost.

## [Physics-Informed Autoencoder for DRT Estimation and SOC Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13305) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a physics-informed convolutional autoencoder that estimates the distribution of relaxation times (DRT) directly from electrochemical impedance spectroscopy (EIS) data without spectrum-specific tuning. The model achieves range-normalized errors below 1.1% on three independent solid oxide cell datasets and enables latent-space condition monitoring. This work addresses a long-standing ill-posed inverse problem in electrochemistry with a novel physics-informed deep learning approach, offering a consistent and interpretable method for DRT estimation. It also demonstrates practical benefits for condition monitoring of solid oxide fuel and electrolysis cells, which is crucial for the durability and efficiency of these energy systems. The model embeds a discretized relation between impedance and DRT into the training process, constraining outputs to be impedance-consistent. Decoder-probe analysis reveals that the latent representation is organized by relaxation timescale, and distances in this space capture operating changes, hydrogen-shortage events, and long-term degradation.

Physics-informed neural networks integrate physical laws into the learning process, improving generalization and interpretability. ## [Optimal Default Reasoning-Token Allocation and Pricing in LLM Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper models LLM service pricing and default reasoning-token allocation as a Stackelberg game, deriving the user's unique optimal customized allocation in closed form and characterizing the provider's optimal default via a three-regime rule. It proves equilibrium existence and reduces computation to a one-dimensional price optimization. This work provides a rigorous framework for understanding how LLM providers should set prices and default reasoning-token allocations, balancing accuracy, cost, and user convenience. It offers practical insights for service design and could influence how reasoning-intensive LLM services are priced and configured in the industry. The paper uses two compact open-weight reasoning models on five mathematics and science benchmarks to validate the accuracy-token model. It shows that defaults only affect the implemented reasoning allocation when users value the convenience of avoiding customization; otherwise, the user's optimal customized allocation is implemented.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In LLM services, reasoning tokens are additional tokens generated during inference that allow the model to 'think' before answering, improving accuracy but increasing cost and latency. A Stackelberg game is a sequential game where a leader (the provider) moves first, and a follower (the user) responds optimally, which is suitable for modeling pricing and allocation decisions in such services.

This paper introduces a loss-guided multi-expert GAN framework for sign language video synthesis, featuring three specialized discriminators (global, hand, head) and a United Loss consensus mechanism to stabilize training. The drift-aware retraining strategy achieves accuracy comparable to periodic retraining while significantly reducing the number of retrained models. Concept drift is a critical challenge in malware detection, as attackers constantly evolve malware, degrading model performance over time. The study compares three drift detection techniques—OCSVM, MK-Means, and MMD—across four learning models: Multilayer Perceptron, Random Forest, Support Vector Machines, and eXtreme Gradient Boosting. It analyzes three scenarios (static, periodic, and drift-aware) and uses Pareto Front analysis to evaluate the accuracy-efficiency tradeoff, finding that OCSVM generally outperforms the other two methods.

## [AaLLM: End-to-End Analog Circuit Design with Multi-Agent LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13472) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AaLLM is an open-source, end-to-end multi-agent LLM workflow that automates analog circuit design from topology generation to sizing, taking user specifications as input and outputting a netlist. It introduces a tri-agent feedback system (Designer, Critic, Evaluator) and a RAG-based knowledge base to reduce hallucinations and improve efficiency. This work addresses fragmentation and hallucination issues in prior LLM-based analog design methods, potentially accelerating the design process and enabling novel topologies. It could significantly impact the EDA industry and AI-assisted hardware design by reducing manual effort and improving design quality. AaLLM achieves up to 3x higher figure of merit (FoM) for certain circuits compared to known topologies, and reduces SPICE calls by 3x-4.5x and wall-clock time by 40x versus state-of-the-art multi-agent LLM pipelines. The framework automatically builds a knowledge base from research papers and textbooks, and uses a RAG model to emulate circuit design expertise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Analog circuit design is a complex, iterative process that traditionally relies on expert intuition. Recent LLM-based approaches have attempted to automate this, but often provide fragmented solutions focusing only on sizing or topology, and suffer from hallucinations. Multi-agent LLM systems leverage multiple specialized agents to handle complex tasks, and RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) enhances LLM outputs by retrieving relevant information from a knowledge base.

## [RL with Symbolic Heuristics Improves Temporal Planning Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13372) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes an evolution of the RL-based heuristic synthesis framework for temporal planning, introducing new reward schemata that leverage symbolic heuristics to mitigate episode truncation issues, and learning a residual correction to existing symbolic heuristics rather than learning from scratch. It also combines the learned heuristic with a symbolic heuristic in a multiple-queue planning approach. This work advances the state of the art in combining reinforcement learning with symbolic heuristics for temporal planning, potentially improving the efficiency of planners in domains with fixed problem sets. It offers a practical approach to leverage both learned and symbolic information, which could benefit the AI planning community and applications like logistics and scheduling. The paper formalizes different reward schemata for RL synthesis and uses symbolic heuristics to handle infinite-state MDPs by mitigating episode truncation. It also proposes learning a residual of an existing symbolic heuristic, and uses a multiple-queue planning approach to balance systematic search with imperfect learned information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal planning extends classical AI planning by incorporating time as a resource, requiring the selection and scheduling of actions. Reinforcement learning has been used to synthesize heuristic guidance from value functions of MDPs constructed over training problems, but handling infinite-state MDPs often requires episode truncation. Symbolic heuristics, such as pattern databases, provide systematic search guidance and can be combined with learned heuristics to improve performance.

## [Closed-Form Bounds for Probabilities of Causation in Multi-Valued Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15274) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper derives closed-form bounds for probabilities of causation (PoCs) in multi-valued treatments and outcomes, extending beyond binary settings. It introduces equivalence classes of PoCs and a replaceability principle to generalize bounds across value permutations. This advance significantly broadens the applicability of counterfactual analysis and personalized decision-making, which previously relied on binary assumptions. It provides a theoretical foundation for deriving bounds in complex real-world scenarios, potentially impacting fields like medicine and policy. The bounds are proven sound in all dimensions and empirically verified tight in low-dimensional cases via Balke's linear programming method. The authors conjecture that tightness extends to all dimensions, and simulations show the closed-form bounds consistently tighten recent recursive bounds while being simpler to compute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilities of causation (PoCs) are fundamental quantities in counterfactual analysis, used to quantify the causal effect of a treatment on an outcome. Traditional results, such as Pearl's definitions of PNS, PS, and PN, were limited to binary variables. This work extends these concepts to multi-valued settings, using structural causal models and standard experimental and observational data.

## [Schema-Grounded Memory: Iterative Extraction for Reliable AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27906) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces xmemory, a schema-grounded memory system that uses an iterative, schema-aware extraction pipeline with validation gates and stateful prompt control to improve reliability of persistent AI memory. The system achieves 97.10% F1 on end-to-end memory benchmarks, outperforming third-party baselines. This work addresses a critical gap in AI memory systems by shifting from retrieval-based recall to schema-grounded, system-of-record memory, which is essential for production agents requiring exact facts and stateful computation. It demonstrates that architecture matters more than retrieval scale or model strength, potentially influencing future agent memory design. The pipeline decomposes memory ingestion into object detection, field detection, and field-value extraction, with validation gates and local retries. On extraction benchmarks, the judge-in-the-loop configuration achieves 90.42% object-level accuracy and 62.67% output accuracy, surpassing frontier structured-output baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Persistent AI memory is often treated as a retrieval problem, storing interactions as text and embedding them for later recall. However, production agents need exact facts, current state, updates, and relations, which require memory to behave like a system of record rather than search. Schema-grounded memory defines explicit contracts for what to remember and what to ignore, shifting interpretation from read-time inference to write-time validation.

## [Residual-Centric Coding Boosts High-Fidelity Scientific Data Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05389) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a residual-centric approach for high-fidelity learned compression of scientific data, proposing two new residual coders: LBRC (a deterministic, training-free pipeline) and NGLR (which adds a causal neural predictor). These coders significantly improve compression ratios over existing Guaranteed Autoencoder (GAE) methods in the high-fidelity regime (NRMSE 10^-6 to 10^-4). This work addresses a critical limitation of learned compressors in high-fidelity scientific data compression, where residual correction streams dominate the rate. By tailoring residual coding to the structure of learned residuals, it preserves the advantages of learned compression and outperforms traditional compressors like SZ, which is vital for massive spatiotemporal simulation data. LBRC adaptively quantizes the learned residual to the target NRMSE and losslessly encodes the integer residual using 3D Lorenzo differencing, zigzag mapping, bit-plane coding, and entropy coding. NGLR adds a causal neural predictor that outputs a normalized bias for integer-rounded Lorenzo prediction, reducing entropy while preserving deterministic decoding; predictor weights are serialized and counted in the bitstream.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Lossy compression is essential for massive spatiotemporal data from scientific simulations. Learned compressors can achieve high compression ratios at moderate accuracy targets, but their aggregate reconstruction losses do not guarantee per-block accuracy. Guaranteed Autoencoder (GAE) methods add per-block residual correction by retaining SVD/PCA-style coefficients until the target is met, but in high-fidelity regimes the correction stream dominates the rate. This paper proposes a residual-centric view, coding the learned residual with a representation designed for its structure.

## [ReTeX: Recovering Task Experts from Merged Multi-Task Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26902) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ReTeX, a framework that predicts and corrects parameter perturbations in merged multi-task models to recover task-specific experts without storing redundant components. It also proposes a router-free task identifier based on SVD subspace signatures. This work addresses the parameter interference problem in multi-task model merging, which is a significant barrier to efficient inference. By enabling recovery of task experts from a single merged checkpoint, it could reduce storage and computational costs, benefiting applications in vision and NLP. ReTeX models parameter interference as affine transformations approximated by additive offsets, and predicts these offsets to undo interference. The router-free task identifier uses SVD subspace signatures computed offline, selecting the task with the smallest projection residual for each input. ReTeX recovers over 95% of individual-expert performance in both vision and NLP domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task model merging combines several task-specific models into a single unified model, but static merging often suffers from parameter interference, where parameters for different tasks conflict. Dynamic merging methods attempt to mitigate this but often require storing and loading redundant expert components at inference. ReTeX aims to recover task experts from a single merged checkpoint without such overhead.

## [DAPD: Dual-Anchored Policy Distillation Fixes Privilege Illusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01735) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Anchored Policy Distillation (DAPD), a framework that addresses information asymmetry in on-policy self-distillation for language models. It achieves significant performance gains, outperforming standard OPSD by +2.00 points on Qwen3-4B and scaling to +2.69 at 4B and +2.78 at 32B. This work identifies and mitigates a core failure mode in on-policy self-distillation, which is increasingly used for LLM post-training. By preventing privilege-dependent behavior degradation, DAPD could improve the reliability and performance of distilled models across various scales, benefiting the AI community's efforts to create efficient and capable models. DAPD consists of two components: Dual-Path Anchoring (DPA) introduces a self-conditioned bridge to align reference and rollout behavior along matched-information paths, and Dual-Source Anchoring (DSA) applies these paths in both directions to reduce reliance on privileged guidance. The framework is validated on Qwen3 models, showing consistent improvements across scales.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) is a post-training technique where a single model acts as both teacher and student, using its own rollouts for supervision. However, it can suffer from a 'privilege illusion' where the student learns behaviors dependent on privileged information unavailable at inference, degrading performance. DAPD addresses this by anchoring the distillation process to mitigate information asymmetry.

## [DiffImaginE: Diffusion-Based Entity Type Verification for MNER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03025) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DiffImaginE introduces a novel approach to multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) type verification by formulating it as conditional latent diffusion inference, using denoising error as a surrogate for type-conditional negative log-likelihood to rank entity type hypotheses. This replaces the deterministic imagine-and-compare verifier with a classifier-free-guided diffusion scorer trained with Min-SNR weighting. This work provides a probabilistic interpretation of type verification, offering a principled way to rank entity type hypotheses, which is a meaningful improvement over existing deterministic methods. It demonstrates consistent gains on Twitter-2015 and Twitter-2017 benchmarks, potentially advancing the field of multimodal named entity recognition. The method retains a standard multimodal encoder stack and replaces the deterministic verifier with a classifier-free-guided diffusion scorer. It directly supervises per-type diffusion scores as classification logits, learns aggregation across noise levels, and uses antithetic sampling to reduce Monte Carlo comparison variance. Experiments show consistent gains over a matched deterministic ImaginE control, supported by ablations and paired significance tests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) determines whether each candidate span and entity-type hypothesis is supported by joint textual and visual evidence. Existing imagine-and-compare verifiers map each (span, type) pair to one predicted visual feature, compressing diverse visual realisations into a single prototype and providing a compatibility score without explicit probabilistic semantics. Conditional latent diffusion models perform diffusion in a lower-dimensional latent space, separating encoder-based representation learning from iterative, condition-guided denoising, which can provide a probabilistic framework for such verification tasks.

## [Surrogate Substitution Preserves PHI Detectability Across Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03172) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study introduces a paired, multi-detector evaluation protocol using equivalence testing (TOST) to assess whether surrogate substitution in de-identified clinical text preserves PHI detectability. Across 11 detectors, 7 benchmarks, and 7 languages, recall on masked spans changed from 76.1% to 74.9%, which was statistically equivalent to zero within a ±2-point margin. This work provides rigorous evidence that structure-preserving de-identification does not degrade downstream PHI detection, which is crucial for enabling safe sharing of clinical data. The proposed protocol offers a standardized method for auditing any structure-preserving transform, benefiting the clinical NLP community. The study uses a paired protocol that scores utility only on masked spans, decoupling coverage from utility, and employs TOST equivalence testing instead of null-hypothesis significance testing due to the large sample size (57k paired spans). Residual loss was concentrated in malformed and out-of-distribution surrogates, such as truncation ('Chicago' -> 'Illino') and salience loss ('Cedars-Sinai' -> 'Vidant'), indicating the effect is a property of well-formed substitution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: De-identification in clinical text often replaces protected health information (PHI) with placeholders like [NAME], which can disrupt natural language flow and downstream tools. Structure-preserving de-identification instead uses realistic same-type surrogates (e.g., 'Anna S.' becomes 'Maria S.') to maintain fluency. Equivalence testing (TOST) is a statistical method used to determine if two measurements are practically equivalent within a predefined margin, unlike traditional significance testing which only checks for differences.

## [NDS: FAIR Data Infrastructure for AI-Driven Nutrition Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10363) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Nutrition Data Service (NDS), a source-preserving infrastructure that operationalizes FAIR data principles for AI agents, enabling release-specific findability, typed crosswalks, and replayable analyses. NDS outperforms prior models on NutriBench and demonstrates stable outputs in glycemic-index analysis. This matters because AI agents in nutrition research often rely on ambiguous data, leading to reproducibility issues. NDS addresses this by making data identity, search, and crosswalk policies explicit, potentially setting a standard for data infrastructure in AI-driven scientific research. NDS uses description resolution to make release-specific records findable and typed crosswalks to connect independently released resources. External and blinded evaluations show that its typed contract favors defensible links and rejects unsupported mappings, while pinned inputs produce identical outputs across models and runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: FAIR data principles, published in 2016, emphasize findability, accessibility, interoperability, and reusability, with a focus on machine-actionability. In nutrition research, AI agents analyze datasets that often have identity, semantic, and release ambiguities, which NDS aims to resolve by providing a source-preserving infrastructure.

## [Rule of Thumb: New XAI Method Explains AI with Partial Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10766) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose 'Rule of Thumb' (RoT) explanations, a novel model-agnostic XAI approach that identifies the most relevant features for predicting an AI system's behavior on a given datapoint. The method is demonstrated in zero-shot classification with LLMs, auditing opaque AI systems without model access, and scientific discovery. RoT offers a faster, model-agnostic alternative to existing XAI methods, with broad applicability across LLMs, AI auditing, and scientific discovery. It also aligns with regulatory requirements, potentially democratizing access to AI explanations and enhancing transparency and accountability. The method requires only observations of prior model outputs, bypassing barriers like trade secrecy. It provides a familiar interface and visualizations for XAI practitioners, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Explainable AI (XAI) aims to make black-box AI decisions understandable. Traditional methods often require model access or are computationally expensive. RoT leverages partial information to generate explanations, making it suitable for auditing systems where only input-output pairs are available.

## [MBA-Bench: First Multimodal Benchmark for Business Ideation Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MBA-Bench, the first multimodal benchmark for training and evaluating business ideation agents, containing 30K samples across six domains. It also presents two agents, MBA-b and MBA-k, trained with novel reward objectives, which outperform baselines by significant margins. This work addresses a critical gap in AI-driven business ideation, which has been largely text-only despite the inherently multimodal nature of real-world business contexts. By providing a multimodal benchmark and agents, it enables more realistic and effective business idea generation, potentially impacting entrepreneurs, product managers, and AI researchers. The benchmark uses GPT-4o to generate reference ideas via retrieval query generation, market evidence retrieval, and evidence-augmented synthesis, and evaluates agents using MLLM-as-a-Judge across six criteria. MBA-b and MBA-k are trained with LoRA-based supervised fine-tuning followed by group relative policy optimization, with MBA-k also optimizing the six disclosed criteria.

## [LLM-Generated ICU Summaries Boost Mortality Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16818) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study introduces a multi-representational framework that fuses LLM-generated expert summaries of ICU notes with physiological data, achieving an AUPRC of 0.4977 and AUROC of 0.8429 for in-hospital mortality prediction, significantly outperforming physiology alone (AUPRC 0.3625, AUROC 0.7770). This work demonstrates a practical way to leverage LLMs to improve clinical prediction models, potentially enhancing ICU decision-making and patient outcomes. It also provides a rigorous methodology for assessing the added value of LLM-generated features, which is crucial for trustworthy AI in healthcare. The study used MIMIC-III data with 19,211 first ICU stays (12.83% mortality) and generated summaries under a prompt forbidding prognostication. Redundancy analysis via ridge recoverability showed that 40.8% of summary-embedding variance is explained by note embeddings, and a note-orthogonal residual retained only 28% of the gain, indicating summaries mostly reorganize existing information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In-hospital mortality (IHM) prediction in ICU settings typically relies on physiological data and clinical notes. Large language models (LLMs) can generate concise expert-like summaries from unstructured notes, potentially capturing relevant information. AUPRC (Area Under the Precision-Recall Curve) is a metric for imbalanced classification, and ridge recoverability is a technique to measure how much variance in one representation can be predicted from another, used here to assess redundancy.

## [Rehearse: Training-Free Method Improves LLM Confidence Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14390) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Rehearse is a training-free method that improves LLM confidence calibration by having models learn from their own scored confidence experiences in a game-like setting. It achieves the lowest average ECE among training-free methods, reducing average ECE by 58% relative to the uncalibrated baseline across four LLMs and three benchmarks. This method addresses a critical reliability issue in LLMs, where verbal confidence often misaligns with actual correctness, limiting their use in safety-critical applications. By providing a practical, training-free solution, it could enhance trust and decision-making in various LLM applications without requiring additional training resources. The method uses a credence-calibration game grounded in a strictly proper scoring rule, where the model receives feedback on prior confidence decisions, summarized in a post-game trajectory prefix. At inference time, the model applies this cross-instance calibration signal to the chain-of-thought reasoning trace for each new question.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Confidence calibration in LLMs refers to aligning the model's expressed confidence with its actual accuracy. Existing prompt-based methods treat calibration as a one-shot inference problem, relying on instance-level reasoning or post-hoc self-assessment. Rehearse instead enables models to adapt from their own scored confidence experiences, using a game-like setting to provide feedback and improve calibration.

## [StarEmbed: First Benchmark for Time Series Foundation Models on Astronomical Light Curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06200) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StarEmbed is introduced as the first public benchmark for light curves, comprising real observations of ~40,000 stars labeled across seven classes, and evaluates time series foundation models (TSFMs) on clustering, classification, and out-of-distribution (OOD) detection. The results show that the Chronos family achieves state-of-the-art performance in clustering and OOD detection, despite being pre-trained on non-astronomical data. This benchmark addresses a gap in TSFM training corpora, which often omit irregularly sampled data like astronomical light curves. It provides a standardized evaluation for TSFMs on challenging real-world data, potentially driving improvements in model generalization and broadening the application of TSFMs to astronomy and other scientific domains. The benchmark includes ~40,000 stars with expert labels across seven classes, and evaluates TSFMs with different architectures and training strategies, as well as domain-specific transformers. Notably, no TSFM strictly surpasses the classification performance of the established domain baseline, but they demonstrate excellent generalization abilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) are pre-trained on large collections of time series data to enable zero-shot forecasting and other tasks. Astronomical light curves, which plot the brightness of stars over time, are abundant and exhibit irregular sampling, multiple variates, and heteroskedasticity, making them a challenging testbed for TSFMs. Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection is a technique to identify test samples that differ from the training distribution, which is crucial for reliable model deployment.
