## [Unicode Text Watermarking Methods Vulnerable to LLM Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a systematic security analysis of ten Unicode text watermarking methods against six large language models (LLMs), including GPT-5, GPT-4o, Teuken 7B, Llama 3.3, Claude Sonnet 4, and Gemini 2.5 Pro. The findings reveal that, especially the latest reasoning models, can detect watermarked text, but none can extract the watermark without source code. This research addresses a critical gap in AI safety and content provenance, as text watermarking is a key technique for protecting digital content and identifying AI-generated text. The findings highlight vulnerabilities in current methods, urging the development of more robust watermarking schemes to withstand detection by advanced LLMs. The study implemented ten existing Unicode text watermarking methods in a controlled testbed of three experiments. Notably, all models failed to extract the watermark unless implementation details (source code) were provided, indicating that watermark extraction is challenging without specific knowledge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Digital text watermarking embeds invisible information into text to protect copyright and verify authenticity. Unicode-based methods often use homoglyph substitution or zero-width characters to preserve visual appearance. As LLMs become more capable, their ability to detect such watermarks poses a threat to the security of these techniques.

## [LAMaS: Latency-Aware Orchestration for Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10560) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces LAMaS, a latency-aware orchestration framework for multi-agent systems that optimizes critical path latency while preserving task accuracy. It reduces end-to-end latency by over 50% on four benchmarks while maintaining competitive or better accuracy. This work addresses a critical yet underexplored problem in multi-agent LLM systems: latency. By providing a modular framework that reduces latency without sacrificing accuracy, it could enable more responsive and practical deployment of multi-agent AI systems in time-sensitive applications. LAMaS operates at two levels: at training time, it learns latency-aware execution graphs via constrained optimization with critical-path-aware credit assignment; at inference time, a lightweight controller adaptively eliminates redundant future agent interactions. The framework is modular and transfers to other MAS with minimal changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) coordinate multiple LLM-powered agents through structured workflows, which increases reasoning power but also incurs high inference latency due to multi-step execution and repeated model invocations. End-to-end latency in such systems is governed by the critical execution path, so simply reducing total cost does not reliably reduce latency. Naive latency optimization can misassign operator-level credit and degrade task accuracy, making the problem non-trivial.

## [Individuating Neural Architecture via Represented Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11618) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a novel framework for individuating neural architectures based on the represented process at a receiver, using factorizations and kernels to classify architectural distinctions beyond composite functions. This framework could change how neural architectures are analyzed and designed, providing a more precise way to compare architectures that may be functionally equivalent but structurally different. It highlights context dependence that modular labels obscure, potentially impacting architecture search and interpretability. The paper introduces the concept of an 'extensional shadow' that classifies unmarked surjective factorizations up to unique carrier re-presentation, but does not determine marked receiver organization. An exact two-token construction shows that local and attention schemas can be distinction-equivalent after one injective prefix and inequivalent after another, despite no loss of predecessor information.

**Background**: Neural architecture is often identified by module syntax, computation graphs, or the composite functions they realize. This paper argues that these descriptions answer different identity questions and proposes to study the represented process available at a receiver, focusing on factorizations and kernels to capture architectural distinctions.

## [VIBES: Bayesian-Guided VLM Reasoning for Efficient Expressway Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VIBES, an asynchronous framework that uses Bayesian inference to guide focused Vision-Language Model (VLM) reasoning for far-field anomaly detection in expressway surveillance videos. It triggers VLM analysis only on selected frames and localized regions, reducing computational overhead while improving detection accuracy. This work addresses a practical challenge in video surveillance by enabling real-time, efficient anomaly detection for far-field vehicles, which is critical for traffic safety. The asynchronous trigger mechanism could inspire more efficient VLM applications in other video analytics domains. VIBES uses an online kinematics-guided Bayesian inference module to estimate a context-dependent normal-motion distribution from vehicle trajectories and update probabilistic boundaries. Deviations from these boundaries generate asynchronous triggers that localize candidate anomalies in time and space, allowing the VLM to reason only over relevant frames and regions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Anomaly detection in surveillance videos aims to identify unusual events, such as traffic accidents or erratic driving. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but processing full frames is computationally expensive and can miss subtle anomalies in distant objects. Bayesian inference provides a probabilistic framework for updating beliefs based on new evidence, which can be used to model normal motion patterns and detect deviations.

## [SAFE-SVD: Sensitivity-Aware Compression for Physics Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17985) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SAFE-SVD introduces a sensitivity-aware fidelity-enforcing compression framework for physics foundation models (PFMs), explicitly modeling loss-aware layer sensitivity in the output function space. Experiments show substantial gains over existing methods, achieving significantly higher compression ratios while maintaining accuracy, in some cases by orders of magnitude. This work addresses the underexplored problem of compressing PFMs while preserving physical fidelity, which is crucial for deploying AI in scientific applications. It could lead to a new subfield of efficient, deployable, and sustainable scientific foundation models, impacting AI for Science and model compression research. The method explicitly models loss-aware layer sensitivity in the output function space, addressing the functional nature of physics data where partial derivatives encode spatiotemporal dynamics. Conventional compression methods ignore this structure, often causing severe performance degradation or failure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Physics foundation models (PFMs) are a new trend in AI for Science, trained on physical data rather than text or images. Model compression is essential for reducing memory use and accelerating inference in large foundation models, but it remains under-explored for PFMs, where preserving physical fidelity is crucial. The challenge lies in the functional nature of physics data, where partial derivatives encode spatiotemporal dynamics and exhibit high sensitivity to compression.

## [Dimensional Balance Boosts Large-Scale Spatiotemporal Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18793) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a scalable adaptive framework that uses entropy-based diagnostics to balance spatial and temporal feature representations, improving large-scale spatiotemporal prediction. The framework compresses spatial dimensionality via low-rank matrix embedding and extends the temporal horizon to capture long-range dependencies. This work addresses a key bottleneck in spatiotemporal prediction, offering a novel diagnostic and scalable solution that could benefit fields like urban traffic, meteorology, and public health. The framework's broad applicability and substantial accuracy gains suggest it may influence future modeling strategies in these domains. The framework uses entropy measures as diagnostic indicators of spatiotemporal complexity mismatch, not as guarantees that alignment improves forecasting. Experiments on urban traffic, meteorological, and epidemic datasets show substantial accuracy gains, and code is available on GitHub.

Existing methods often face performance bottlenecks and limited cross-domain transferability. Entropy measures can quantify complexity in data, and low-rank matrix embedding is a technique to reduce dimensionality while preserving essential structure. ## [Stability-Aware Checkpoint Selection for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a progressive framework for selecting final MLLM checkpoints under evaluation uncertainty, using subsampling and percentile-based aggregation to improve ranking robustness. Experiments show that quality-aware curation of OCR-heavy inputs reduces ranking flip rate from 32.5% to 11.2% and increases inter-run agreement from 0.61 to 0.84. This work addresses a practical and underexplored problem in MLLM training: checkpoint selection under noisy evaluation. It could significantly impact model development workflows by providing a more reliable method to choose final checkpoints, especially when candidates are closely matched. The framework combines pointwise filtering, listwise ranking, and pairwise refinement, and uses repeated evaluation-set subsampling to characterize ranking stability. The study also observes divergence between validation-loss progression and downstream checkpoint preference in two independent MLLM settings, and a public Qwen2.5-VL-7B reproduction across 11 checkpoints shows tightly clustered pointwise scores and tie-dominated final pairwise comparisons.

## [INSHAPE: Instance-Level Shapelets for Interpretable Time-Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20088) ⭐️ 7.0/10

INSHAPE is a novel framework that discovers instance-specific, non-overlapping shapelets for time-series classification, modeling their temporal dependencies. It outperforms state-of-the-art shapelet-based methods on 128 UCR and 30 UEA benchmark datasets. This work addresses fundamental limitations of population-level shapelets, which often misalign with instance-specific features and overlook temporal dependencies. By providing clear instance-level interpretations while maintaining strong predictive performance, INSHAPE advances interpretable machine learning for time-series analysis. INSHAPE identifies variable-length, discriminative patterns as non-overlapping segments and models their temporal dependencies. It also bridges local and global interpretability through a bottom-up approach that aggregates instance-level shapelets into prototypical (population-level) shapelets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Shapelets are discriminative temporal patterns within time series that help make time-series classification (TSC) more transparent. Traditional methods focus on population-level shapelets optimized across the entire dataset, which can lead to suboptimal performance and misleading interpretations. INSHAPE instead discovers instance-specific shapelets, addressing these limitations and providing more intuitive insights.

## [Annealed Softmax Greedy Achieves Near-Optimal Bayes Regret in Many-Armed Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31034) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that annealed softmax greedy achieves near-optimal Bayes regret in many-armed Bayesian Bernoulli bandits under a linear upper-tail condition on the prior. Specifically, it attains a regret of O~(m + T/m), which is O~(√T) when the number of arms m scales as Θ(√T). This result provides a theoretical basis for why uncertainty-agnostic policy updates like GRPO can be effective in reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards. It bridges the gap between Bayesian bandits and RLVR, offering a novel explanation for the success of such methods in practice. The proof relies on a β-regularity condition (specifically β=1), which implies an abundance of near-optimal arms. The paper also shows that with a small number of arms, the same softmax policy can suffer linear regret, highlighting the importance of the many-armed regime.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandits model the exploration-exploitation trade-off, where an agent chooses among multiple actions to maximize cumulative reward. Bayesian bandits incorporate prior distributions over arm rewards, and Bayes regret measures the expected loss relative to the optimal policy. Annealed softmax (Boltzmann) exploration selects actions with probabilities proportional to exponential of scaled empirical means, with temperature decreasing over time. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method that updates a policy by comparing multiple sampled completions without an explicit critic, similar to the uncertainty-agnostic updates studied here.

## [Generative Inpainting Reduces Distribution Shift in Hand Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01896) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates whether generative inpainting, which edits only the hand region of real images to add accessories like gloves or tattoos, can reduce distribution shift in hand detection. Using YOLOv8n detectors, they conducted six experiments (A-F) with multiple seeds and found that multi-stage training procedures can improve deployment performance on real test sets. This work addresses a practical problem in safety-critical applications where hand detection models often fail due to distribution shift caused by underrepresented hand appearances. By demonstrating that synthetic data can be effectively leveraged through specific training procedures, it provides a cost-effective way to improve model robustness without collecting extensive new real-world data. The study used a paired dataset of real images and their synthetic counterparts, evaluating YOLOv8n detectors across six experiments (A-F), with four involving training under three random seeds each. They reported mean average precision (mAP) at two overlap thresholds (mAP@0.5 and mAP@0.5:0.95) and used paired statistical tests; a two-stage experiment (train on real+synthetic, then fine-tune on real-only) directionally improved mAP@0.5 and narrowed the real-gloves out-of-distribution gap.

## [Certifiable Semantic Agreement Among LLM Agents: Admissibility Instrument Decides](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07316) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces H-CSC, a protocol that enables a committee of LLM agents to reach certifiable semantic agreement, emitting semantic commit, verdict commit, or typed abort outcomes under a 2f+1 distinct-signer certificate. It proves a containment lemma showing no coverage separation from certificate-wrapped majority at matched deterministic guarantees, and identifies the admissibility instrument as the key factor for performance. This work addresses a critical problem in multi-agent LLM systems: achieving agreement at the level of meaning rather than just labels. The findings clarify the theoretical limits of semantic certification and highlight the importance of the admissibility instrument, which could guide the design of more robust and trustworthy multi-agent LLM protocols. The paper reports that a 442 MB fine-tuned encoder achieves AUROC 0.621-0.744 at 0-8% TPR (5% honest FPR) against adversaries corrupting reasoning, while a training-free lexical predicate reaches 0.865-0.982 at 38-80% TPR. It also finds that honest agents disperse further than attacks displace (90th-percentile angular distance 0.992 rad vs 0.65 radius), and a similarity-preserving sketch separates faithful from substituted derived statements at AUROC 0.741, where cryptographic hash and verdict-only digest score 0.500. The paper also discloses a tie-break capture vulnerability and its four-line fix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent LLM systems, agents often need to reach consensus on answers or decisions. Traditional majority voting is simple but lacks guarantees about the semantic validity of the outcome. This paper proposes a protocol that provides certifiable semantic agreement, meaning the agreement is verifiable at the level of meaning, not just labels. The protocol uses certificates with 2f+1 distinct signers to ensure Byzantine fault tolerance, and introduces an admissibility instrument to filter out invalid rationales.

## [VLM Integration Paradigms Reveal Hidden Visual Token Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20077) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides a fair comparison of in-context and layer-wise injection paradigms for integrating visual tokens into LLMs, under identical training conditions across single-image, multi-image, and video benchmarks. It uncovers a hidden evolution where visual tokens are progressively reshaped, each paradigm capturing different frequency characteristics of the visual signal. This research addresses an underexplored question in VLM architecture design, providing insights that could influence future VLM development. Understanding how integration paradigms affect visual representation quality and alignment with language space is crucial for improving multimodal model performance across various tasks. The paper demonstrates that attention allocation alone is insufficient; performance is driven by the quality of visual representations at each layer. Layer-wise injection methods keep visual tokens static and do not allow them to attend to one another across frames, unlike in-context methods.

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) integrate visual information into Large Language Models (LLMs) by converting images into visual tokens. There are two main integration paradigms: in-context injection, where visual tokens are treated as part of the input sequence, and layer-wise injection, where visual features are injected into intermediate layers. The choice of paradigm affects how visual information is transformed and aligned with the language space, impacting model performance.

## [OpenVLA Failure Detection Under Visual Shift: Strong Retrospective, Poor Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates whether OpenVLA's internal activations can provide early warning signals before failure under visual distribution shift. The authors find strong retrospective detection (AUROC 0.972) but poor calibration, with 3.32 warning onsets per clean episode. This work addresses a critical issue in vision-language-action (VLA) policy robustness under visual distribution shifts, which is essential for safe deployment in real-world robotics. The finding that strong retrospective discrimination does not imply operationally quiet warning behavior highlights a key gap that must be bridged for practical failure detection. The study uses OpenVLA on LIBERO-10, freezing the policy and recording one MLP activation per step, then fitting linear monitors. Occlusion reduces task success from 57% to 17%, and a layer-16 logistic probe achieves AUROC 0.972 and AUPRC 0.352, while action disagreement achieves only AUROC 0.496. The occlusion-trained probe transfers to camera-jitter episodes with AUROC 0.689, but the same monitor averages 3.32 warning onsets per clean episode, indicating poor calibration.

**Background**: OpenVLA is a 7B-parameter open-source vision-language-action model pretrained on 970k robot episodes from Open X-Embodiment, combining a Llama 2 language model with visual encoders DINOv2 and SigLIP. LIBERO-10 is a benchmark with 10 tasks for evaluating lifelong learning and knowledge transfer in robotics. AUROC and AUPRC are common metrics for binary classification performance, especially under class imbalance.

## [LLM Test Oracles: New Source-of-Authority Taxonomy from Systematic Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05031) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A PRISMA-guided systematic literature review screened 2,436 records to 54 included studies, extended by snowballing to 83 total, and introduced a source-of-authority taxonomy for LLM-based test oracles. The review found that just over half of the oracles reach a verdict with no explicit specification. This taxonomy addresses a gap in prior secondary studies, which classified oracles by form or technique but not by the source of authority. It highlights that many LLM-based oracles lack explicit specifications, raising concerns about the trustworthiness of their verdicts and impacting software testing practices. The review reads the corpus along three axes: source of authority, form, and adjudication mechanism. It finds that source and mechanism cross-cut rather than coincide, so labels like 'LLM-as-a-judge' describe how a verdict is produced, not why it should be trusted. Oracle quality is most often judged by resemblance to a known oracle rather than by fault detection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to decide whether software behaves correctly, either by writing test oracles or acting as them. A test oracle is a mechanism to determine whether a test passed or failed. Prior studies classified oracles by form or technique, but this review focuses on the source of authority—the property that governs how far a verdict can be trusted. The PRISMA 2020 guidelines provide a standardized framework for reporting systematic reviews and meta-analyses.

## [drXAI: XAI-Driven Data Reduction for Scalable Time Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15774) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces drXAI, a novel methodology that repurposes XAI attribution methods for data reduction in Time Series Classification (TSC). It uses a fast GPU-accelerated classifier (Hydra) to generate local attributions, aggregates them into global feature importance scores, and applies an automated elbow-cut heuristic to select salient features, achieving 80-90% data reduction while maintaining accuracy. This work addresses the scalability bottleneck of modern TSC models like Transformers, which have quadratic complexity in sequence length and linear complexity in channels, making them prohibitive for massive datasets. By enabling resource-intensive models to scale to previously inaccessible datasets, drXAI demonstrates XAI's practical utility beyond interpretability, potentially benefiting the broader TSC community and applications dealing with large-scale time series data. drXAI is evaluated on synthetic and real-world univariate and multivariate datasets, successfully recovering ground-truth features on synthetic benchmarks where traditional baselines fail. The method allows models like ConvTran to scale to datasets previously inaccessible due to memory constraints, and all code and data are openly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Time Series Classification (TSC) is a fundamental task in machine learning, but modern state-of-the-art models like Transformers suffer from high computational complexity, especially with long sequences and many channels. Explainable AI (XAI) methods provide attributions that explain model predictions, but their use for performance gains in downstream tasks has been underexplored. Hydra is a fast and accurate dictionary-based classifier that uses competing convolutional kernels, making it suitable for generating attributions efficiently. The elbow-cut heuristic is an automated technique to select the most salient features without manual thresholds.

## [Study Reveals 'Strategic Oscillation' in Designers' AI Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23126) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An exploratory qualitative study of five expert UI/UX designers (11-20 years experience) identified 'Strategic Oscillation'—the intentional alternation between vague and specific language when prompting generative AI. The researchers propose the ECRT cycle (Expectation-Collision-Reflection-Transformation) to model this tacit knowledge externalization process. This research provides a novel framework for understanding how expert designers interact with generative AI, which could inform the design of better AI tools and educational practices. As generative AI becomes ubiquitous in creative workflows, insights into effective prompting strategies are increasingly valuable for both practitioners and tool developers. The study used think-aloud protocols and identified shifts from 'instruction' to 'consultation' mode, deep reflection triggered by semantic collisions, and one participant's disengagement as a tentative boundary condition. The ECRT cycle operationalizes the 'externalization' phase of the SECI model for human-AI collaboration, yielding three design patterns for metacognitive support.

'Strategic Oscillation' refers to experts' deliberate return to vague language (Vibe) after progressing toward specific specifications (Code), leveraging AI's probabilistic nature for creative exploration.

## [New CRoMa Metric Better Assesses Pathology Foundation Model Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25497) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CRoMa (Cross-confounder Robustness Margin), a new metric that measures whether biologically matched samples differing in confounders lie closer in representation space than confounder-matched samples differing in biology. It overcomes structural limitations of the existing Robustness Index (RI) for evaluating pathology foundation models. CRoMa provides a more principled and reliable way to assess robustness of pathology foundation models against non-biological variations, which is critical for ensuring generalizability across institutions. This could lead to better model selection and development in medical AI, ultimately improving clinical reliability. The authors evaluated frozen representations from 20 tile-level encoders across three benchmarks and 4 slide-level encoders on a fourth. They found that while median CRoMa rankings were broadly consistent across cohorts, all encoders contained confounder-dominated subsets whose prevalence and severity varied substantially across models and cohorts. Higher CRoMa margins were associated with smaller shortcut-induced performance drops after supervised adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Pathology foundation models are large neural networks, often based on Vision Transformers, trained on histopathology images. They can encode non-biological variations from tissue preparation, staining, and scanning, leading to shortcut learning that undermines generalization across institutions. The Robustness Index (RI) was previously proposed to assess whether local representation geometry is dominated by biological or non-biological variation, but it has structural limitations. CRoMa recasts robustness as a cohort-wide distribution of sample-level margins, providing a more comprehensive assessment.

## [C4 Coordinates Adaptive Sampling and Early Exit in Diffusion LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28166) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C4, a method that coordinates adaptive sampling and early exit in diffusion language models via two separate gates: Confidence-Verified Early Exit (CVEE) and Commit-Core-Then-Confirm (CCTC). On 12 zero-shot tasks with LLaDA and Dream, one frozen configuration removes 64–95% of decoding steps and delivers measured end-to-end speedups of 2.6 to 8.6 over full decoding. This work addresses a significant performance bottleneck in diffusion language models by jointly optimizing two acceleration axes that were previously handled separately. The substantial speedups without task-specific tuning could make diffusion LMs more practical for real-world applications, potentially influencing future efficient inference designs. CVEE decides when the sequence may stop, requiring confidence and sustained argmax stability over a candidate span re-extracted at every step. CCTC decides which token positions a step may commit by borrowing an autoregressive freezing order inside each block, committing a boundary-anchored core and confirming deferred positions one step later. The method is validated on LLaDA and Dream models across 12 zero-shot tasks, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models generate text by iteratively denoising a corrupted sequence, producing provisional predictions at each step. Unlike autoregressive models that generate tokens sequentially, diffusion models can refine all positions in parallel, but this often requires many denoising steps, making inference slow. Early exit and adaptive sampling are techniques to reduce computation by stopping or skipping steps when confident, but they have typically been applied independently.

## [Coordinated Incentives Key to Curbing AI Misinformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07070) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study uses a three-party evolutionary game to show that neither unilateral regulation nor market incentives alone can curb AI-generated misinformation, and that an evolutionarily stable regime of real-information production requires coordinated incentives across regulators, enterprises, and users. This finding is significant because it provides a formal framework for designing effective AI governance policies, highlighting the need for coordinated and adaptive policy mixes rather than isolated measures. It will inform policymakers, AI companies, and researchers working on misinformation and AI ethics. The model incorporates heterogeneous rewards and punishments and uses replicator equations to characterize evolutionary stability. The analysis identifies critical thresholds for regulatory rewards and punishment intensity, enterprise reputation loss, and user adoption incentives that must be collectively surpassed to achieve real-information production.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary game theory models strategic interactions where players' success depends on the frequency of strategies in a population. The replicator equation describes how strategy frequencies change over time, favoring those with higher-than-average fitness. An evolutionarily stable strategy (ESS) is one that, once adopted by a population, cannot be displaced by alternative strategies. This study applies these concepts to the governance of AI-generated misinformation, where a government regulator, an AI enterprise, and users interact strategically.

## [Aftab Benchmark Boosts PQN Performance on Atari-57](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07335) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Aftab, a composite architecture that benchmarks eight CNN encoders and advanced value functions within the Parallelized Q-Network (PQN) framework. Aftab achieves an IQM Human-Normalized Score of 6.592 on Atari-57, compared to 2.715 for standard PQN, and a nAUC of 0.541 on Procgen-Hard versus 0.216 for PQN. This work addresses an underexplored area in deep reinforcement learning by systematically studying encoder architectures and value functions in a replay-free, parallelized setting. The significant performance improvements demonstrate that careful architectural design can substantially enhance PQN, potentially influencing future RL algorithm development and applications. Aftab integrates the Hadamax encoding paradigm with categorical, ensemble, and dueling value heads, and includes eight distinct CNN topologies with characterized parameter and computational requirements. The framework is open-sourced at https://github.com/tahashieenavaz/aftab, including model definitions, training configurations, and raw experimental logs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Parallelized Q-Network (PQN) is a deep reinforcement learning algorithm that enables off-policy value learning without experience replay buffers or target networks, using parallel agents for efficiency. Hadamax encoding is a technique that enhances performance in model-free Atari benchmarks, and dueling value heads are a known improvement in DQN architectures. This paper builds on these concepts to explore architectural choices in PQN.

<a id="item-285"></a>
## [Private Etymology: A Provenance Model for Shared Symbols in Human-AI Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08443) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Private Etymology, a machine-representable relational provenance model that records how dyad-specific symbolic expressions are proposed, interpreted, negotiated, repaired, reused, revised, stabilized, contested, forgotten, or retired over time in long-term human-AI interactions. It also proposes relational reuse, which reactivates a dyad-specific expression in later sessions without fully explaining its meaning again. This work addresses a gap in current design models for long-term human-AI systems, providing a structured way to manage shared symbols and relational microcultures. It could impact conversational AI and HCI by enabling more persistent and evidence-grounded symbolic interactions, potentially improving user experience in long-term AI companionship. The paper presents a lifecycle model, an illustrative machine-readable schema, and a working Apple Watch prototype. In the prototype, a language model classifies discrete conversational evidence, while deterministic local code decides whether a Shared Symbol can be updated, preventing free-form model confidence scores or AI proposals from directly updating persisted symbols.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Previous research has shown that people develop shared symbols, partner-specific expressions, and inside jokes as part of a relational microculture. Recent work has examined how humans and conversational AI negotiate and revise symbolic meanings, but long-term systems lack a clear design model for recording and reusing these dyad-specific expressions. Private Etymology integrates prior ideas into persistent, revisable, and evidence-grounded symbolic units, serving as infrastructure for conversational agents to participate in changing relational microcultures.

<a id="item-286"></a>
## [REOPD: Reliability-Adaptive Reward Extrapolation for On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11698) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REOPD introduces a reliability-adaptive reward extrapolation framework for on-policy distillation (OPD) that adjusts token-level coefficients to prevent reward hacking and improve training stability. It combines a token-level compatibility weight with a batch-level adaptive budget, yielding a token-wise coefficient that preserves teacher alignment while selectively extrapolating along reliable directions. This work addresses a known issue in LLM alignment and distillation—reward hacking—by providing a practical, training-stable solution that requires no extra models or rollouts. It could improve the efficiency and reliability of distilling large language models, benefiting researchers and practitioners working on model compression and alignment. REOPD outperforms G-OPD on single-teacher mathematics and on both domains in the multi-teacher setting, while matching G-OPD on single-teacher code. The method requires no verifier, reward model, value model, or extra rollout beyond standard OPD, making it a lightweight enhancement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own trajectories under dense token-level supervision from a teacher. Reward-extrapolation methods like ExOPD amplify the teacher-reference log-likelihood ratio to move beyond direct imitation, but they apply a single global coefficient to all tokens, which can cause reward hacking and unstable training. REOPD addresses this by adaptively scaling extrapolation per token based on reliability.

## [LLM-Assisted Multi-Agent Scheduling for Edge Stream Processing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12371) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces MAS-DecStream, a multi-agent scheduling framework that extends the Contract Net Protocol with LLM-assisted semantic negotiation and deterministic validation for stream processing in mobile edge computing. Experiments show reduced latency violations to 3%, eliminated resource overcommitment, and up to 22% utility improvement over rule-based baselines. This work addresses the challenge of decentralized scheduling in heterogeneous edge-cloud environments, where workload volatility and QoS constraints are critical. By integrating LLMs with a classical negotiation protocol, it offers a promising approach to improve scheduling efficiency and adaptability, potentially benefiting edge computing and IoT applications. The framework, LLM-MR-CNP, incorporates semantic CFP formulation, progressive context disclosure, multi-round proposal revision, negotiation memory, and deterministic validation. Evaluations on Alibaba ASI Trace show that multi-round CNP refinement is the main protocol-level gain, with LLM assistance adding value for qualitative and uncertain runtime context, though accuracy-cost trade-offs are model- and prompt-dependent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: The Contract Net Protocol (CNP) is a classic task-sharing protocol in multi-agent systems, introduced in 1980 by Reid G. Smith, where a manager announces tasks and agents bid to execute them. Stream processing in mobile edge computing involves distributed data processing across edge and cloud resources, requiring efficient scheduling to meet QoS requirements. This paper combines CNP with large language models (LLMs) to handle qualitative context and improve negotiation.

This paper introduces a sociotechnical AI pipeline that combines LLM-based schema normalization, HDBSCAN sub-topic clustering, and hierarchical agglomerative clustering to convert raw ITSM ticket data into multilevel decision-support artifacts. A stakeholder evaluation with five raters from Sales Engineering and customer success roles showed all four decision-support metrics (interpretability, actionability, trust, and likelihood of use) averaged above 4.0 out of 5.0. This work addresses a practical gap in ITSM analytics by framing it as an Information Systems problem of transformation, abstraction, and human-centered design, rather than a purely technical one. The high stakeholder ratings suggest the pipeline could be adopted in real-world settings, potentially improving how sales and executive teams leverage ticket data for strategic decisions. The pipeline uses LLM-based schema normalization to standardize heterogeneous ticket fields, HDBSCAN for granular sub-topic clustering, and hierarchical agglomerative clustering to form executive-facing main topics. The evaluation involved six artifacts and five raters, with trust being the most consistent signal across all metrics.

## [OGR-MARL: Option-Guided Residual MARL Boosts USV Pursuit in Ports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12995) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces OGR-MARL, a framework that combines option-guided rule-based behaviors with residual multi-agent reinforcement learning to improve heterogeneous USV cooperative pursuit in constrained port waterways. The OGR-MASAC instantiation achieves a 75% capture rate in an abstract Xiazhimen port scenario, and zero-shot transfer to a QGIS/AIS-informed map shows promising generalization. This work addresses the challenge of applying MARL to safety-critical maritime operations by integrating domain knowledge (rule-based behaviors) with learned residual policies, reducing the need for exploration from scratch. It demonstrates a practical approach that could improve the reliability and deployment of autonomous USV systems in real-world port environments. OGR-MARL is algorithm-agnostic and can be instantiated with MADDPG, MATD3, MAPPO, and MASAC, yielding OGR-MADDPG, OGR-MATD3, OGR-MAPPO, and OGR-MASAC. The framework incorporates shared evader belief, role-conditioned option targets, adaptive rule penalties, and residual policy learning, with experiments conducted in an abstract Xiazhimen port-waterway scenario.

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) trains agents to learn cooperative policies, but in constrained environments like port waterways, learning from scratch can be inefficient and unsafe. Residual policy learning (RPL) improves an existing (possibly rule-based) policy by learning a residual correction, while options provide a way to structure behavior hierarchically. Heterogeneous USVs (unmanned surface vehicles) have different capabilities, making cooperative pursuit challenging.

## [Uniform Herding: Replay with Representation Refresh for Class-Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13061) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Uniform Herding, a replay-based method for class-incremental learning that refreshes exemplars in the current representation. On CIFAR-100 with ten tasks, it achieves 44.00% final accuracy and 17.22% forgetting, outperforming iCaRL's 42.33% and 24.87%. This work addresses catastrophic forgetting in continual learning, a key challenge for deploying models in dynamic environments. The proposed method offers a simple yet effective improvement over the widely used iCaRL baseline, potentially benefiting applications like online learning and adaptive systems. Uniform Herding allocates a uniform active exemplar budget across observed classes and uses a bounded candidate pool to refresh exemplars. Experiments show that removing distillation increases forgetting, and changing the retrieval budget has a smaller effect than changing the active budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Class-incremental learning (CIL) is a continual learning scenario where a model learns new classes over time without forgetting previously learned ones. Catastrophic forgetting occurs when updating weights for new tasks degrades performance on old tasks. Exemplar replay stores representative past samples to mitigate this, and iCaRL is a classic method that combines exemplar selection with distillation.

## [LLM Explanatory Engagement Under Rare Failures: Condition-Dependent Patterns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13063) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper empirically studies how LLMs' explanatory engagement (length, specificity, confidence) changes as task failure rates become asymptotically rare, using three open-weight models and a local harness. It finds that the initial hypothesis of a rise-then-collapse pattern is false overall, but condition-dependent patterns emerge, such as a plateau under immediate_forced prompting. This work highlights that elicitation structure is a first-class moderator of LLM behavior under rare failures, which is crucial for designing reliable AI workflows. It also reveals model-specific self-monitoring behaviors, informing future research on anomaly detection and model reliability. The study used three models (qwen3:8b, llama3.1:8b, mistral:7b) with failure probabilities swept from 0.2 to 0.0001 under five elicitation conditions. Under immediate_forced, length peaked at 28.4 words at p=0.05 and settled to 17.4-19.0 words at rarest rates; confidence rose from ~53% to 70s-90s. A companion guaranteed-failure run (72 cells) showed models differ in anomaly recognition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used in workflows where failures are rare but impactful. Prior work often asks whether models notice anomalies, but this paper narrows the focus to explanatory engagement—how models explain failures when they occur. The study uses open-weight models and a local harness to control failure rates, providing a systematic empirical analysis.

## [Learning-Based Framework for Robust Sleep Stage Labels from Multiple Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12446) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a learning-based hypnogram (LBH) framework that models each expert scorer's behavior using confusion matrices derived from machine-learning models, then aggregates probabilities across scorers to assign final sleep stage labels. Evaluated on DOD-H and DOD-O datasets, LBH improved accuracy, precision, and F1-score compared to dataset hypnogram and best-scorer hypnogram, achieving up to 86.07% accuracy with random forest and EEG+EMG. This work addresses inter-scorer variability in sleep staging, a known challenge that affects the reliability of reference labels used to train and evaluate automatic staging models. By leveraging multi-scored datasets, the proposed framework can produce more robust reference hypnograms, potentially improving the development and validation of sleep staging algorithms in both research and clinical settings. The study used EEG (C3-M2) and chin EMG signals segmented into 30-s epochs, extracting 30 features per modality for a total of 60 features. LBH was evaluated with random forest, support vector machine, and multilayer perceptron classifiers under EEG-only and EEG+EMG settings, with the best results from random forest and EEG+EMG: 86.07% accuracy, 85.46% precision, and 85.29% F1-score on DOD-H, and 86.04% accuracy, 85.21% precision, and 84.70% F1-score on DOD-O.

**Background**: Sleep stage classification is crucial for diagnosing sleep disorders, but manual scoring by experts is subject to inter-scorer variability. Multi-scored datasets like DOD-H and DOD-O, which include labels from five sleep technologists, provide an opportunity to derive more reliable reference labels. A hypnogram is a visual representation of sleep stages across the night, and confusion matrices are used to model the agreement between scorers and true labels, enabling probabilistic aggregation of expert opinions.

## [Heterogeneous Vision-Language Ensemble with Disagreement-Aware Reranking for Text-Based Person Anomaly Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12843) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The GENAI4E team proposed a framework that integrates heterogeneous vision-language models through score alignment and iterative ensemble fusion, followed by disagreement-aware VLM reranking, achieving 90.92% mAP, 85.13% Recall@1, 97.72% Recall@5, and 98.68% Recall@10 on the PAB benchmark for AI City Challenge 2026 Track 4. This work demonstrates a competitive solution for text-based person anomaly retrieval, a challenging task requiring fine-grained cross-modal reasoning. It highlights the effectiveness of combining complementary vision-language representations and selective multimodal reasoning, which could influence future approaches in anomaly detection and retrieval systems. The framework builds on a strong retrieval backbone and progressively integrates heterogeneous vision-language embedding models. The disagreement-aware reranking specifically targets ambiguous queries, improving retrieval accuracy on the large-scale PAB benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Text-based person anomaly retrieval aims to retrieve pedestrians with anomalous behaviors from a large image gallery using natural language descriptions. Unlike conventional text-based person retrieval, it requires reasoning over appearance, behaviors, object interactions, and scene context. The AI City Challenge provides benchmarks like PAB to evaluate such systems. Ensemble fusion and reranking are common techniques to improve retrieval performance.

## [EGRL: GNN Framework Improves RNA-Protein Interaction Prediction in Cold-Start Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12906) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces EGRL, a novel graph neural network framework that uses edge generation and relation-aware learning to improve RNA-protein interaction prediction, achieving significant gains in cold-start settings. This work addresses the cold-start problem in RNA-protein interaction prediction, which is crucial for discovering interactions involving unknown molecules. The proposed method could accelerate biological research and drug discovery by providing more reliable computational predictions. EGRL incorporates implicit meta-path learning, a multi-relation-aware attention mechanism, a graph generator for predicting soft edges, and a multi-feature fusion predictor. On four benchmark datasets, it achieves an AUROC of 0.867 and AUPR of 0.861 on unknown molecules, improving over prior state-of-the-art by 8.6% and 5.0%, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: RNA-protein interactions are vital for cellular functions, and computational prediction methods are needed due to the cost of wet-lab experiments. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are promising for modeling interaction networks, but existing methods often rely on homogeneous graphs or predefined meta-paths, limiting their ability to handle data sparsity and cold-start scenarios. EGRL aims to overcome these limitations by learning relational semantics implicitly and generating potential edges for unknown nodes.

## [UniTraffic-Agent: Unified Traffic Video Reasoning for AI City Challenge 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13031) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The MR-CAS team introduced UniTraffic-Agent, a unified system for traffic video reasoning, which follows an observe-reason-act-verify workflow and achieved competitive rankings on the AI City Challenge 2026 Track 3 public leaderboards: 16th on TAR (0.5780), 2nd on FETV (0.4884), and 4th on PSI-VQA (64.4161). This work demonstrates a practical approach to handling sparse and varied traffic video events with multimodal LLMs, addressing real-world challenges in intelligent transportation. Its strong performance on out-of-domain evaluations (FETV and PSI-VQA) suggests the method generalizes well, which could benefit future traffic safety and autonomous driving systems. The system samples timestamped visual evidence and reasons over all questions from the same clip in a single request, then converts responses through task-specific action adapters. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/Roclp/UniTraffic-Agent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic video understanding is crucial for intelligent transportation, as it helps explain how events develop, why they happen, and when interactions occur. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) struggle with this due to sparse events and varied viewpoints. The AI City Challenge is a competition that drives innovation in such applications, and Track 3 focuses on traffic anomaly reasoning and pedestrian intention prediction.

## [Self-Referential Prompts Increase LLM Response Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13258) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.13258) measures response instability in LLMs and finds that self-referential questions produce more inconsistent responses than unresolvable or verifiable questions. Using the Gemini API with 360 total responses, the study reports instability scores of 0.343 for self-referential, 0.192 for unresolvable philosophy, and 0.105 for verifiable questions. This study provides a quantitative baseline for the consistency of self-referential reports in LLMs, showing they occupy a distinct, less stable position in the output distribution. This has implications for understanding AI behavior, particularly in contexts where self-referential prompting is used to elicit subjective-experience-like reports, and for developing more reliable evaluation methods. The study used 30 independent responses per question (360 total) across four questions per group, with temperature set to 0.7. Response instability was defined as one minus the mean pairwise cosine similarity of sentence embeddings computed over a compressed core claim extracted from each response.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Self-referential prompting involves asking an LLM to report on its own subjective experience, which has been shown to reliably induce first-person reports resembling consciousness. However, prior work did not measure how consistent these reports are across repeated trials. This study compares such consistency against other open-ended question types, using sentence embeddings to quantify instability.

## [MLLM-Routed Heterogeneous Ensembles for Robust Cross-Dataset Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13463) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ARMDIL, an ensemble method that uses a multimodal large language model (MLLM) agent to dynamically route each image to the most suitable vision backbone among ResNets, self-supervised learners, and vision-language models. This approach aims to improve cross-dataset image classification robustness. This work addresses the challenge of models failing to generalize across domains, which is critical for real-world applications like AI assistants and autonomous robots. By enabling dynamic routing and interpretability through natural language reasoning, it offers a promising path toward more reliable general-purpose vision systems. ARMDIL's ensemble includes convolutional neural networks (ResNets), self-supervised representation learners (SSL), and vision-language models (VLMs), all trained on a unified label space from multiple datasets. The method performs competitively with specialized training-based routers while allowing new information to be integrated via simple prompt modifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional image classification models are typically trained on single task-specific datasets and often fail to generalize across different domains and difficulty levels. Ensembles combine multiple models to improve robustness, but static fusion methods may not adapt to each input. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can understand both text and images, enabling them to serve as dynamic routers that select the most appropriate model for each image, potentially improving performance and interpretability.

## [Bayesian Optimization Automates Termination of Neutron Scattering Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16946) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a Bayesian-optimization-based method to automatically determine when to stop inelastic neutron-scattering experiments by optimizing bin widths and comparing them to target resolutions. The method reduces search cost to about 10% of an exhaustive search in numerical experiments. This innovation could significantly improve experimental efficiency by preventing excessive data collection and saving valuable beam time at neutron facilities. It also demonstrates a novel application of Bayesian optimization to real-time experimental control, potentially inspiring similar automation in other scientific measurements. The method terminates the experiment when the optimal bin widths become smaller than the target resolutions. Numerical experiments using real inelastic neutron-scattering data showed that even data downsampled to 1/5 yield optimal bin widths comparable to resolution limits, indicating current measurements are excessive.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Inelastic neutron scattering is a technique used to study the dynamic properties of materials, such as magnetic excitations and phonons. Experiments often collect large amounts of event data, but measuring beyond the instrument's resolution wastes beam time. Bayesian optimization is a sequential model-based strategy for optimizing expensive black-box functions, commonly used in experimental design. This work applies it to bin-width optimization, a key step in histogramming neutron data, to enable automatic termination.

## [Q-Learning-Based QoS-Aware Multipath Routing Protocol for IoMT WBANs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15489) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces QQMR, a Q-learning-based QoS-aware multipath routing protocol for wireless body area networks (WBANs) in the Internet of Medical Things (IoMT). It classifies data into three priority levels and uses adaptive multi-level queuing and fuzzy C-means clustering to optimize routing, achieving improved packet delivery and reduced delay, overhead, and energy consumption. This work addresses critical challenges in IoMT, such as dynamic topology, energy constraints, and diverse QoS requirements, which are essential for reliable healthcare monitoring. By improving routing efficiency and energy consumption, QQMR could enhance the practicality and longevity of WBANs in real-world medical applications. QQMR maintains separate Q-learning policies for each data type and selects primary and backup paths accordingly. Experimental results show significant improvements in packet delivery ratio, delay, routing overhead, and energy consumption compared to existing methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 15, 04:00

**Background**: Wireless Body Area Networks (WBANs) consist of small sensors placed on or in the human body to monitor vital signs, and they are a key component of the Internet of Medical Things (IoMT). Routing in WBANs is challenging due to limited energy, node mobility, and the need to meet diverse Quality of Service (QoS) requirements. Q-learning is a reinforcement learning technique that enables agents to learn optimal actions through trial and error, making it suitable for adaptive routing in dynamic environments. Fuzzy C-means clustering is a soft clustering method that groups data points based on membership degrees, which can help in organizing network nodes efficiently.
